That guard is what backfired here: in both purchases, an early assistant message contained a short bold phrase that passed the pass-3 fallback — a roster line `1. **The Sheriff** — a full suspect...` in one chat, and a clarifying question `1. **The empire** — what did the family build...` in the other — and locked in as the title *before* the real `# TITLE` heading arrived in a later message (`# DEAD MAN'S HAND: A GOLDEN GULCH MYSTERY` and `# BLOODLINE: THE LAST CHECK-IN` respectively, confirmed by reading each conversation's `approved_concept_message_id` content directly). By the time the real heading streamed in, the title was no longer a placeholder, so the correct match was silently discarded.

**Checked whether this is a new pattern:** queried every paid conversation ever for short/generic titles (word count ≤ 2, and specifically the `^The [Capitalized]$` shape) — these two are the *only* matches in the corpus, ever. The underlying race is old (no commits touched this code path in the last 7 days) and has presumably been dormant/rare because it needs a specific ordering (a short bold phrase streaming in before the real heading), but landing twice in one day appears to be coincidence, not a shift in generation behavior.

**Fixed, both parts:**
1. **Data** — corrected `conversations.title` and `mystery_packages.title` for both packages directly from their `approved_concept_message_id` content: "The Sheriff" → "Dead Man's Hand: A Golden Gulch Mystery" (conversation `9b821518-4a1e-48eb-a53a-5897d36f7992`, package `ee018906-0e4d-49d4-b54d-6719b33bfbaf`); "The Empire" → "Bloodline: The Last Check-In" (conversation `c2a6c0fd-d06d-49ff-9e61-5c43e342d612`, package `5949180b-f147-440d-9e8a-10b0ddb5b65a`). Verified via `get_recent_public_sales(10)` that the homepage widget now shows the corrected titles.
2. **Race condition** — `extractTitleFromMessages` (`src/utils/titleExtraction.ts`) now also exposes *which* pass matched, via a new `extractTitleWithConfidence()` (the old function is now a thin string-only wrapper around it, so the other three read-only call sites — `HomeDashboard.tsx`, `MysteryPurchase.tsx`, `MysteryView.tsx` — are unaffected). `MysteryChat.tsx`'s per-message write path now allows a `# TITLE` heading match (`isHeaderMatch: true`) to overwrite an existing title even when it's not a placeholder, while still blocking lower-confidence bold-fallback matches from overwriting an existing title — preserving the intent of the original May 2026 fix in the one case (pass-3 bold fallback) it was actually meant to guard against, while closing the gap in the other (pass-1 heading, which should always win).

**Deliberately NOT done:** `MysteryView.tsx`'s separate title-write effect (`updateMysteryTitle`, lines ~260-288) still gates on its own stale `isDefaultTitle` predicate (checks for the literal `"<theme> - N Players"` format) — a second, independent placeholder check that's already out of sync with `MysteryChat.tsx`'s broader placeholder logic (same paired-predicate-drift shape as prior bugs, see `[[project_gate_detector_deadlock]]`). With the `MysteryChat.tsx` fix in place, this path is now a dormant secondary safety net rather than the thing actually producing wrong titles, so left as-is rather than refactored under this addendum's scope. Flagged as a follow-up if a title-mismatch bug of a different shape shows up here again.

## Addendum 17 (2026-09-04): new bug class — a genuine "accomplice trio" story left a confessed conspirator unarrested in `detective_script`'s reveal; no existing detector or alert can see this shape

Sweep of "Lethal Mutations: Death At The Helix Institute" (conversation `11610ef0-bade-4a88-926f-679c381a03ef`, package `6958982f-1a3c-4f4a-bd20-3cc68866cc70`, 11 players, `has_accomplice=true`, `mystery_style='detective'`, `language='en'`, purchased 2026-09-04, generated same day post-ADR-0074 with no scrutiny discount warranted). All three automated detectors and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` came back clean, and Jonathan separately confirmed no `notify-generation-issue` alert fired for this package.

**All three were right to stay quiet — this isn't a data-completeness or leaked-text defect, it's a narrative-logic one, and nothing in the current detector suite checks for it.** `master_context`'s structured suspect roster explicitly designs a three-person conspiracy — Dr. Odessa Helix (murderer) is described as "mastermind of the **accomplice trio** with Nikolai/Nikki Petrov and Dr. Marlowe/Marlo Fenn," and both Nikolai/Nikki and Marlowe/Marlo carry `role: "accomplice"` in that same roster, each with a full motive/means/elimination-reason writeup. This is corroborated downstream: `mystery_characters.character_role = 'accomplice'` was correctly set on *both* characters, and both had complete, well-written, self-consistent content — including a `final_statement` where Nikolai/Nikki explicitly confesses to serving as lookout ("I agreed to help... I kept watch... I helped end a life instead of just surviving what that life had done to me").

**The break is in `master_context`'s own schema: `accompliceDetails` is a singular object, and only captured Marlowe/Marlo.** Whatever downstream step generates `detective_script`'s climactic REVEAL evidently reads from that singular field, not from the roster's `role: "accomplice"` entries — so the reveal named and arrested only Dr. Odessa Helix and Dr. Marlowe Fenn, never mentioning Nikolai/Nikki at all. The practical effect: a guest playing Nikolai/Nikki openly confesses to being part of the murder conspiracy during final statements, and then the detective's arrest a few minutes later simply never brings it up — a proven, self-confessed co-conspirator walks out of the story unaddressed. This would read as a continuity error to any table playing the game, not just a quality nitpick.

**Fixed this package by hand** — edited `detective_script`'s REVEAL section directly (SQL `UPDATE` via `mcp__supabase__execute_sql`, `replace()` on the exact matched substring, verified before and after) to add a confrontation beat for Nikolai Petrov (grounded in the roster's own elimination-reason detail — "lingering near the main floor bar... consistent with his role as lookout" — so the added text doesn't introduce new unsupported facts) and updated the arrest line from "you are both under arrest" / "what was done to you both" to cover all three. Re-read the full REVEAL section after the edit to confirm it now reads coherently start to finish. Also fixed one unrelated grammar slip found during the same read of `game_overview` ("A investor watching millions evaporate" → "An investor..."). Grepped `host_guide`, `evidence_cards`, and `hosting_tips` for other "accomplice" references — none found, so no other document needed the same fix. Grepped all 11 characters' full content for leaked brackets/placeholders — clean.

**Not determined — and not something to decide solo:** whether this is a rare one-off (a story concept that happened to grow a third conspirator from the customer's chat despite `has_accomplice` being a single boolean toggle) or a recurring gap in how `master_context` is generated whenever a customer's underlying story concept naturally implies more than one accomplice. No corpus-wide check was run for other packages with multiple `character_role='accomplice'` rows sharing one package — that's the natural next step if this recurs, following this checklist's existing precedent (see Addendum 13's German register bug) of treating a second occurrence as the trigger to decide, with Jonathan, between another one-off patch and a structural fix (e.g., teaching the detective-script-reveal generation step to read the roster's plural `role` field instead of, or in addition to, the singular `accompliceDetails`).

## Addendum 18 (2026-09-04): new bug class — "empty relationships" fallback leaks meta-commentary ("relationship matrix", "cast dynamics", "self-reference") into character sheets; confirmed corpus-wide, not a one-off, detector regex extended same day

Sweep of "Murder At The Golden Feather Awards" (conversation `97115032-8948-4810-a8ca-3a09c9a30ea1`, package `569eb956-5c73-482d-807a-b4340fd1f2c6`, 22 players, `has_accomplice=false`, `mystery_style='detective'`, `language='en'`, purchased 2026-09-04, generated same day post-ADR-0074 with no scrutiny discount warranted). All three automated detectors and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` came back clean, victim name ("Fuchsia Flambeau") was consistent across `game_overview`, `detective_script`, and every character checked, and the murderer's identity matched cleanly between `mystery_characters.character_role='murderer'` and the detective script's reveal.

**Manual spot-check of character content (checklist step 6) found a real leak the detectors missed.** 2 of the 22 characters (Hedwig/Harlan Hummingbird, and the murderer Percival/Patricia Penguin) had a bracketed fallback sentence in their "RIVALS & ENEMIES" section for the case where a character has no hostile relationships, e.g. `[No hostile relationships apply to this character per the established cast dynamics.]` — phrasing like "per the established cast dynamics" reads as leaked model reasoning about its own generation process, not in-character narrative.

**Checked whether this was a one-off before doing anything — it wasn't.** A quick `ILIKE '%[No%]%'` sweep of `mystery_characters.relationships` across the whole corpus turned up the same pattern, in wildly different phrasings ("per the relationship matrix", "Self-reference — this would be removed in actual implementation", "[No self-entry needed]", "[No hostile relationships listed in the relationship matrix for X]"), in **at least 10 other historical packages** spanning unrelated themes (corporate HR, political drama, fantasy, western, gothic, Disney-crossover, high-society gala). Confirmed `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()`'s marker regex had zero coverage for any of this phrasing — a genuine, longstanding detector blind spot, not a fluke miss on this package.

**Decision (with Jonathan): patch this order now, extend the detector, defer a prompt-level fix pending real scale data.** Reasoning: the phrasing varies too much across hits to be one template bug — the model is freelancing a structural gap (character has no listed enemies) differently each time, so a proper prompt fix would need real design work and cross-theme testing, not a quick tweak. Better to get accurate frequency data from the extended detector first, then decide if a prompt fix is worth it.

**Fixed this package by hand** — rewrote both characters' `relationships` field (SQL `UPDATE` via `mcp__supabase__execute_sql`, using `#>> '{}'` to unwrap the jsonb string scalar, `replace()` on the exact matched substring, re-verified after) to drop the brackets and any meta phrasing, keeping the sentences in-voice and consistent with the rest of each character's writing. Re-selected both fields after the edit to confirm clean prose with no residual brackets.

**Extended `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()`'s marker regex** (migration `meta_text_leak_detector_add_relationship_matrix_pattern`) to add `relationship matrix`, `established cast dynamics`, `self-reference`, `no self-entry needed`, `this would be removed in actual implementation`, and a general `\[no [^\]]{0,80}hostile` bracket-pattern catch. Re-ran the detector: this package no longer flags (confirming the fix), and the corpus-wide count is now **16 packages flagged (15 paid, 1 unpaid)** — real backlog, not yet remediated.

**Not determined — and not something to decide solo:** whether/when to remediate the other 15 paid packages now flagged by the extended detector, and whether the frequency once fully counted justifies the prompt-level fix discussed above. Both are follow-up decisions for Jonathan, not resolved by this addendum — this addendum only covers the current order plus the detector extension.

## Addendum 19 (2026-09-04): corpus check on Addendum 17's "accomplice trio" bug — 3 real paid hits, root-caused to a specific gap in `MM Live - Parent62`'s prompt, blueprint fix drafted (not deployed)

Follow-up to Addendum 17. Jonathan asked whether the "accomplice trio" bug had been addressed systemically. Ran the corpus check Addendum 17 deferred: every `mystery_packages` row joined to `mystery_characters` grouped by package, filtered to `count(*) filter (where character_role = 'accomplice') > 1`.

**4 packages corpus-wide, 3 paid.** One unpaid outlier ("Death In The Spotlight," 25 players, `is_paid=false`, generated 2026-04-21) has **9** `accomplice`-tagged characters — a different, older failure shape, deprioritized since it was never purchased or played. The 3 paid hits, checked individually:

| Package | Extra `accomplice`-tagged character | Actual narrative role |
|---|---|---|
| Lethal Mutations (Addendum 17, fixed) | Nikolai/Nikki Petrov | Genuine co-conspirator — planned the murder, served as lookout, confesses to it in `final_statement` |
| Sunset Songs Scandal (32p, purchased 2026-08-14) | Sienna Strobe | Innocent bystander who merely overheard a suspicious comment — no involvement in the crime |
| The Cognitive Dissonance Incident (16p, purchased 2026-07-27) | Dr. Jordan/Jules Blackwood | Institutionally complicit enabler (buried misconduct complaints) — `final_statement` explicitly states "I didn't kill Morgan," never claims to have helped plan or execute it |

**This split matters: it's one root cause producing two different symptoms, not one bug.** For Lethal Mutations, the reveal was actually wrong (a real conspirator went unarrested). For the other two, the reveal was already correct — Sienna and Jordan shouldn't be arrested, because they didn't do it. Confirmed both packages' `detective_script` reveals only ever named the murderer + the one formally-selected accomplice, matching this. The actual defect in those two is the `character_role` tag itself: `character_role === 'accomplice'` also drives `isReassignRole()` in `GuestDropoutPanel.tsx` (ADR-0088's Remove-a-Character feature) — a mistagged character would incorrectly trigger the heavyweight murderer/accomplice plot-reassignment flow if a host ever removed that guest, corrupting a package that was otherwise fine.

**Root-caused in the live blueprint (`temp-files/MM Live - Parent62 (Package ID Race Fix).blueprint.json`), read locally, nothing edited in Make.com.** Confirmed this repo's own code never writes `character_role` — it's set entirely by the Make.com parent scenario and this repo just persists whatever it sends (`grep -rln character_role` across `src/` and `supabase/functions/` turns up only downstream *readers*: `GuestDropoutPanel.tsx`, `adaptationService.ts`, `CharacterAccess.tsx`, none of them writers). Found the gap in Part 2's prompt: `motiveDistribution[]` (the per-character roster) assigns each character `"role": "[murderer/accomplice/redHerring/suspect]"` — free choice among 4 values per character, with **no constraint tying `accomplice` to the single character named in Part 1's `accompliceSelection`**, and Part 1's `<accomplice_handling>` block never explicitly forbids describing a second character as a co-conspirator. `has_accomplice` on the customer-facing form is a plain boolean (confirmed with Jonathan — the product has never offered multiple accomplices as an option), so any second `accomplice` tag is purely the model freelancing a richer conspiracy in Part 1's murderer-selection writeup and then carrying that framing into Part 2's roster — consistent across all 3 real hits, just landing with different narrative weight (full conspirator vs. mere enabler) depending on how far the model took it.

**Fixed the two live mistags directly** — `character_role` on Sienna Strobe (package `64d63a5d-041f-4814-aadb-25cf22acc4b7`) and Dr. Jordan/Jules Blackwood (package `a0a985a9-2d2d-4176-a825-82ee6b2990be`) changed from `accomplice` to `suspect` (SQL `UPDATE`, matches their actual narrative role, closes the Remove-a-Character mistrigger risk). No `detective_script` changes needed for either — those were already correct.

**Blueprint prompt fix drafted, deliberately NOT deployed.** Two text edits proposed for `MM Live - Parent62`'s Part 1 and Part 2 system prompts:
- Part 1 `<accomplice_handling>`: add "There is EXACTLY ONE accomplice, always. Do not develop any other character as a co-conspirator, lookout, or knowing participant in planning or executing the murder... A character can be written as morally complicit... WITHOUT making them a co-conspirator in the murder itself."
- Part 2 `motiveDistribution[].role`: constrain the field description so `'accomplice'` may only be assigned to the exact character named in `accompliceSelection.character`, with `'suspect'`/`'redHerring'` as the explicit fallback for complicity-flavored characters.

Not deployed because applying it means duplicating the live Make.com scenario into a new numbered version (this project's established never-edit-in-place convention — Parent49 through Parent62, each a new duplicate) and a real test generation to verify, which costs live Anthropic spend requiring its own explicit go-ahead. Jonathan reviewed and accepted the diff, so the file was built the same way every prior version bump has been (see `build-parent-v33.py` for precedent): `temp-files/build-parent-v63.py` applies both edits as verified single-match substring replacements to `Parent62`'s JSON-decoded prompt text and writes `temp-files/MM Live - Parent63 (Single Accomplice Constraint).blueprint.json`. Diffed leaf-by-leaf against Parent62 — exactly the 2 intended prompt fields changed, nothing else. Both files are local-only (`temp-files/*.py` and `temp-files/*.json` are gitignored, matching every prior Parent build script/export). No Make.com API call made, no scenario duplicated on the platform, no test generation run — importing `Parent63` into Make.com and testing it (real Anthropic spend) are still Jonathan's to do.

**Not done:** the unpaid 9-accomplice outlier ("Death In The Spotlight") wasn't investigated further — never purchased, so no customer impact, and 9 tagged characters suggests a distinct enough failure mode from today's 2-character pattern that it likely doesn't share the same root cause. No corpus-wide check for other `character_role` mistagging shapes unrelated to `accomplice` (e.g., a `redHerring` narratively behaving like a `suspect`) — out of scope for this addendum, which was specifically triggered by the accomplice-count query.

**Decision (2026-09-04, with Jonathan): validate v63 against the next real `has_accomplice=true` purchase instead of a synthetic test run.** Jonathan imported `Parent63` into Make.com and confirmed it's wired to the same production webhook `mystery-webhook-trigger` already calls — so it's now the live scenario, not just an imported-but-inactive duplicate — but explicitly chose not to run a paid test generation now. Follow-up for whoever runs the next `sweep` on an accomplice-enabled purchase: explicitly re-run the Addendum 19 corpus query (`character_role='accomplice'` count > 1) scoped to that package as part of the standard checklist, so the fix gets a real confirm/deny rather than being caught only if it happens to recur.

## Addendum 20 (2026-09-04): Addendum 18 follow-up — caught my own detector's false positives, root-caused the leak to a live (not dead) prompt concept, fixed 9 more paid packages, drafted a targeted prompt guard

Jonathan asked whether the 15 other packages the Addendum 18 detector extension flagged were recent enough to be worth fixing, and separately asked whether "relationship matrix" might be an unused artifact worth deleting. Both questions led to real corrections.

**Caught a false positive in my own regex before trusting the count.** The bare term `relationship matrix` (added in Addendum 18) also matches two legitimate, non-leak contexts: (1) the `rumors` field's standard host-instruction text — "target 3 DIFFERENT characters... one Hostile from your relationship matrix" — present in 3 older packages (Apr 2026), and (2) a literal in-story prop in one package's `detective_script` ("Jordan's personal notes and the relationship matrix," a forensic-lab theme). Confirmed via direct substring checks in both cases. Rescoped the migration (`meta_text_leak_detector_scope_matrix_pattern_to_narrative_fields`) so `relationship matrix`/`established cast dynamics`/`cast dynamics` only fire against `mystery_characters`' narrative fields (`introduction`, `background`, `secret`, `relationships`, `description`), not `rumors`/round-scripts/package-level fields — the other markers (`self-reference`, `no self-entry needed`, `this would be removed in actual implementation`, the bracket-hostile pattern) stayed global since they showed no false positives on inspection. Corpus count dropped from 16 to 11; of those, 2 (`Death At Villa Amore`, `Before The Nikah`, both Apr 2026) turned out to be a separate, pre-existing `[choose ` placeholder match unrelated to this addendum's pattern — left untouched, flagged as its own open backlog item, not conflated with today's fix.

**Answered whether "relationship matrix" is a dead artifact — it is not, and should not be removed.** It's Section 2 of the live master-context planning prompt (`temp-files/intrigue-master-constraints-prompts.md:112`, `2. RELATIONSHIP MATRIX — Map every character's relationship to every other character...`) — a load-bearing planning step, not clutter. Confirmed the current live Child blueprint (`MM Live - Child (Unified)36-WebhookLanguageField.blueprint.json`) already carries two anti-leak guards naming this exact phrase (`<no_chain_of_thought_in_output>` forbids "reviewing the relationship matrix"; `<no_meta_text_in_output>` forbids "Pick a character from your relationship matrix" and strips bracketed placeholders generally) — essentially the same language already in `regenerate-child-content/index.ts`. The guard exists; it just isn't 100% effective. Every one of the 9 confirmed leaks (below) shares one exact trigger the guard doesn't positively address: **a character with zero hostile relationships, or zero extra allies beyond one real entry** — the guard says what not to write for this case, never what to write instead, so the model falls back to narrating the data structure.

**Fixed the 9 remaining confirmed-genuine packages** (all paid, 2026-05-07 through 2026-08-30 — all within the last 4 months): "The Cookie, The Crown, And The Jester's Heart," "Murder In Paradise: Death At Coral Cove Resort," "Camp Blood Reunion: The Final Summer," "The Birthday Betrayal," "Murder On The Midnight Express," "Death At The Velvet Viper," "Death By High Tea: Murder At The Velvet Shaker," "Death At Sumpter Hall," "The Purchasing Director." Three distinct leak shapes required different fixes, all via SQL `UPDATE` + `#>> '{}'`/`replace()`, verified after each: (1) the empty-relationship fallback bracket (5 characters) — reworded to plain in-character prose or, where a redundant bracket trailed an already-real entry, deleted outright; (2) the older self-listed-as-own-ally bug (2 characters, same shape as the Aug 16 self-relationship bug already fixed in `regenerate-child-content`, e.g. `[Note: This is yourself; no self-entry needed]`) — deleted the nonsensical self-entry line entirely, leaving the real ally intact; (3) literal unfilled template placeholders (2 lines on 1 character, e.g. `[Hostile relationship needed] - [Additional rival from relationship matrix]`) — deleted, since there was no real content to preserve. Re-ran the detector after all 9: zero remaining hits from this pattern corpus-wide (the 2 unrelated `[choose ` hits noted above are the only survivors).

**Drafted a targeted prompt fix.** Duplicated the live Child blueprint to `MM Live - Child (Unified)37-EmptyRelationshipFallbackGuard.blueprint.json` (confirmed 36 was the current head before duplicating, per this ADR's own versioning convention) and added a new `<empty_relationship_case>` instruction to all 8 AI-call modules in the scenario: explicitly forbids the structural-absence phrasing, and — the part the existing guards were missing — tells the model what to write instead (one grounded in-character sentence, or nothing at all if there's truly no more to say). Validated the file is still well-formed JSON and diffed clean (exactly 8 targeted insertions, nothing else changed) against the source file.

**Status (2026-09-04): Jonathan imported v37 into Make.com.** Same caveat as Addendum 19's Parent63 import — this session has no Make.com API access, so it's confirmed the file was imported, not confirmed that the production webhook's active scenario is actually `Child (Unified)37` rather than some other version. Not yet validated against a real generation. Follow-up for the next `sweep` (or any `has_accomplice=false`/`=true` purchase with several minor/no-enemy characters): after this checklist's usual manual character spot-check, explicitly re-run `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()` scoped to that package to get a real confirm/deny on whether v37 actually suppresses the empty-relationship leak, rather than waiting for it to resurface on its own.

**Closed the "2 unrelated `[choose ` hits" loose end instead of leaving it as vague backlog.** Read both: `Death At Villa Amore` and `Before The Nikah` both matched on `[choose a name or leave blank for host to personalise]` in the detective's opening statement — the exact same legitimate host-fill-in convention this checklist's step 5 already documents (analogous to `[MURDERER NAME]`-style brackets), not a leak at all. This was always a false positive in the *original* pre-Addendum-18 `\[choose ` marker (meant to catch authoring-instruction leaks like `[choose someone with a strong motive]`), just never previously surfaced because these 2 packages predate this detector or a prior sweep run. Confirmed via a corpus-wide check that no other package anywhere matches `\[choose ` with anything other than this exact host-fill-in phrase, so excluding it costs zero real detection coverage. Refined the marker to `\[choose (?!a name or leave blank)` (migration `meta_text_leak_detector_exclude_legitimate_choose_a_name_placeholder`). Re-ran the detector: **0 packages flagged corpus-wide** — the full backlog opened by Addendum 18/20, plus this pre-existing false positive, is now completely clear.

## Addendum 21 (2026-09-05): closed the "left as-is" gap from Addendum 2/3 — backfilled Cypress/Celine Beaumont's guilty branch, deliberately deferred for 11 days

A manual sweep (triggered by a request to investigate `package_completion_blocking_defects()` flagging `missing_round_content.Cypress/Celine Beaumont` on `b8428a57-1c2b-4bf1-881c-98c8436be6a9`, "Operation: Thirty & Murdery") found this wasn't a new incident — it's the exact gap Addendum 2/3 already root-caused and explicitly chose not to backfill on 2026-08-25: `round2_guilty`, `round3_guilty`, `round4_guilty`, and `final_guilty` (plus all four `_pointform` variants) were still `NULL` on this one character, out of 33 total, 11 days after the original incident.

**Why it resurfaced now.** The live `validate_package_characters()` trigger only fires on `UPDATE` to `mystery_packages` (ADR-0108), never on a direct edit to a `mystery_characters` row — so once this package's `generation_status` first read `'completed'`, nothing in the automated pipeline would ever re-check this specific character's fields again on its own. The gap was only ever going to be caught by a manual sweep calling `package_completion_blocking_defects()` directly, which is exactly what happened here.

**Confirmed the gap was still exactly as Addendum 3 left it** — `round2_innocent`/`round3_innocent`/`round4_innocent`, `round2_accomplice`/`round3_accomplice`/`round4_accomplice`, `final_innocent`/`final_accomplice`, and `reveal_confession_accomplice` all populated and correct; only the guilty branch (5 fields, matching Addendum 3's "one malformed call zeroes out everything it was responsible for" description) plus `reveal_confession_guilty` were empty. All 32 sibling characters have full guilty-branch content.

**Decided to backfill rather than re-defer**, since the detector is now actively flagging it on every manual sweep and the underlying `parse-claude-json` bug that caused the original failure was fixed the same day (Addendum 3) — a fresh regeneration attempt was very unlikely to repeat the exact same failure. Got explicit sign-off from Jonathan first (per his standing rule on paid API spend) with a ~$0.15–0.20 estimate before making any Anthropic call.

**Fixed:**
1. Confirmed the exact call contract via `regenerate-child-content`'s own documented POST body and `PromptGroup` definitions (`GROUP_ROUNDS_GUILTY` = `round2_guilty`/`round3_guilty`/`round4_guilty`/`final_guilty`, one call group, matching the Child blueprint's module-513 call boundary).
2. Ran a `dry_run: true` call first — confirmed the plan (1 target, 1 group, exactly the 4 requested fields, $0.10 estimate) before spending anything.
3. Ran the real call. Succeeded cleanly on the first attempt — all 4 fields generated, `outcome: "fixed"`, actual cost $0.10 (under the estimate, since only one call group was needed).
4. **Deliberately did not call `generate-pointform-summaries`** for the `_pointform` variants — same reasoning already on record in this ADR's main body (the "Deliberately NOT done" note on `reveal_confession_*_pointform`): that function has no field-level targeting, only a per-character `characterIds`/`characterName` filter, and would have re-summarized all ~20 already-correct pointform fields on this character (plus a second, unapproved Sonnet 5 call for `reveal_confession_*`) to backfill 4. Wrote the 4 `_pointform` fields by hand instead — zero additional API cost, matching the existing sibling-character bullet format (4-7 bullets, "- " prefix, ≤20 words each, no headers, no quoted dialogue) and grounded directly in the freshly-generated prose (the $310,000 unpaid commission motive, tour-guide method/opportunity, and the reveal confession).
5. Re-verified: `package_completion_blocking_defects()` now returns `NULL` for this package (was `["missing_round_content.Cypress/Celine Beaumont"]` before the fix). Manually re-checked all 8 fields (4 prose + 4 pointform) are non-null and read coherently against her established background/secret.

**Left open, matching the original scope decision:** `reveal_confession_guilty` (prose) and `reveal_confession_guilty_pointform` are still `NULL` — the fifth field from the same original Addendum 3 failure, but not checked by `package_completion_blocking_defects()`'s `missing_round_content` class (which only covers round2/3/4/final, not the reveal-confession beat) and outside the scope of what was approved for this spend. Per Addendum 2's original reasoning, this only matters if Recast/reassignment is ever used to make her the murderer — still true today, since `character_role` is unchanged (`suspect`). Flagged here rather than silently left for the next sweep to rediscover from scratch.

## Addendum 22 (2026-09-06): closed Addendum 21's remaining open item — `reveal_confession_guilty` backfilled, Cypress/Celine Beaumont now 100% complete

Follow-up requested the same way as Addendum 21: sign-off first, then execute. Got explicit approval for a second, separate ~$0.10 Anthropic spend (a distinct call group from Addendum 21's — `regenerate-child-content`'s `GROUP_REVEAL_CONFESSION`, not `GROUP_ROUNDS_GUILTY`) before touching anything.

**Fixed:**
1. Dry-ran `regenerate-child-content` with `fields: ["reveal_confession_guilty"]` first — confirmed the plan (1 target, group `reveal_confession`, exactly the 1 requested field, $0.10 estimate) and, critically, that requesting only `reveal_confession_guilty` does NOT pull in her already-correct `reveal_confession_accomplice` (the group's `fieldsToGenerate` is filtered to the intersection of the group's fields and the caller's requested fields — confirmed in code before relying on it, same mechanism verified in Addendum 21 for `GROUP_ROUNDS_GUILTY`).
2. Ran the real call — generated cleanly on the first attempt, actual cost $0.10. Confirmed via direct read afterward that `reveal_confession_accomplice` (1548 chars) was untouched.
3. Content reads coherently against her established guilty branch: same $310,000 unpaid-commission motive, the same tour-schedule opportunity, the same borrowed-vial/champagne-tray/orchid-scent method from `round2_guilty`/`round3_guilty`/`round4_guilty` — this is expected, since the prompt group's `anchorHints` show `final_guilty`/`final_accomplice` as coherence anchors when generating this field.
4. Hand-wrote `reveal_confession_guilty_pointform` (zero additional cost), matching the sibling-character format (7 bullets, confession-toned, grounded in the newly-generated prose) rather than calling `generate-pointform-summaries` — same reasoning as Addendum 21 point 4.
5. Re-verified: `package_completion_blocking_defects()` still returns `NULL`. Directly confirmed all 12 of this character's round/reveal fields (4 guilty prose + 4 guilty pointform + reveal_confession_guilty + pointform + reveal_confession_accomplice + pointform) are non-null. Cypress/Celine Beaumont is now the only one of the 33 characters in this package that's been individually field-by-field verified complete, rather than just clean-by-detector.

**Nothing left open on this character.** Both fields the original 2026-08-25 incident zeroed out are now fully backfilled across two separate, individually-approved spends ($0.20 total for this character's complete repair, plus the original $0.15 x 2 escalated-but-unapplied attempts on 2026-08-25).

## Addendum 23 (2026-09-06): corpus-wide check — the Cypress/Celine bug shape is confirmed a genuine one-off, zero other live instances

Follow-up requested to Addendum 21/22's own open item ("no corpus-wide check for other characters with the same single-character guilty-branch gap"). Ran a direct SQL query, not `package_completion_blocking_defects()`, scoped to the EXACT signature of this incident — a character where `round{N}_innocent IS NOT NULL AND round{N}_guilty IS NULL` (or the `final_` equivalent) — across every character in every package in the live database (169 packages, 87 `mystery_style='character'`):

```sql
-- per-character gap flags, then aggregate: packages with >=1 gapped character
-- AND >=1 character with both innocent+guilty populated (proves it's a
-- per-character anomaly, not a package-wide model mismatch)
```

**Result: zero rows.** No character, in any live package, currently has an innocent branch populated with a null guilty branch while any sibling character in the same package has both populated. Before this session's fix, Cypress/Celine Beaumont's row was exactly this shape (confirmed directly in Addendum 21/22's own investigation); now that it's repaired, this query returns nothing at all. **This confirms the 2026-08-25 `parse-claude-json` bug's damage was contained to the single incident already known and now fully repaired — not a wider silent-failure pattern.**

**Found something adjacent, deliberately NOT investigated further (Jonathan's call, scoped out this session):** the broader `package_completion_blocking_defects()` scan used to build the query above surfaced ~32 packages with SOME defect flagged (self_directed_question, meta_text_leak, identity_conflict, and a `missing_round_content` shape on several old packages). Spot-checked one — "Death On The Dance Floor" — and confirmed it's a *different* shape entirely: `round2_script` populated, `round2_innocent`/`round2_guilty`/`round2_questions` all null, i.e. an old single-script-model character mistagged/generated under `mystery_style='character'`. This exactly matches the historical corpus ADR-0096's own migration comment already named and sized (`Death On The Dance Floor` "17/18" characters affected, `Death At The Deadwood Saloon` "9/9" — both counts match this scan precisely) — a known, pre-existing, already-priced-in gap, not a new discovery from this session. **Left untriaged**, along with the rest of the ~32-package list — a real but separate investigation from what this addendum was scoped to answer.

## Addendum 24 (2026-09-07): sweep of "Murder In The Majlis" — clean on every automated/manual check except a stray trailing quote mark in 2 characters' guilty confessions, fixed

Routine New-Purchase sweep, conversation `e826a708-824c-49b6-858d-82f24106ef73`, package `a49181e0-a49f-499e-b491-98732799887d` (6-player, `mystery_style='character'` slip-style, no accomplice, generated 2026-09-06 — well post-ADR-0074 upgrade). All three scoped detectors (`list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`, `list_packages_with_victim_mismatch`, `list_packages_with_unresolved_victim_name`) returned zero rows, and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` returned null. Victim name ("Sheikha bint Hamdan Al Marri") cross-checked consistent across `game_overview`, `detective_script`, and all 6 characters' `background`/`relationships`/`secret` text. Full `detective_script` read clean — the only bracket present, `[The murderer (player) reads their confession aloud.]`, is the legitimate slip-style host-fill-in convention matching `game_overview`'s "the culprit is randomly selected at game time" design note. All 6 characters use the by-design dual-gender name convention (e.g. `Aisha/Aamir Al Mansoori`) per [[project_dual_gender_name_convention_sweep_note]] — not a leak.

Spot-checked all 6 characters' full content (not just 2-3, since the package is small) — `background`, `relationships`, `secret`, `rumors`, `reveal_confession_guilty`, `accusations` all coherent, no leaked authoring notes, no dangling references to removed characters (none were ever removed from this package). English-language, so the T-V register check (Addendum 13) doesn't apply.

**One real defect found and fixed:** 2 of 6 characters' (Hessa Al Nuaimi, Latifa Al Qassimi) `reveal_confession_guilty` prose ended with a stray, unpaired trailing single-quote character (e.g. `"...for her to spend.'"` with no opening quote anywhere in the field) — a generation artifact, since these fields are unquoted first-person monologue, unlike the `rumors`/`round*_questions` fields where every line is deliberately wrapped in matching quote marks (confirmed those were a false alarm on the same grep). The other 4 characters' `reveal_confession_guilty` and all 6 `_pointform` variants were clean. Fixed via a direct SQL `regexp_replace` stripping the trailing stray quote from just those 2 rows; re-ran all three detectors plus `package_completion_blocking_defects()` post-fix, still clean. Low severity (cosmetic, would only be noticed if this specific character drew the guilty slip and the host read the confession verbatim), but real — noting since a similar stray-trailing-punctuation shape hasn't been seen in a prior sweep and is worth having a name for if it recurs.

## Addendum 25 (2026-09-07): "Blood Moon Requiem" was clean content but stuck `needs_review` — traced to a month-old regression of ADR-0055's own fix, resolved

Sweep triggered by both a "New Purchase!" notification and a separate "Generation Display Issue" alert (later corroborated by a GitHub Actions health-check comment) for the same conversation `488f078b-349d-4066-9be8-18d3c50d03c9`, package `323e0cc7-801f-4d0e-b35c-69e36ebc4dfc` (14-player, `mystery_style='detective'`, no accomplice, generated 2026-09-07 03:35 — well post-ADR-0074). Content itself was clean: 14/14 characters, victim name ("Konstantin Draven") consistent across `game_overview`, `detective_script`, and 3 spot-checked characters' `background`/`secret`/`relationships`; `detective_script` brackets are all legitimate host-direction convention including a deliberate host tip pointing to real free tools (Google AI Studio / NotebookLM) for pre-recording audio — not a leak. Dual-gender name convention used throughout, correctly.

`generation_status.status = 'needs_review'` was real and correctly held: `package_completion_blocking_defects()` returned `self_directed_question.Sable/Saben Crimson` — a genuine chain-of-thought leak, not a false positive. Round 2's first question literally read `**To Sable/Saben Crimson (self-reflection aside; addressed instead to Ambrose/Ambrosia Sterling):**` — the model caught its own mistake mid-generation and left the correction as a printed annotation instead of just writing the corrected question.

This defect class has a known, free, deterministic auto-fix (ADR-0047/0055's `auto-remediate-packages` worker), but it hadn't fired despite ~3 hours elapsed. Root cause traced to a real, systemic regression — full account and fix in **[ADR-0055's Addendum](0055-widen-detector-rpcs-to-gate-held-packages.md#addendum-2026-09-07-the-fix-regressed-silently-for-a-month-found-via-a-routine-purchase-sweep-re-applied)**: a 2026-08-10 migration silently reverted ADR-0055's `needs_review`-visibility fix across all 8 detector RPCs it originally widened, live for a month before this sweep caught it. Fixed via a new migration re-applying the same widening (excluding the one detector created after ADR-0055 that was never part of its scope), then a scoped, zero-cost invocation of `auto-remediate-packages` (`{classes: ["self_directed_questions"], only_needs_review: true}`) to fix this customer's package immediately. Re-verified `package_completion_blocking_defects()` returns `NULL` and `generation_status.status` is now `completed`.

**Why this belongs in both ADRs:** the customer-facing symptom and its correct diagnosis (a real leaked chain-of-thought, not a false alarm) is a coherence-sweep finding: ADR-0103. The mechanism that stranded it — a shared status predicate silently drifting out of sync between the gate and the worker that heals it — is ADR-0055's problem domain, recurring for the second time in the same shape. Recording once each, cross-linked, rather than duplicating the technical account.

## Addendum 26 (2026-09-07): closed Addendum 24's open root cause — hardened `regenerate-child-content`'s JSON rules against the trailing-quote bug, not just the 2 rows

Addendum 24 fixed the 2 affected rows via direct SQL but explicitly left the generation-time cause untouched ("worth having a name for if it recurs"). Root cause: `CRITICAL_JSON_RULES`' rule 2 told the model to wrap "any quoted text inside string values" in single quotes — correct for `rumors`/`round*_questions`, whose schema template literally shows `'...'` around the text, but wrong for prose fields like `reveal_confession_guilty` and backgrounds/confessions, whose schema template shows plain unquoted monologue. The model generalized the instruction across both field types, occasionally leaving a lone trailing apostrophe on a field that was never supposed to be quoted at all — exactly the Hessa Al Nuaimi / Latifa Al Qassimi shape.

Reworded rule 2 to make the single-quote convention conditional on the field's own schema template rather than a blanket instruction, and extended rules 4/5's self-check pass to explicitly flag an unpaired trailing quote mark on an unquoted-prose field as a bug. Deployed to `supabase/functions/regenerate-child-content/index.ts`; confirmed live via `get_edge_function` (old blanket wording absent, new conditional wording present). Not independently re-verified against a fresh live generation yet — this closes the prompt-level root cause but the actual recurrence rate (zero vs. reduced vs. unchanged) is still an open question for the next sweep that happens to touch a package this pipeline regenerated.

## Addendum 27 (2026-09-07): sized the Addendum 24/26 trailing-quote bug across the full corpus, backfilled 28 confirmed rows, drafted the matching Make.com blueprint fix

Jonathan asked whether the Addendum 24 fix was systemic. It was — and a naive first pass badly overstated the scope, which is worth recording since the correction method is reusable. A "field ends with an apostrophe" scan across the 14 monologue-shaped fields (`round{2,3,4}_{guilty,innocent,accomplice}`, `final_{guilty,innocent,accomplice}`, `reveal_confession_{guilty,accomplice}`) flagged **3,853 hits across 48 packages** — almost all false positives. Many packages use a *different, valid* template convention where the entire field IS wrapped in matching quotes (e.g. `'I'll be honest with you—...'`), including a bulleted `- '...'` dialogue style my first structural check didn't parse, so it undercounted openings and overcounted "bugs."

Landed on two independent, cross-checked signals to isolate genuine cases: (a) a contraction-aware "real quote count is odd" parity check — strip only word-internal apostrophes (contractions like `don't`) before counting, which also flags true unpaired quotes while correctly excluding non-quote apostrophes like possessive plurals (`parents'` alone would otherwise false-positive); (b) a structural check that the field's actual prose content — after stripping markdown headers, bold labels, italic instructions, and bullet/numbered-list markers — does NOT itself open with a quote mark. Requiring **both** signals narrowed 3,853 raw hits down to **28 confirmed genuine cases** across 14 packages (13 `final_innocent`, 12 `reveal_confession_guilty`, 3 `reveal_confession_accomplice`). Manually read all 28 before touching anything, including one case (`Caleb`'s `reveal_confession_accomplice`) with a legitimate embedded mid-text quoted line *plus* a genuine stray trailing quote — confirmed the fix (strip only the final trailing quote character) correctly leaves the legitimate embedded quote pair untouched.

Backfilled all 28 via targeted SQL `regexp_replace` scoped by the exact confirmed-id set (not a blind global regex), one `UPDATE` per column. Re-ran `package_completion_blocking_defects()` across all 14 affected packages afterward — all clean. Re-ran the confirmed-case query once more post-fix: 0 remaining.

Drafted the equivalent prompt fix into the live Make.com child blueprint as `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)38-StrayApostropheGuard.blueprint.json` — the blueprint's own `<critical_json_rules>` block (2 wording variants across 8 module occurrences: modules 501/505/509 detective-style, 513/517 character-style) carries the same root-cause bug Addendum 26 fixed in `regenerate-child-content`, including an even more pointed smoking gun in the short variant: *"Rumor ends with one closing apostrophe"* — rumor-specific phrasing copy-pasted verbatim into the guilty/accomplice/confession modules, where it doesn't apply. Same rewording pattern applied to both variants, verified the JSON still parses and all 8 occurrences updated identically via a Python script (not hand-edited, to guarantee exact consistency across occurrences). **Imported into Make.com by Jonathan 2026-09-07**, same day — not yet confirmed as the scenario the production webhook actually calls, not yet validated against a real generation (same open items as every prior blueprint import, e.g. Addendum 20).

**Deliberately left untriaged:** ~183 additional fields corpus-wide have an odd real-quote-count but do NOT fail the "opens with quote" check — some other quote-parity anomaly (a genuinely unpaired *opening* quote with no close, a nested-quote edge case, or a markdown pattern this pass's structural stripper still doesn't handle). A real but separate investigation from what this addendum was scoped to fix.

**Note on this addendum's own provenance:** this session found and root-caused the same bug independently, in parallel with the session that shipped Addendum 26 — both landed on effectively identical prompt wording for `regenerate-child-content` (confirmed via `git show`, not just recollection). Only one deploy took effect (both were byte-identical, so no conflict); this addendum's contribution is the corpus sizing, the 28-row backfill, and the blueprint draft, none of which Addendum 26 covered.

## Addendum 28 (2026-09-07): closed Addendum 27's "left untriaged" item — the remaining odd-quote-count fields are a false positive, not a second bug; scoped to packages from the last ~2 months per Jonathan's direction

Jonathan's guidance: only investigate mysteries from the last couple of months, not the full historical corpus — reasonable, since older delivered packages aren't worth re-opening at this point. Re-ran the "odd real-quote-count" scan scoped to `mystery_packages.created_at >= '2026-07-01'`: 39 fields across 17 packages, of which 1 was a trailing-quote case that turned out to structurally open with a matching quote too (false positive, correctly excluded by the Addendum 27 structural check — confirms the backfill is complete with zero regressions in this window). The remaining 38 all share one shape: no opening quote, no closing quote, plain unquoted prose throughout, yet an odd "real" apostrophe count.

Manually read 2 full fields to find the actual source of the parity imbalance — both times it was a legitimate **possessive plural** apostrophe my contraction-stripping regex doesn't catch (it only strips apostrophes flanked by letters on both sides, e.g. `don't`, `Jack's`; a plural possessive like `husbands'` or `folks'` has a letter before the apostrophe but a space/punctuation after, so it survives the strip and reads as a "real" quote character). Adeline Voss's `round2_guilty`: *"my three husbands' deaths"*. Georgie Finch's `round4_guilty`: *"other folks' furniture"*. Both fields are otherwise clean, coherent prose with zero actual quote-wrapping bug — the odd parity is fully explained by ordinary English grammar, not a defect. Given both spot checks (chosen from opposite ends of the flagged list, different packages, different characters) landed on the identical explanation, and all 38 share the exact same structural signature (no open, no close), this closes the item: **no second bug exists in the packages checked.**

This does not clear the full historical corpus (packages older than ~2026-07-01) of the same false-positive noise — that's fine, since it was never a real defect to begin with, just an artifact of the detection heuristic itself. No further action needed; the detection method (not the data) was imprecise, and it's now understood well enough not to re-chase.

## Addendum 29 (2026-09-08): "missing characters" complaint was mid-generation viewing, not a real gap — but the sweep found and fixed a genuine garbled reveal-line bug

**Trigger:** Nathan Dubois's purchase ("The Death Of Nathan's Twenties", `conversation_id` `d21deb52-2a63-4d6e-bbb7-6c3f14c32160`, `package_id` `5849f5a2-b7ef-4cb1-9cf4-b240a03e734a`, player_count=8, mystery_style='detective', has_accomplice=false, purchased 2026-09-07, post-Sonnet-5-upgrade) came with a support email: "I required 8 characters... only 5 were actually generated... I don't see an option to add the missing 3 myself."

**Character-count complaint: not a current bug.** `mystery_characters` has all 8 rows for this package, each with full background/introduction/round-script content (2000+ chars) and matching the 8 named characters in the conversation's `additional_details`. `extracted_characters` also lists all 8. `package_completion_blocking_defects()` returns null. However, the character rows were written in three bursts — 5 by 21:32, 2 more by 21:41, the last (Yvonne) at 21:51 — while `mystery_packages.generation_completed_at` was stamped `completed`/100% at **21:33:32**, ~17 minutes before the roster actually finished. `ready_email_sent_at` (21:52) correctly waited for all 8, so the email itself was fine, but the package page has no "generating character N of 8" indicator — a customer who loads the page in that mid-generation window (as Nathan evidently did) sees a normal-looking, non-"Finalizing" tab view with only however many characters have landed so far, no signal that more are coming. Confirmed this is not unique to Nathan's package: scanning the last 14 days of completed packages, 4 of 28 (14%) show `generation_completed_at` preceding the last character's `created_at` — one (`Operation: Thirty & Murdery`, 30 players) by **~3 hours**. This is a systemic premature-completion-timestamp pattern, not a one-off. **Not fixed under this addendum** — it's a UX/progress-indicator gap requiring a product decision (add a live progress indicator vs. accept async streaming latency), logged instead as a vault note for Jonathan's call. Separately confirmed via code search: there is no self-serve "add a character" flow anywhere in the app (ADR-0038 explicitly rejected this as a feature — cast size is fixed at generation) — moot here since all 8 already exist, but worth knowing before replying to any future version of this complaint.

**Real bug found and fixed: garbled detective_script reveal line.** The climactic reveal in `detective_script` read: *"...Fernande Cole's killer — I place you under arrest for the murder of Nathan Cole."* Two problems: Fernande (the murderer, Nathan's personal assistant) has no surname "Cole" anywhere else in the package, and grammatically the line implies Fernande *has* a killer — contradicting the fact that she's the one confessing in the preceding paragraph. Almost certainly the model conflated the parallel-construction pattern used for the victim ("Nathan Cole") with the murderer's name mid-sentence. This is read aloud by the host at the literal climax of the game, in front of all guests, so severity is high despite being a single line. Fixed via a targeted `UPDATE ... replace()` scoped to this exact `package_id`, matching the exact original substring — changed to *"Fernande — I place you under arrest for the murder of Nathan Cole."* Re-verified: substring no longer present, replacement string present, `package_completion_blocking_defects()` still null post-fix. `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`, `list_packages_with_victim_mismatch`, `list_packages_with_unresolved_victim_name` all returned 0 corpus-wide for the current month (not a targeted signal for this specific bug, but a clean baseline). Manual cross-check of victim name ("Nathan"/"Nathan Cole") across `game_overview`, `detective_script`, and 4 characters' background/relationships (Fernande, Vanessa, Yvonne) found no other mismatch. Manual read of 2 characters' `accusations` field (Fernande, Mai — the two with unusually long accusations text vs. the other six's generic 189-char boilerplate) found deflection-note content consistent with the murderer/red-herring design, not a leak.

**Not logged as a CHANGELOG entry** — this was a one-package targeted SQL content fix (a garbled sentence in one customer's `detective_script`), not a code/pipeline change; it doesn't fit the "shipping behaviour" bar the CHANGELOG is for. The systemic premature-completion-timestamp finding is the durable artifact worth tracking going forward and is captured as a vault note instead (see below).

## Addendum 30 (2026-09-08): shipped the systemic fix for Addendum 29's "no progress indicator" finding

Addendum 29 flagged the gap but deliberately didn't fix it pending Jonathan's call on direction. He chose: add a progress indicator. Investigation found one already existed and was just being bypassed — `src/components/GenerationProgress.tsx` already renders a correct "N of M characters" screen with `charactersDone`/`charactersExpected`, wired up via `renderGenerationProgress()` in `MysteryView.tsx`. The actual bug was in the *reveal gate* deciding which screen to show: `MysteryView.tsx`'s condition for rendering the finished `MysteryPackageTabView` only checked `characters.length > 0` (at least one character), not whether the roster matched what was actually expected — so the moment `generation_status` flipped to `completed` (even prematurely, mid-roster) and `evidenceCards`/`detectiveScript` existed, it skipped straight to the full tab view with whatever partial roster had landed.

Fix: added `characterGateTarget` (expected character count, computed the same way as the existing progress bar, but capped at `player_count`) and `allExpectedCharactersPresent` (`characters.length >= characterGateTarget`), and used `allExpectedCharactersPresent` in place of the bare `characters.length > 0` check in both the reveal-gate condition and its complementary "fall back to progress screen" branch.

Two regression guards added after live-data testing surfaced real risk, not just theoretical:
1. **Capped at `player_count`.** `extracted_characters` has a known history of over-counting (ADR-0068/0069/0110/0120 — roster-parsing bugs). Querying currently-completed, paid packages found one live case (`eb0f3f1d-c404-49bb-8c7c-6daa135faa15`, "De Bittere Verjaardag"): `extracted_characters` says 18, but `player_count`=14 and `mystery_characters` actual count=14 — i.e., the package is genuinely complete and the extraction just over-counted. Without the cap, this (and at least 3 similar live packages found in the same query) would have been permanently stuck behind the progress screen post-fix, since `characters.length` can never reach an inflated target. `player_count` is the stable, customer-set ceiling a real roster should never exceed, so `characterGateTarget = min(extractedCount, player_count)`.
2. **4-hour freshness window (`generationRecentlyStarted`, gated on `generation_status.startedAt`).** Querying further turned up "Whispers From The Void" (`ffea7320-ba7e-48a3-a5c2-9fcf2ba8112a`) at 9 actual characters against `player_count`=10 — this is *intentional* (a Remove-a-Character adaptation, ADR-0036, confirmed by existing memory as a known-owned case, not a bug) and `player_count` never gets updated after a removal. Without a freshness gate, the stricter check would have permanently hidden this customer's own already-delivered, correctly-adapted package. The new check only applies while `generation_started_at` is within 4 hours (covers the worst observed real gap, ~3 hours on a 30-character package, with buffer) — older packages fall back to trusting `generation_status` alone, unchanged from prior behavior. Missing `startedAt` fails open (treated as not-recent), mirroring the existing `needsReviewWithinWindow` pattern's own null-handling philosophy in the same file.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint src/pages/MysteryView.tsx` both clean (zero new errors/warnings introduced — pre-existing `any`-type and hook-dependency warnings elsewhere in the file are untouched). Manually re-checked the fix's logic against real DB data for Nathan's package's actual historical timeline (5/8 characters at 21:33:32, `generation_started_at`=21:24:46 — well inside a 4-hour window — confirms the gate would have correctly shown the progress screen instead of the finished tabs) and against all 4 risky live edge cases found (Nathan's now-complete package, the extraction-inflation case, the Remove-a-Character case, and a 7-month-old 0-character "completed" package) — all resolve correctly with no regression, all now outside the freshness window by the time of testing. Did not attempt a live end-to-end browser reproduction inside the actual bug window (a genuinely-mid-generation paid package) — doing so would require either triggering a new real generation run (real Anthropic/Make.com spend, not authorized for a test) or waiting for a live purchase to land in exactly that ~20-40 minute window, neither of which fits this session.

Not touched: the underlying `generation_completed_at` timestamp staleness itself (Addendum 29's other finding) and the unidentified external writer that sets it prematurely — Jonathan's chosen fix addresses the customer-visible symptom directly and doesn't require resolving that upstream mystery. Left as-is per his decision (progress indicator only, not timestamp hardening).

## Addendum 31 (2026-09-08): two accomplice-branch content gaps found and fixed — plus closed the detection gap that let them ship unalerted, and a language-blind detector mistake caught mid-investigation

**Trigger:** sweep on a same-day purchase, "The Gods Must Be Gossiping: Murder At Aphrodite's Birthday Bash" (`conversation_id` `14d53082-df91-4eac-99f3-9c0c73e4ae1d`, `package_id` `ea610d24-c7c8-4c87-a842-59d64ffeabfb`, player_count=17, mystery_style='character', has_accomplice=false, EN, purchased 2026-09-08). All three named detectors and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` returned clean. Step 6's per-character spot-check found character **Pan/Pandora** missing `round2_accomplice`, `round3_accomplice`, `round4_accomplice`, `final_accomplice`, and `reveal_confession_accomplice` (prose AND pointform — 10 fields total, all null) while all 16 other characters had this content fully populated. The package's `detective_script` explicitly calls for an accomplice confession in the reveal ("I ask that person now, the one holding the accomplice slip, to step forward") — since role is drawn at the table in a slip-style game, any of the 17 players could have been dealt the accomplice slip and found the host had nothing to read them for 4 of the game's key beats. Fixed via `regenerate-child-content` (5 fields, $0.20) + `generate-pointform-summaries`; re-verified all 10 fields populated, content coherent (nice touch: Pan's confession references "down to my hooves," consistent with the character), zero detector regressions.

**Jonathan's question, mid-investigation: why wasn't I alerted — I only caught this because I happened to sweep.** Root-caused: `package_completion_blocking_defects()`'s `missing_round_content` check (ADR-0096) explicitly, deliberately excludes `round*_accomplice`/`final_accomplice` — the ADR's own text reasons this branch is "conditional on `conversations.has_accomplice`." That assumption is wrong for `mystery_style='character'` packages specifically: `validate_package_characters()` resets `conversations.has_accomplice` based on whether any `mystery_characters.character_role = 'accomplice'` is **pre-assigned** — which never happens in a slip-style game, since roles are drawn live at the table and never written to `character_role` at generation time. So `has_accomplice` for this style doesn't reflect whether the package's content includes an accomplice branch at all — it's simply not a reliable signal here, and today's package proves it: `has_accomplice=false` yet 16/17 characters correctly carry full accomplice content. `reveal_confession_guilty`/`reveal_confession_accomplice` were never checked at all, for any character, by this function.

**First attempt at a fix signal was itself wrong, language-blind — caught before shipping.** Initial hypothesis: gate the check on `detective_script ~* 'accomplice'` (does the package's own script establish an accomplice role exists). Verified this correctly distinguished packages with a legitimate accomplice role from murderer-only designs ("Murder In The Majlis" and 5 similar packages: `has_accomplice=false`, script never mentions "accomplice," and correctly have zero accomplice-branch content — not a defect). But running it against a Spanish-language package ("Veneno En La Medianoche," `package_id` `7c56a42d-a7ca-4a84-b7a0-0f507f32ea83`) proved the English keyword regex silently fails on non-English content — this package clearly has a real accomplice role (10/11 characters fully populated) but its script never contains the literal word "accomplice." This is exactly the English-pattern blind spot this ADR's step 6 already warns about for manual checks, now nearly baked into an automated one. Replaced with a language-independent **peer-existence signal**: if any *other* character in the same package has a given field populated, every character in a `character`-style package should (everyone might draw any role) — a real gap on a peer. A package with no accomplice role at all (nobody has the field) never trips this; no keyword-matching, no hardcoded rollout date needed for the newer `reveal_confession_*` fields either.

**Re-swept using the corrected peer-existence signal against all completed `character`-style packages.** Found exactly one more live instance, in the same Spanish-language package the language-blind attempt had exposed: **Aurelio Vanderbilt (Checho)** in "Veneno En La Medianoche," missing only `reveal_confession_accomplice` (round2/3/4/final_accomplice all present). Fixed the same way (1 field, $0.10 + pointform pass); verified the regenerated content came back correctly in Spanish ("Aurelio se pasa una mano por el rostro... 'No fui yo quien vertió el veneno'..."). Every other historical hit the sweep surfaced (~13 packages, 2025-08-14 through 2026-04-22, including Death On The Dance Floor and Death At The Deadwood Saloon by name) is the exact same pre-2026-08-11-fix backlog ADR-0096 already found and explicitly decided not to backfill ("a mystery party that old has almost certainly already been played") — confirmed via the peer-existence query, not re-litigated here.

**Shipped the detection fix**, via migration `add_missing_role_branch_content_defect`: extended `package_completion_blocking_defects()` with a new `missing_role_branch_content` defect class using the peer-existence gate, scoped to `mystery_style = 'character'`, covering `round2/3/4_accomplice`, `final_accomplice`, `reveal_confession_accomplice`, and `reveal_confession_guilty`. Verified read-only before deploying and again live after: correctly flags only the one real gap (Aurelio, pre-fix), correctly stays silent on Aphrodite (post-fix), Majlis (no accomplice role, by design), and the general case. Since `package_completion_blocking_defects()` is already wired into all three completion gates (ADR-0049's pattern), this required zero new wiring — same reuse principle ADR-0096 used originally.

**Explicitly scoped narrower than ADR-0096's full pattern, by choice:** ADR-0096 also widened `notify-generation-issue`'s auto-heal re-fire to match its new detection. Not done here — only 2 live instances found in the month since the last related fix landed (2026-08-11); wiring a new auto-heal path for a defect this rare is more standing machinery than the evidence currently supports. Alert-only (blocks completion status, fires the existing `notify-generation-issue` email) for now. Revisit if this recurs.

**Not done here:** root-causing *why* a single character out of many occasionally drops one field from an otherwise-successful batch response (the same "successful-but-incomplete" shape ADR-0096 flagged as unresolved for the original round-content class, and the same shape the `generate-pointform-summaries` code comment already documents for `reveal_confession_accomplice` on Haiku specifically — though this gap is in the Child *generation* call, not the pointform summarizer, and occurred at a much lower rate: 2 characters out of several hundred checked). This addendum closes the "ships undetected" gap, not the upstream generation-side cause — consistent with ADR-0096's own stated scope.

## Addendum 32 (2026-09-08): hardened `generation_completed_at` too — Jonathan opted for the cheap fix, skipped the Make.com blueprint hunt

After Addendum 30 shipped, discussed whether to also chase down the exact external writer responsible for the stale `generation_completed_at` (would require Make.com blueprint access this session doesn't have, and this ADR's own history of blueprint hunts — Addenda 19, 20, 27 — shows a pattern of real time spent for uncertain payoff, several still "not yet confirmed as the scenario the production webhook actually calls" days/weeks later). Before recommending, grepped every consumer of `generation_completed_at` across `src/`, `supabase/functions/`, `api/`: confirmed nothing reads its *value*, only null/not-null truthiness — so the stale timestamp has zero live customer-facing effect today. Recommended skipping the blueprint hunt but doing the cheap, self-contained trigger hardening (no blueprint needed, closes the gap regardless of writer, per ADR-0108's own stated philosophy). Jonathan agreed.

Fix: `validate_package_characters()`'s existing validated-clean branch (the `ELSE` of the `array_length(_reasons,1) > 0` check, which already correctly re-validates every transition into `completed`) now also stamps `NEW.generation_completed_at := now()`, overriding whatever any caller — this repo's `generation-complete.js`, the client self-heal in `mysteryPackageService.ts`, or Make.com's Child scenario writing directly — set on a premature attempt. Confirmed `promote_complete_packages()` (a plain `UPDATE`, no trigger bypass) is unaffected: its own precondition (`generation_completed_at IS NOT NULL`) is still satisfied by whatever the original premature writer set during the demoted interim state; this only refines the value at the moment genuine completion is actually confirmed.

Verified live (not just read the code): ran a `BEGIN`/`ROLLBACK`-wrapped transaction against Nathan's real package — forced `generation_completed_at` to a deliberately-stale `2020-01-01` alongside a fake `in_progress`→`completed` re-transition, confirmed the trigger overwrote it with a fresh timestamp (`2026-09-08 07:47:07`, matching test execution time), then confirmed the rollback left production data at its original (still historically-stale, unbackfilled) `21:33:32` value — no retroactive correction was attempted or warranted, this only prevents the bug going forward. Migration: `supabase/migrations/20260908074900_stamp_generation_completed_at_on_validated_completion.sql`.

## Addendum 33 (2026-09-08): health-check evidence-image alert was a self-resolved generation-order race, not a defect — but the sweep found and fixed a real inverted-motive bug in a character's secret

**Trigger:** a health-check GitHub Actions comment flagging "The Last Supper At Ravenscroft Manor" (`conversation_id` `b58b507b-f2e3-429e-bc5e-56b85f635ef7`, `package_id` `50817047-0bcf-46fe-9f21-55f701ccfd00`, player_count=6, mystery_style='character', has_accomplice=false, EN, purchased 2026-09-08) as a paid package missing evidence-card images, arriving alongside the purchase notification itself — same-day, post-Sonnet-5-upgrade.

**Evidence-image alert: not a live defect, a timing race.** `list_packages_missing_evidence_images()` (ADR-0016) flags a package when its `generation_status` is `completed`/`needs_review` but `evidence_card_images` is missing a round key the `evidence_cards` text references. Confirmed the GitHub comment posted at 16:42:53 UTC; all three evidence images (round2/3/4) landed in `storage.objects` between 16:43:07 and 16:43:30 — 14 to 37 seconds *after* the health check ran, with `generation_completed_at` not stamped until 16:54:00. The package's `generation_status` must have read `completed`/`needs_review` in that ~15-second window before the images finished uploading, which is the same premature-status shape as Addendum 29/30's reveal-gate bug and Addendum 32's `generation_completed_at` race, just hitting a different consumer (the health check's own query, not customer-facing UI). Re-ran the detector live post-sweep: package no longer appears, all three images confirmed present and correctly sized (1.0–1.4MB each) in storage. No fix needed here — Addendum 30's `MysteryView.tsx` reveal-gate fix already protects the customer-facing symptom of this same race; the health check catching a mid-flight state and self-clearing on the next run is expected behavior for a scheduled poll, not a new gap.

**Real bug found: Winston/Winnie Hargrove's `secret`/`secrets` fields had the pension theft backwards.** Every other piece of this character's content — `description`, `background`, all four `round*_innocent`/`round*_guilty` branches, both `final_innocent`/`final_guilty`, `reveal_confession_guilty`, the rumor another character spreads about them, and the plot's own `master_context` (`howVictimWrongedThem`, `specificMotive`) — agree Reginald diverted ~£9,000 from Winston's pension and threatened them into silence when they objected. Only `secret` and `secrets` (the two duplicate-format columns, same shape as the round-script dual-format gap in [[reference_round_script_formats]]) told the opposite story: that *Winston* had embezzled the £9,000 to cover a private debt and preemptively raised it with Reginald to deflect suspicion, which Reginald "saw through." A player reading this character's own materials would get two irreconcilable accounts of who stole from whom — high severity, since this is exactly the kind of self-contradiction step 6 of this checklist exists to catch, and it lands on the motive text for a character who might be dealt the murderer slip.

No `master_context` field defines a second hidden fact for Winston beyond the pension grievance, so rather than invent a new plot beat, rewrote `secret`/`secrets` to correct the misattribution while adding a self-contained detail consistent with everything else already established: Winston secretly drafted (but never sent) resignation letters to two other households in the days after the threat, evidence their anger had moved from muttering toward a concrete plan to leave — a genuine hidden fact distinct from the already-public grievance, structured the same "why this matters if exposed" way as the other five characters' secrets. Fixed via targeted `UPDATE` scoped to this `package_id` + character name only; verified both columns now match and read consistently with the rest of the package. All three named detectors and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` stayed clean before and after. Not logged as a CHANGELOG entry, matching Addendum 29's precedent — a one-package, one-character targeted content fix, not a code/pipeline change.

## Addendum 34 (2026-09-08): root-caused Addendum 33's secret-inversion bug to a real gap in the live prompt — fix drafted, not yet imported

Jonathan asked how often this had occurred and how likely it was to recur before agreeing to spend on an LLM-based detector. Answered both with evidence instead of a guess.

**Frequency check (free, no API spend):** pulled 15 other characters across 8 other packages whose `background` or `secret` matched a theft/embezzlement keyword regex (`diverted|embezzl|stole|stolen|siphon|skimm|forg|padded|misappropriat|took...money`) and read each by hand. Of those, 3 had the fact stated in *both* fields where a reversal was actually checkable (Persephone, Father Cynric/Sister Cynwyn, Anna) — all three internally consistent, no reversal. Zero additional instances found in this sample. Small and non-random (keyword-filtered, not a full corpus read), but real evidence rather than a guess, and cheap enough to do before spending anything.

**Root cause found: this is a real, live gap, not a random one-off.** Checked the `<content_coherence_rules>` block in `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)38-StrayApostropheGuard.blueprint.json` — the actual production Child blueprint that generated Winston's package (confirmed same schema also mirrored in `supabase/functions/regenerate-child-content/index.ts`'s `GROUP_IDENTITY`). The `background` field's schema template explicitly instructs "*pull specific details from master_context*"; the `secret` field's template has no equivalent grounding instruction — nothing tells the model to check the secret against the background it just wrote two fields earlier in the same completion. `description`, `background`, `relationships`, `secret`, and `introduction` are all generated together in one call (confirmed via the same schema block appearing 8 times, once per character-slot module, matching the pattern already established in Addendum 27) — so this is a within-completion reasoning gap, not two independent calls drifting apart. Given the gap is real and specifically likely to bite on financial/theft-shaped motives (a common trope the model can reach for independently of what it already wrote), called this "plausible, not rare" going forward until closed, despite the clean sample.

**Recommended a one-line prompt fix over an ongoing LLM-based detector** — fixes the cause for free instead of paying to catch the symptom after the fact (~$5-10/month for a semantic-contradiction checker, per the cost breakdown discussed but not commissioned). Jonathan agreed.

**Fix drafted:** `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)39-SecretMotiveConsistencyGuard.blueprint.json`, built off v38 with a single new bullet inserted into `<content_coherence_rules>` (added to all 8 per-character modules, same pattern as Addendum 20/27's blueprint fixes): *"SECRET vs. ESTABLISHED MOTIVE: If this character's background already establishes a specific wrong the victim did TO them ... the secret field must NOT invert that direction ... Before writing the secret, re-read the background paragraph you just wrote above and confirm who-did-what-to-whom stays the same."* Verified via script: exactly 8 occurrences of the anchor text found and replaced, resulting file still valid JSON, same module count (8 `anthropic-claude:createAMessage` calls) and same top-level structure as v38 — nothing else touched. **Imported into Make.com by Jonathan 2026-09-08** — not yet confirmed as the scenario the production webhook actually calls, not yet validated against a real generation (same open items as every prior blueprint import in this ADR). Does not retroactively fix the 167 existing packages; only prevents new ones from getting this specific shape of bug.

## Addendum 35 (2026-09-09): `missing_role_branch_content` caught a fresh live instance exactly as designed — two characters, `regenerate-child-content` fix run with explicit spend approval

**Trigger:** sweep on a same-day purchase carrying a `needs_review` alert, "Blood Tide: The Cove Of Broken Oaths" (`conversation_id` `874f4f59-3a3b-4bce-93df-80a8a882fcc5`, `package_id` `f40984b1-8b95-4df0-b589-5e75b81e4127`, player_count=18, mystery_style='character', has_accomplice=true, EN, purchased 2026-09-09). All three named detectors (meta-text leak, victim mismatch, unresolved victim name) came back clean. `package_completion_blocking_defects()` — the function Addendum 31 built specifically for this defect class — flagged `missing_role_branch_content` for exactly 2 of 18 characters: **The Beachcomber, Wick/Wanda Orlov** (entire accomplice branch null: `round2/3/4_accomplice`, `final_accomplice`, `reveal_confession_accomplice`, all 5 fields) and **Netmaker Tomas/Talia Finn** (only `final_accomplice` null, rest of that character's accomplice branch present). All other 16 characters had complete accomplice content — confirmed by hand querying null-ness of all 5 accomplice-branch fields across every character in the package, not just trusting the defect list.

**Fix, with an explicit spend gate this time.** Unlike Addendum 31 (same-session judgment call), this fix required a live Anthropic API call, so — per the global no-paid-API-without-permission rule — ran `regenerate-child-content` in `dry_run` mode first (free) to get an exact plan (2 Claude calls / $0.20 for Beachcomber's 5 fields across the `rounds_accomplice` + `reveal_confession` prompt groups; 1 Claude call / $0.10 for Netmaker's single field), presented it to Jonathan, and got explicit go-ahead before spending. Both live calls returned `"outcome":"fixed"` with all requested fields `"status":"generated"` — actual spend $0.30, matching the dry-run estimate exactly.

**Re-verification, not just trusting the tool's own success response:** re-ran `package_completion_blocking_defects()` (returned `null` — no defects) and all three named detectors (all zero rows) post-fix.
