## Addendum 47 (2026-09-16): "Festa Fatal: Os 34 Anos Que Ninguém Vai Esquecer" — murderer's own materials cited the wrong blackmail amount; a new numeric-contamination variant of the cross-field-mismatch bug class

New-Purchase sweep, triggered by a Generation Display Issue alert firing on this package mid-generation (`641044a5-e443-42eb-a39d-9094ab3a14d3`, conversation `5ee80c28-c557-4cfa-ae7c-19a8357f1df9`, $19.99, `sesevignesilva@gmail.com`, purchased 2026-09-16, `mystery_style='detective'`, `script_type='both'`, 17 players, `has_accomplice=true`, `pt` (Brazilian Portuguese — theme text and currency confirm `pt-BR`, not `pt-PT`), post-ADR-0074).

**The alert itself, resolved by standing infrastructure, not this sweep.** At alert time: 12/17 characters generated, 5 missing rows entirely, 3 with empty content, 7 marked "Auto-Recovery Capped" (hit the 2-attempt/$10 shared-spend cap on the immediate webhook re-fire, "will not retry itself" per the alert's own text). Live DB checks during this sweep showed that claim was stale in practice: 6 of those 7 "capped" characters (Ana Carolina, Davi, Sandra Denise, Samira, Camila, Claudiane) were recovered during the sweep window (12:33–12:38) by the standing self-heal cron infrastructure (`sweep_incomplete_packages_2min`, `heal_completed_packages_2min` — both every 2 min; `auto-remediate-held-packages` — every 5 min; `auto-remediate-packages` — `:13,:43`) — evidently not subject to the same per-character cap as the immediate webhook re-fire path. As of this sweep's close, 2 characters (Adriano Nacife, Verônica) still had empty round content and 1 (Paulo César) still had no row at all — visibly still converging (most recent auto-heal touch 4 minutes before the last check), so no manual intervention taken; flagged for a follow-up check rather than racing the cron with a manual retry.

**The real finding: a numeric mismatch in the murderer's own materials.** Manual cross-check (background/secret/relationships) on Júnior (murderer) and Luiza Nacife (accomplice) against `master_context` and the `detective_script` REVEAL, per the checklist's standard step. Júnior's `background` and `secret` both said Lucas blackmailed him for "R$25.000" mensais. Everywhere else in the package — `master_context` (7+ separate references), `detective_script`'s spoken REVEAL ("chantagem de trinta mil reais mensais"), and Marcela's content — the canonical figure is R$30.000. R$25.000 turned out to be a real number in this package, just belonging to someone else: Marco Antônio's old, unrelated personal debt from Lucas, correctly cited by Claudiane and Verônica's own secrets (verified both those hits are legitimate — checked before assuming Júnior's was the only occurrence in the package). This reads as cross-character numeric contamination during content generation, not a copy-paste-style same-field inversion (Addendum 33's bug shape) or a narration-person bug (Addendum 45) — a new variant worth naming for pattern-matching on a future sweep: **a character's own field citing a number that is canonically correct elsewhere in the package, but for a different character's subplot.**

Fixed via direct `UPDATE` on `mystery_characters` — confirmed exactly one `R$25.000` occurrence in each of `background` and `secret` before writing (a targeted string replace, not a blanket regex, per the established bulk-regex-caution discipline), then re-verified both fields now read `R$30.000` and no `R$25.000` remains on that row.

**Verification beyond the one fix.** All four standard SQL detectors scoped to this package — `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`, `list_packages_with_victim_mismatch`, `list_packages_with_unresolved_victim_name`, `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch` — clean. `package_completion_blocking_defects()` (live, real-time) correctly tracked the in-progress recovery, only ever flagging characters actually still empty at query time. Full `detective_script` read end to end: victim name (Lucas Nacife) consistent with `game_overview` and every character reference checked; only bracketed content is legitimate host stage-direction (detective-style, not slip-style, so no host-fill-in convention to misapply); no leaked meta-text, no garbled sentences. Manual full-content read of Júnior, Luiza Nacife (accomplice), and Karen (the pill source referenced in the REVEAL's method beat) — all first-person, no self-reference/narration-person issues, no secret-vs-background inversions, register consistent (BR Portuguese's `você`-only convention makes the tu/você check from other sweeps largely inapplicable here).

**Not done:** no detector built for this bug class — first occurrence, matches this ADR's own 2+-occurrence bar (Addendum 31) for genuine semantic/numeric-contradiction defects, which would need something like a cross-field LLM-judge check rather than a cheap SQL pattern; a naive "flag any character field containing a currency amount that doesn't match that character's own `master_context` entry" detector is plausible but not attempted here on one instance. No sweep of other completed `mystery_style='detective'` packages for the same defect shape — only this package was in scope.

### Key files (Addendum 47)
- `mystery_characters` — `background`, `secret` fixed for `character_name='Júnior'`, `package_id='641044a5-e443-42eb-a39d-9094ab3a14d3'`

**Correction (same day, follow-up investigation):** the recovery-attribution above was wrong. `auto_remediation_log` has zero rows for this package after 12:32:02, and `auto-remediate-packages`'s `DEFECT_CLASSES` map has no entry that could have produced the `regenerate_character:<name>` action names seen in the log — so none of the 4 standing self-heal crons named above plausibly recovered the 6 characters. The real, evidence-backed explanation: this was never a stalled generation. The original Make.com Child scenario was still actively running the full 17-character `script_type='both'` cast (per-character finish timestamps span 12:28:20–12:40:09, a ~12-minute tail consistent with ADR-0097's ~100s/character estimate) — `notify-generation-issue`'s alert fired mid-run off a 3-minute quiet-period gate keyed to the single most-recent character write, misread the still-progressing package as stuck, fired its own redundant `regenerate_character` recovery webhooks (13 at 12:20:04, up to 17 more at 12:26:17–19) that raced the original generation, and those redundant calls are what actually burned the 2-attempt-per-character cap (confirmed: all 7 "capped" characters hit exactly 2 `regenerate_character:*` attempts by 12:26:19). The 6 that later completed almost certainly did so because the original, already-paid generation simply finished them itself, not because of any cron. Only Adriano Nacife, Verônica, and Paulo César were genuinely still short after that.

**Resolution (same day, ~13:46 UTC):** the 3 remaining characters did not self-heal — 46+ minutes after `generation_completed_at` they were unchanged, and Adriano Nacife had already shown one reproducible micro-failure of his own (a manual retry rewrote his `background` cleanly but left round content empty a second time), ruling out "still converging." Jonathan asked whether Make.com had run out of operations credits; DB evidence argued against a global outage — the same redundant re-fire webhooks hit most of the other 14 characters too, and those completed successfully scattered across a 12-minute window rather than stopping at a hard cutoff, which a credits exhaustion would produce. Manually re-fired the same `CHILD_WEBHOOK` Make.com endpoint `notify-generation-issue` itself uses (`https://hook.eu2.make.com/3l26wasbsjzh5396np25qoyv8g82u6j3`) directly for these 3 names, bypassing only that function's own attempt-cap bookkeeping (not a new mechanism — same search-then-update-or-create webhook, same payload shape, real Sonnet 5 spend, ~$0.15/character, ~$0.45 total, approved by Jonathan before firing) — logged the 3 calls into `auto_remediation_log` to keep the audit trail consistent with the automatic path. All 3 completed cleanly within ~90 seconds (confirmed via Make.com's own execution log showing no incomplete runs, and via DB: full background/4 rounds/final statement for all 3, Paulo César's row created). **Final verification: 17/17 characters present, `package_completion_blocking_defects()` returns `null`, all 4 standard SQL detectors clean.** Package fully resolved and customer-ready.

## Addendum 48 (2026-09-17): "Dead On Arrival: The Blackwood Victory" — a supporting character's own materials disagreed on her title, a new (minor) cross-field-mismatch variant

New-Purchase sweep on `e2c2027c-bbe8-4345-a438-4841d2a52360` (conversation `36f87bfd-33c4-45b8-9cb2-fb69f9250b38`, $24.99, `sfulm24@gmail.com`, purchased 2026-09-17, `mystery_style='character'`, `script_type='both'`, 10 players, `has_accomplice=true`, English, well post-ADR-0074). All 4 standard SQL detectors (`list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`, `list_packages_with_victim_mismatch`, `list_packages_with_unresolved_victim_name`, `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch`) came back clean, and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` returned `null`.

**The finding.** This package uses the documented dual-name convention (`project_dual_gender_name_convention_sweep_note`) on a supporting character's *title* rather than her first name: `character_name = "Deacon/Reverend Evangeline White"`. The convention is by design and resolves correctly almost everywhere it appears — as the literal dual form in structural headers (her own `background`'s `**Name:**` line; two peer characters' bolded relationship headers; the `round4_questions` label used by all 7 other characters checked), and as a single resolved title in natural first-person prose (her own `introduction` says "Reverend Evangeline White"; one peer's relationship prose independently also resolved to "Reverend"). The one outlier: her own `description` field — a *different* solo field about the same character — said "**Deacon** Evangeline White," disagreeing with her own `introduction`'s "**Reverend**." Same character, two of her own fields, two different titles for herself. Low severity (cosmetic, both are plausible clergy titles, no plot/logic impact) but a genuine coherence bug of the same general family as Addendum 33's secret-inversion and Addendum 47's numeric contamination: a fact about a character disagreeing with itself across that character's own fields, just on a name/title instead of a motive or a number.

Fixed via direct `UPDATE` on `mystery_characters.description` for this row — confirmed exactly one occurrence of "Deacon Evangeline White" before writing, replaced with "Reverend Evangeline White" (matching the majority resolution used in her own `introduction` and the one peer reference), then re-verified the field now contains zero "Deacon" occurrences and one "Reverend."

**Not done:** no detector built — first occurrence of this specific shape (a resolved-name/title mismatch on a *non-victim* character, across that character's own fields rather than against `master_context`), matching this ADR's 2+-occurrence bar (Addendum 31) for defects that would need something semantic rather than a cheap SQL pattern. `list_packages_with_unresolved_victim_name` is scoped to the victim only by design and wouldn't have caught this even if widened without changes. Full manual read otherwise clean: `game_overview` and `detective_script` both consistent on victim name (Madeline Schultz Blackwood) and plot beats; evidence cards match the REVEAL's method references; spot-checked two full character packets (Mrs. Ivy Sable — victim's estranged sister — and cross-referenced her `reveal_confession_guilty` naming Miss Scarlett as accomplice against Scarlett's own `reveal_confession_accomplice`, which independently corroborates the same pairing) with no secret/background inversions, no narration-person issues, no leaked brackets or authoring notes; no T-V register check applicable (English).

### Key files (Addendum 48)
- `mystery_characters.description` — fixed for `character_name='Deacon/Reverend Evangeline White'`, `package_id='e2c2027c-bbe8-4345-a438-4841d2a52360'`

## Addendum 49 (2026-09-19): Terminus 13's customer flagged low Remembered representation post-delivery — traced to a visibility gap, not a missing-content bug; 2 characters' descriptions/introductions corrected

A week after the Addendum 43 roster completion, the customer (Angie Smedley) emailed: she'd only found 2 Remembered characters ("Sundberg and Fairweather") and asked whether at least 3 more could be changed to Remembered. Worth checking directly against ground truth rather than assuming she was wrong or that content was actually missing — 5 of the 13 characters added in Addendum 43 were seeded as Remembered (Cole, Sato, Winthrop, Achebe, Renner, per that script's `NEW_CHARACTERS` seed descriptions), so the real question was why 5 real Remembered characters weren't registering for her.

**Confirmed live: `character_name` is the only place "Remembered" appears for 2 of 28 characters** (Sundberg, Fairweather — both literally titled "The Newly/First Remembered, [name]"). The other 5 Remembered characters' *titles* are purely occupational (Registry Advocate, Records Keeper, Groundskeeper, Medical Aide, Outbreak Survivor) even though 4 of the 5 (Cole, Sato, Winthrop, Achebe) clearly and unambiguously state Remembered identity in their own `description` — Achebe's and Cole's lead with it in the first sentence — which is the single strongest evidence this is a visibility gap (skimming 28 names/titles, not a missing-content bug): anyone reading those 4 descriptions in full would already have found them.

**Renner was the one genuine exception — a real content gap, not just visibility.** `background` clearly states "Renner became Remembered — transformed by whatever the outbreak leaves in its survivors" and the `**Role:**` header even reads "Long-time Remembered resident" — but `description` and `introduction` both drifted into ambiguous "works among/helps the Remembered community" phrasing that reads as an ally or helper, never actually stating Renner *is* one. This is an internal inconsistency between fields on the same character, not a stale assumption to correct against — fixed by aligning `description`/`introduction` with what `background` already established, not inventing new lore.

**Considered and rejected: renaming the 5 characters' titles to match Sundberg/Fairweather's convention** (the fix that would most directly address "I can only tell from the name"). Checked cross-references first: all 5 are referenced by title dozens of times across at least 10 other characters' `relationships`/`rumors`/`round*_questions` fields (e.g. "Registry Advocate Zephyrine Cole," consistently, everywhere). A title rename would require rewriting every one of those references to stay consistent — a large, error-prone bulk edit across many characters' fields for a problem the description-level fix already solves without touching anything else. Not done.

**Sato's Remembered status is deliberately absent from her own `introduction` — correct by design, not a gap to close.** Her `description`/`secret` establish she's concealing her transformation even from the other characters at the table ("what no one in the settlement knows is that Briallen is Remembered... the Registry's classification reviews are the single greatest threat to her survival"); making her announce it in her Round 1 self-introduction would break the concealment her own character concept is built around. Left untouched — explained to the customer instead of "fixed."

**Fixed directly** (hand-edited text, no Anthropic call — same practice as the Addendum 37 "Scavenger Row" rename): Renner's `description` ("Renner works among the Remembered community" → "Renner is one of the Remembered — transformed in those same first chaotic days — and has settled into... that community") and `introduction` ("I help the newly Remembered find their footing" → "I have been Remembered myself since those first days, and I help..."); Winthrop's `introduction` gained one added sentence acknowledging her already-established Remembered identity, matching her `description`'s existing "Tallis is a Remembered" — Winthrop's `description` needed no change, already clear. Confirmed exact-substring match before each replace (aborts loudly if the target text isn't found verbatim, same discipline as every other hand-edit in this ADR). Re-verified: `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()` and `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch()` (ADR-0103 Addendum 45's detector) both clean post-edit.

**Net result reported to the customer: 6 characters now clearly, independently identifiable as Remembered** (Sundberg, Fairweather by name; Achebe, Cole, Winthrop, Renner by reading their own materials) — satisfies "at least 3 more" — plus Sato as a 7th whose Remembered status is an intentional secret revealed through play rather than stated upfront.

### Key files (Addendum 49)
- `mystery_characters.description`/`introduction` — fixed for `character_name` in `('Outbreak Survivor Isolde/Isambard Renner', 'Groundskeeper Talon/Tallis Winthrop')`, `package_id='e86e0bee-560e-435b-910a-5e636e0421bc'`

## Addendum 50 (2026-09-19): "Attendance For Dinner: A Hinwick House Mystery" — package clean, but the sweep's `player_count` cross-check surfaced a live three-way cap mismatch, fixed under new ADR-0126

New-Purchase sweep on package `04110b77-ba3c-45d0-bb51-03fcbe1a2ba1` (conversation `3f98e184-f0a9-46aa-9064-04a04ee706db`, $24.99, `jonpineda1988@gmail.com`, purchased 2026-09-19, `mystery_style='detective'`, `script_type='full'`, `player_count=32` at time of purchase, `has_accomplice=true`, English, well post-ADR-0074). All 5 standard detectors (`list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`, `list_packages_with_victim_mismatch`, `list_packages_with_unresolved_victim_name`, `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch`, `package_completion_blocking_defects`) came back clean.

**Package content: clean, no fix needed.** Victim name ("Thomas Ainsworth") consistent across `game_overview`, `detective_script`, and evidence cards. The reveal's climactic "lantern" detail — Donald Cope's habitual evening lantern found untouched, proving he lied about his walking route — appears nowhere in the public-facing evidence cards or round scripts, which only established a pocket-watch/seating-chart time window; traced this to `master_context`'s `round4.murdererFlaw` field before flagging it as a hallucination, and confirmed it's an intentional design pattern (a reveal-only flourish tying evidence together, not evidence players needed for their own accusations, which the pocket-watch + Round 3 grounds-knowledge evidence already narrows to Donald via elimination) — not a bug. 5 characters spot-checked (Donald Cope, Robin Williams, Jacob Aland, Joanna James, Marcia Pineda) with no leaked authoring notes, no secret/background inversions, no narration-person issues, no dangling references. No T-V register check applicable (English).

**The finding was in `conversations.player_count`, not the package.** `mystery_characters` had 38 rows for this package against `conversations.player_count = 32` — a discrepancy this ADR's own checklist doesn't explicitly test for, but worth cross-checking since a stale count feeds other automation (completion gating, `notify-generation-issue` scaling). Chat transcript confirmed the customer explicitly negotiated the roster from 32 up to 35, then 37, then 38 during concept chat, locking in "38 players confirmed, no typos left to chase" before generating — the 38-character package is exactly what was ordered, not a generation defect.

Root cause, confirmed via `function_logs`: `mystery-webhook-trigger`'s auto-sync (added specifically to fix the earlier Fotini "Multiverse" `player_count`-drift incident) correctly detected the drift and attempted `UPDATE conversations SET player_count = 38`, but the write was rejected by `conversations_player_count_check`, hard-capped at `<= 32` — a number that had never been a deliberate hard limit, just wherever the DB constraint happened to be set before the setup form and marketing copy independently drifted to a 35 cap. The failure was only a `console.warn`, no alerting, so it went unnoticed until this sweep. Tracing it further surfaced a second, more severe, not-yet-independently-reported failure mode: any new customer entering 33-35 players on the setup form (a range the form's own Zod schema validates as allowed) would have their `conversations` row insert rejected outright by the same constraint, surfacing only as a generic "Error saving mystery" toast.

**Fixed under new ADR-0126** (raised the technical ceiling to 50 — DB constraint, `mystery-webhook-trigger`'s roster-plausibility checks, and a new shared `MAX_ROSTER_SIZE` constant — while deliberately leaving the customer-facing 35 cap unchanged; also fixed an adjacent, previously-unnoticed 32-vs-35 inconsistency in `mystery-ai`'s own chat-side player-count detection regexes). This ticket's `conversations.player_count` backfilled from 32 to 38 as part of that fix. See ADR-0126 for the full decision, alternatives considered, and product framing (32 was always meant as a soft "recommended cast size" guide, not a hard wall — confirmed directly rather than assumed).

### Key files (Addendum 50)
- `conversations.player_count` — backfilled to 38 for conversation `3f98e184-f0a9-46aa-9064-04a04ee706db`
- See ADR-0126 for the full set of code/schema changes (this addendum only covers what the sweep found and where the fix landed)

## Addendum 51 (2026-09-20): "Murder At The 19th Hole: The Viagha Affair" — a fully-custom submitted plot was silently rewritten mid-concept-chat, and the detective's name didn't survive into the generated script either; new bug class, prompt guardrail shipped same session

New-Purchase sweep on package `7aa38e75-ed3d-47b2-b45f-313eea08d0cd` (conversation `974680de-b6dc-4b06-8070-d8bbcb91342e`, $19.99, `fawthrop.graham@gmail.com`, purchased 2026-09-20, `mystery_style='detective'`, `script_type='pointForm'`, `player_count=6`, `has_accomplice=false`, English, well post-ADR-0074). All standard detectors (`list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`, `list_packages_with_victim_mismatch`, `list_packages_with_unresolved_victim_name`, `package_completion_blocking_defects`) came back clean, and a full manual read (all 6 characters, `detective_script`, `game_overview`, dual-name convention, register, narration person) found nothing wrong mechanically — the package was internally coherent. The bug was invisible to every existing check because nothing was broken; the generated story just wasn't the story the customer asked for.

**What happened.** The customer's first message was an ~1,000-word fully-worked-out custom plot for a live in-person event (48 guests, 8 tables, hired actors), including a specific requested murder motive: Ulrike and Felisa had a past romantic relationship, Nigel discovered and blackmailed both of them over it, and Ulrike kills him "motivated by love" for Felisa, ending in a specific quoted confession line ("Felisa didn't know. It wasn't planned. But yes… I did it."). The concept chat's very first reply explicitly promised to keep "all your motive work intact — the blackmail, the VIAGHA fraud, Damien's history, and Ulrike's devastating final line," and its next reply did, verbatim, including the quoted line. Then, at message 12, the customer sent an incremental clarifying note — "Ulrike loves her husband Mike" — that could be read as additive color (both women love their own husbands, on top of everything already established) or as a walk-back of the earlier romance. The very next assistant reply silently picked the second reading, invented an entirely unrelated substitute secret for each woman (Ulrike: covering up "Mike's own old indiscretion"; Felisa: a lie to Ian about borrowed money), dropped the confession line, and never told the customer any of this had changed — despite having named the dropped detail as something being kept "intact" only two turns earlier. `master_context`'s `murdererSelection.reasoning` shows the invented substitute was already locked in before the payload ever reached Make.com, confirming the loss happened in the concept chat itself, not in per-character generation downstream.

**Separately, and independently:** the customer explicitly named their live host-detective actor "Graham Miguel Poirot" (their own first name, confirmed against the purchasing email `fawthrop.graham@gmail.com`) and an assistant-detective role, "Paola," which the concept chat correctly and immediately told the customer isn't a supported second character type (existing product constraint, correctly surfaced — no bug there). "Graham Miguel Poirot" was preserved correctly everywhere through all 18 chat messages and into `master_context` (`additionalSpecificDetails`, 1 occurrence of "Poirot"). But the generated `detective_script` — the actual script the host will read aloud at their event — called the detective "Detective Inspector Thornton" 4 times throughout, a name that appears nowhere else in the entire package. This is a Make.com-side generation bug (the detective_script generation step evidently doesn't consume the confirmed detective name from the snapshot/`master_context` at all, and invents its own), not a concept-chat bug — the name was correctly captured everywhere available to inspect.

**Fixed directly on this package** (Jonathan confirmed before writing): corrected `master_context` (both JSON objects — `murdererSelection.reasoning`, `victimProfile.wrongedEachCharacter` for both characters, `conversationDetailsExtraction`'s `evidencePossessed`/`characterSpecificDetails`/`financialAndTransactionDetails`/`relationshipHistoryDetails` entries, and `motiveDistribution` in the second object) to restore the Ulrike/Felisa romantic-history motive; Ulrike's and Felisa's `description`/`background`/`secret`/`secrets` to match; Ulrike's `final_statement` and `final_statement_pointform` rewritten to include the requested confession line; and `detective_script`'s 4 "Thornton" references plus the REVEAL paragraph's motive line, renamed to "Detective Graham Miguel Poirot" and re-framed around the restored motive. All edits were hand-written (no Anthropic call — avoids any paid-API question entirely) and re-verified: no "Thornton" remains, `package_completion_blocking_defects()` still clean, `master_context` still valid JSON (2 objects, re-parsed successfully), both characters' secrets confirmed to reference the romance, Ulrike's final statement confirmed to contain the confession line.

**Prevention, shipped same session:** added a new unconditional `CRITICAL` guardrail to `supabase/functions/mystery-ai/index.ts` (same established pattern as the existing player-count/round-count/investigator-role guardrails in that file — appended after all branch-specific prompts so it also reaches the production `MYSTERY_FREE_PROMPT` database-driven path, which isn't otherwise editable from this repo) instructing the model to never silently drop, replace, or reinterpret an already-established plot detail — especially one it already called out as important or promised to keep "intact" — without asking the customer directly when a later message seems to add to or conflict with it. Deployed via CLI (`supabase functions deploy mystery-ai --project-ref mhfikaomkmqcndqfohbp`), confirmed a clean 16-line diff matches what shipped.

**Blueprint fix prepared, pending import.** No Make.com MCP access this session (`AUTH_HEADER_REJECTED`, HTTP 401), so the fix couldn't be applied live — but the root cause was traceable directly from the exported blueprint already in this repo: `temp-files/MM Live - Parent67 (Child Webhook URL Rotation).blueprint.json`'s detective/investigator-script generation call (module id `5000`, mirrored at `5004`/`5008`/`5012` for the character-style and intrigue routes) contains an explicit `NAMING RULE` instructing the model to invent a gender-neutral rank+surname name for the detective — "Inspector Thornton" is literally one of its three example names, which is exactly what leaked into this package. The rule never checked whether a name was already established; the correct name ("Graham Miguel Poirot") was sitting in the same `master_context` blob the call already receives (via `additionalSpecificDetails`), just never looked for. Patched the `NAMING RULE` in all 4 routes (detective/character × murder/intrigue — confirmed the rule text was byte-identical across all 4 before patching) to check `master_context` for an already-established name first and use it verbatim, falling back to the existing invent-a-name logic only when none exists. Saved as a new copy, `temp-files/MM Live - Parent68 (Detective Name Fidelity Fix).blueprint.json` — an 8-line diff against Parent67 (4 matched find/replace pairs, nothing else touched), JSON-validated. **Imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-09-20.** Not yet end-to-end tested against a real generation (a custom-plot conversation that names its own detective, plus a plain one that doesn't, to confirm the fallback still invents a name correctly) — that's the one remaining step before this is fully closed out.

**Judgment call worth flagging:** the "Ulrike loves her husband Mike" message that triggered the drop was genuinely ambiguous — a defensible read exists where the customer meant to simplify away the same-sex-affair subplot, not just add color to it. Restoring the original romance was Jonathan's explicit call given the weight of evidence (the customer's original brief was extremely detailed and specific about this exact motive, named it their "preferred choice," and wrote the confession line by hand) and the fact the concept chat had explicitly told the customer nothing here would be dropped. A different, less detailed or less explicitly-flagged customer plot might warrant a different call.

### Key files (Addendum 51)
- `mystery_packages.master_context`, `.detective_script` (package `7aa38e75-ed3d-47b2-b45f-313eea08d0cd`) — hand-corrected
- `mystery_characters.description`/`.background`/`.secret`/`.secrets`/`.final_statement`/`.final_statement_pointform` for Ulrike/Ulrich Sterne and Felisa/Felix Fleeting (same package) — hand-corrected
- `supabase/functions/mystery-ai/index.ts` — new CRITICAL guardrail against silently dropping established plot details; deployed
- `temp-files/MM Live - Parent68 (Detective Name Fidelity Fix).blueprint.json` — new blueprint copy with the `NAMING RULE` fix in all 4 routes; imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-09-20. Not yet end-to-end tested against a real generation with a customer-named detective — see follow-up vault note.

## Addendum 52 (2026-09-20): "The Raven And The Rose" — Make.com's own plot-generation step overrode a customer's unambiguous, explicit answer key and named the wrong murderer; new, more severe bug class; two more blueprint prompts fixed

New-Purchase sweep on package `7b7bd8bf-3482-41a4-9c78-18720548b121` (conversation `6aadc5de-c9eb-4353-9c8e-9d474d181f3c`, $19.99, `matt@wgsocial.com`, purchased 2026-09-20, `mystery_style='detective'`, `script_type='both'`, `player_count=4`, `has_accomplice=true`, English, well post-ADR-0074, imported same day as Addendum 51's fix). All standard detectors came back clean — the package was internally coherent. This was found entirely by manually cross-checking generated content against a customer-supplied plot, exactly as Addendum 51 did, and it found something worse.

**What happened.** The customer submitted an extraordinarily detailed 18,612-character custom brief for a real, already-scheduled live Halloween event (WGS Halloween party) — full scripted dialogue, an 8-drop timed evidence schedule, an already-built external QR-code scoring app, and an explicit, unambiguous final answer key: **"Who murdered Danny Mercer? ... Correct: Susan"** (30 points), with Matt, Tom, and Lisa all explicitly described as red herrings or an accomplice. The concept chat handled this correctly end-to-end — the approved concept snapshot sent to Make.com faithfully described Susan as the one who "orchestrated the cover-up then — and will do anything to keep it buried now" and Matt as merely someone who "fought bitterly with Danny... and concealed how much he really knew." Nothing was dropped or reinterpreted at the concept-chat stage; Addendum 51's guardrail wasn't even needed here.

**Make.com's plot-generation step (the "Part 1" master-constraints call, module id `171` in the Parent blueprint) then independently overrode this and selected Matt as the murderer anyway**, inventing an entirely fabricated motive with no basis in the customer's brief ("Danny had also uncovered something Matt did in 1996... something Matt has never admitted"). `mystery_characters.character_role` reflected the same inversion (Matt tagged `murderer`, Susan tagged `redHerring`). Every downstream field built on this — `master_context`'s `victimProfile`, `motiveDistribution`, `accompliceDetails`, `evidenceProgression`, `hostBriefing`, `deceptionGuidelines`; both characters' `description`/`background`/`secret`/`final_statement`/`accusations`/`round4_script`; `detective_script`'s reveal; both Round 3 and Round 4 `evidence_cards` significance notes — was internally self-consistent with the wrong answer, which is exactly why no detector caught it. Root-caused directly in the exported blueprint: module 171's `murderer_selection_criteria` explicitly instructs the model to treat "whoever seems most suspicious on first read" as something to actively avoid selecting ("Not the obvious choice: Avoid selecting whoever seems most suspicious... Misdirection potential: Can 2-3 OTHER characters plausibly seem MORE guilty initially?"), with no exception for a case where the "obvious" suspect is obvious *because the customer already decided the answer*, not because the AI needs to manufacture a twist. This is a more severe version of Addendum 51's bug: even a fully-preserved customer intent got treated as raw material for a fictional twist rather than as a fixed, must-preserve solution — and unlike Addendum 51, it isn't a "genuinely ambiguous customer message" judgment call. The customer's answer key was completely unambiguous.

**Practical severity:** the customer's live event was already scheduled to run their own externally-built scored quiz app with "Susan" hard-coded as correct, while the delivered host script would have accused Matt in front of the room — a direct, public contradiction between the product they paid for and the tool they were already planning to use it alongside. Package was already delivered (emailed) before this was caught.

**Fixed directly on this package:** swapped `character_role` (Matt → `redHerring`, Susan → `murderer`); corrected `master_context`'s `murdererSelection`, `victimProfile.wrongedEachCharacter`, `conversationDetailsExtraction.characterSpecificDetails`, `motiveDistribution`, `accompliceDetails` (fully reoriented from Matt to Susan, matching the brief's actual mechanism — Lisa's false-alibi phone call from Susan, not raven-cleanup help for Matt), `evidenceProgression`, `hostBriefing`, and `deceptionGuidelines`; rewrote Matt's and Susan's `description`/`background`/`secret`/`final_statement`(+pointform)/`accusations`(+pointform)/`round4_script`(+pointform) to match the brief (Matt: cleared by his brief-established 6:27 PM Pilars departure; Susan: false 6:25 PM departure contradicted by the brief's 6:39 PM photo/brooch evidence); rewrote Lisa's `final_statement`/`round4_script`/`accusations`(+pointform) to reorient her accomplice cover story from protecting Matt to protecting Susan, matching the brief's actual mechanism; rewrote `detective_script`'s reveal (borrowing heavily from the customer's own reveal-script wording) and both flawed `evidence_cards` significance notes. All hand-written, no Anthropic call. Found and fixed 3 leftover "helping Matt" references on a post-fix verification pass (an `accompliceSelection` field in `master_context` object 0 that the first pass missed, plus two entries inside `conversationDetailsExtraction`/`motiveDistribution` that referenced Lisa's cover story) — a reminder that a single `grep` pass across the *entire* package for the old character's name in a guilt-specific context is worth doing after a swap this size, not just fixing the fields you planned to touch. Re-verified: `package_completion_blocking_defects()` clean, `master_context` still valid JSON (2 objects), exactly one `"isMurderer": "true"` entry (Susan's), all three characters' confession/denial content internally consistent.

**Prevention, shipped same session:** root-caused directly from the exported blueprint (`temp-files/MM Live - Parent68 (Detective Name Fidelity Fix).blueprint.json`, the version Addendum 51 had already fixed for the detective-name bug) rather than guessing — found the exact `murderer_selection_criteria` block (module 171, the detective/murder route) and a nearly identical but weaker `culprit_selection_criteria` block (module 2417, the detective/intrigue route, which already had a soft, easily-outweighed "if the conversation hints at a culprit, honor that" clause buried as priority 5 of 5 - a prior, incomplete attempt at this exact problem). Added a new `<explicit_solution_override>` block ahead of both criteria sections: if the conversation already establishes an unambiguous, customer-specified solution, select exactly that character and skip the misdirection/not-obvious-choice criteria entirely; only apply those criteria when genuinely inventing the solution. Saved as `temp-files/MM Live - Parent69 (Explicit Solution Override).blueprint.json`, built on top of Parent68 - a 4-line diff (2 matched find/replace pairs), JSON-validated. **Imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-09-20.** Not yet end-to-end tested against a real generation with an explicit customer answer key, or against a plain AI-generated one (to confirm the existing misdirection logic still produces a good "surprising yet inevitable" reveal when no answer was pre-specified) — both await the next relevant sweep.

**Standing pattern worth naming:** this is the second time in one day the same underlying gap has surfaced in a different part of the pipeline (mystery-ai's concept chat, Make.com's detective-naming, now Make.com's murderer selection) - nothing in this generation pipeline has a general, first-class notion of "the customer already decided this; preserve it, don't creatively subvert it." Each fix so far has been local (guardrail text at the specific point of failure). Worth flagging to Jonathan as a candidate for a more structural fix (e.g., an explicit `solutionOverride` field extracted once and threaded through every downstream prompt) if a fourth instance of this pattern turns up somewhere else in the pipeline - consistent with this ADR's own established bar of not building structural fixes on the strength of one or two occurrences.

### Key files (Addendum 52)
- `mystery_packages.master_context`, `.detective_script`, `.evidence_cards` (package `7b7bd8bf-3482-41a4-9c78-18720548b121`) — hand-corrected
- `mystery_characters.character_role` + `.description`/`.background`/`.secret`/`.secrets`/`.final_statement`(+pointform)/`.accusations`(+pointform)/`.round4_script`(+pointform) for Matt/Mattie and Susan/Stuart; `.final_statement`/`.round4_script`/`.accusations`(+pointform) for Lisa/Leo (same package) — hand-corrected
- `temp-files/MM Live - Parent69 (Explicit Solution Override).blueprint.json` — new blueprint copy fixing module 171 (`murderer_selection_criteria`) and module 2417 (`culprit_selection_criteria`); imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-09-20

## Addendum 53 (2026-09-21): "Champagne & Crocodile Tears" — a customer's own self-insert character was silently dropped from her 18-player package; roster-extraction bug, not a Make.com prompt bug this time; full writeup lives in ADR-0125

New-Purchase sweep on package `80b2519f-47b4-43b1-93c6-abc4891e1ba4` (conversation `424537af-8a03-48e4-a6d0-a27cfeb476d2`, $24.99, `anya.kavanagh@gmail.com`, `mystery_style='character'`, 18 players requested, has accomplice). All standard detectors clean — the delivered 17 characters were internally consistent with each other, which is exactly why nothing flagged that an 18th was missing.

Manual check caught it: `game_overview` said "Eighteen guests" while only 17 `mystery_characters` rows existed. Traced to the customer's own first message ("for 18 players") and the approved concept snapshot, which listed her as `1. **[YOU] Baby/Bianca Delacroix**` — her own self-insert playable character, silently dropped before ever reaching the database. Root cause was a real bug in the shared roster-extraction code (`isPlaceholderCharacterName`, in `_shared/rosterExtraction.ts`), not a Make.com prompt issue like Addenda 51-52 — a filter built to catch draft placeholder slots (`[RESERVE CHARACTER - ...]`) also matched any real name with a leading bracketed tag, since it only checked `startsWith('[')` rather than requiring the bracket to span the whole name. Masked from detection because `conversations.player_count` was independently already wrong (17, never reconciled against the customer's stated 18), so the pipeline's own drift-detection saw the broken 17-extraction match the wrong 17-stored value and concluded there was no drift to fix.

Fixed the filter (now requires `startsWith('[') && endsWith(']')`), added a regression test, deployed all three consumers (`mystery-webhook-trigger`, `extract-concept-roster`, `mystery-ai`). Hand-built the missing character (all 44 fields a `character`-style suspect needs) directly on the live package, sourced from `master_context` (which still had her full planning data — 28 references — since it reads the raw conversation independently of the broken extraction) and cross-checked against all 17 existing characters' own mentions of her for consistency. `conversations.player_count` corrected 17 → 18. Full technical writeup, root cause, and fix detail: ADR-0125 Update 2026-09-21 (that ADR owns `_shared/rosterExtraction.ts`).

### Key files (Addendum 53)
See ADR-0125 Update 2026-09-21's "Key files" list — not duplicated here.

## Addendum 54 (2026-09-23): Two same-day purchases, both clean — but a leaked prompt-template word-count directive found in `background` fields, recurring across 4 packages; fixed at the source and a new detector shipped same session

New-Purchase sweep on two packages purchased within hours of each other: "The Isla Serene Incident" (package `bd2673a3-032a-4313-b5f0-6593fa1e6311`, conversation `aa119be0-8377-4d13-bbab-e30d5715dc4a`, $19.99, `laurenryan1339@gmail.com`, purchased 2026-09-22, `mystery_style='detective'`, `script_type='both'`, `player_count=7`, `has_accomplice=false`) and "Last Call: The Underground" (package `ce4054f8-29be-4aa1-8d81-f1b45c48e959`, conversation `7679d023-6617-47e8-b3bf-b576b17a1578`, $19.99, `cameronmlott@gmail.com`, purchased 2026-09-23, `mystery_style='detective'`, `script_type='both'`, `player_count=17`, `has_accomplice=true`). Both English, both well post-ADR-0074. All four standard detectors (`list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`, `list_packages_with_victim_mismatch`, `list_packages_with_unresolved_victim_name`, `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch`) and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` came back clean for both. Character counts matched `player_count` (7 and 17), `character_role` assignments matched `master_context.murdererSelection`/`has_accomplice` and the `detective_script` reveal in both, victim names were consistent across `master_context.victimProfile.name`, `game_overview`, and `detective_script` (Veronica Vane; Dr. Kerri Mitchell), and manual reads of both full `detective_script`s plus 3 characters per package (murderer, accomplice/suspect, and one more) found no brackets leaks, garbled text, or secret/background inversions.

**What manual review did find:** Rachelle Coleman's (Isla Serene) `background` field ended with a trailing sentence that isn't character content at all: *"Total background: concrete facts only."* Read as a leaked authoring instruction rather than in-character prose. A corpus-wide check (`ilike background '%Total background:%'`) found 5 characters total across 4 packages — both of today's purchases (Rachelle Coleman here; Catherine and Arista Bomalick in Last Call) plus 2 pre-existing historical packages already delivered to paying customers: "Rank Zero" (package `e71da9da-78b2-4a21-8478-30f39ab6c755`, generated 2026-09-20, Devon/Devonna 'Sponsor' Whitfield) and "Sweet Tea, Secrets, And A Slug Of Bourbon" (package `4dca8af6-2e1c-4f12-b0f2-22d819b18ccd`, generated 2026-09-21, S. Barnaby Whitfield). The variant on Arista Bomalick's and S. Barnaby Whitfield's records — *"Total background: 148 words."* / *"Total background: 145 words"* — confirmed this wasn't paraphrase drift but a literal leaked directive: the model was both following AND echoing a length instruction.

**Root cause:** `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)42-ConciseCharacterContent.blueprint.json`'s `background` field template ended with `...pulled from master_context]\n\nTotal background: no more than ~150 words. All concrete facts — no scene-setting, no atmosphere.` — phrased exactly like the `**Label:** value` pattern the model had just been filling in for `**Name:**`, `**Role:**`, etc. two lines above, so the model pattern-matched it as one more field to complete and either paraphrased it ("concrete facts only") or literally reported compliance ("148 words"). None of `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`'s existing marker patterns matched this phrasing, so all 5 instances shipped to customers undetected.

**Fixed same session, per this ADR's established rule for genuinely fixable prompt/instruction gaps (no need to wait for a 2nd occurrence — this had already recurred 5 times):**
1. All 5 live `mystery_characters.background` rows hand-stripped of the trailing leaked sentence via direct UPDATE (narrow text-field edit, re-verified corpus-wide count back to 0 after).
2. Prompt fixed at the source: the leaked snippet rewritten as an explicit bracketed instruction consistent with the template's own placeholder convention (`[fact: ...]` etc.), now reading `[Keep the full background field to roughly 150 words total, concrete facts only — no scene-setting or atmosphere. This is a length instruction for you to follow silently; never include this instruction, a word count, or the words "Total background" anywhere in your actual output.]`, applied at both of the 2 sites in the blueprint where the `background` field template appears. Saved as `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)43-BackgroundLengthLeakFix.blueprint.json`, JSON-validated. **Imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-09-23.**
3. Detector extended: `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`'s marker regex gained `\mtotal \w+:\s*(no more than|~?\d+\s*words?|concrete facts)`, migration `supabase/migrations/20260923000000_extend_meta_text_leak_for_background_word_count.sql`. Initially deferred — `supabase migration list` (CLI) showed every local migration as unapplied against remote (empty `remote` column across the entire history), and a blind `supabase db push` across that much apparent drift was judged too risky to run unattended. Resolved by reconnecting the Supabase MCP (had failed to connect earlier in this session) and diffing the live function definition directly via `pg_get_functiondef()` before touching anything — it matched the pre-fix version exactly, confirming the CLI's "all unapplied" reading was stale/wrong noise, not real drift, and this one `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` was safe to ship in isolation without resolving the CLI's broader migration-history desync. **Applied to remote 2026-09-23**, verified live: the new regex matches both leaked variants ("148 words.", "concrete facts only.") and does not false-positive on plain "background" prose; `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()` returns zero rows post-fix.

**Not fixed:** the 2 historical packages' `mystery_characters.background` rows for Devon/Devonna 'Sponsor' Whitfield and S. Barnaby Whitfield were included in the same corpus-wide UPDATE (all 5 rows fixed together, not just today's 3) — no separate action needed there.

**Follow-up, same day:** ran down whether the CLI's "entire local history unapplied" reading meant any *other* local migrations were genuinely missing from remote. Checked 15 local-only-looking migration names by verifying the actual live object (`pg_get_functiondef()` for functions, `information_schema.columns` for columns, `cron.job` for a job body, row existence for data acknowledgments) rather than trusting the migration-log bookkeeping — all 15 were genuinely live. Root cause confirmed: `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` records each migration under its actual apply-time timestamp, not the local filename's timestamp, and this project's migrations have long been applied via Supabase Studio / the Supabase MCP tool rather than `supabase db push` — so the CLI's version-string diff will likely always show drift here, but it's cosmetic, not a real backlog. No missing schema found. Documented as a standing practice in `CLAUDE.md` under "Database Migrations — `supabase migration list` / `db push` Is Not Reliable Here" so future sessions don't re-investigate from scratch or risk a blind `db push`.

### Key files (Addendum 54)
- `mystery_characters.background` — 5 rows hand-corrected (`bd2673a3-032a-4313-b5f0-6593fa1e6311`, `ce4054f8-29be-4aa1-8d81-f1b45c48e959` ×2, `e71da9da-78b2-4a21-8478-30f39ab6c755`, `4dca8af6-2e1c-4f12-b0f2-22d819b18ccd`)
- `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)43-BackgroundLengthLeakFix.blueprint.json` — new blueprint copy fixing the field-template leak; imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-09-23
- `supabase/migrations/20260923000000_extend_meta_text_leak_for_background_word_count.sql` — extends `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()`; applied to remote via Supabase MCP 2026-09-23, verified live

## Addendum 55 (2026-09-25): "The Gala Of Daggers" — package clean; a stray unmatched quote mark turned out to be a corpus-wide generation artifact (51 rows, ~30 delivered packages), root-caused, fixed at the source, and a new detector shipped; separately, victim characterization flagged and resolved as customer-intended creative latitude, not a coherence bug

New-Purchase sweep on package `7d8a3015-6e48-468d-a860-3144e7a8200b` (conversation `6012decc-00e9-4dd8-b627-0d3a29189ffc`, $19.99, `lallenl2000@gmail.com`, purchased 2026-09-24, `mystery_style='detective'`, `script_type='full'`, `player_count=5`, `has_accomplice=false`, English, well post-ADR-0074). All three standard detectors (`list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`, `list_packages_with_victim_mismatch`, `list_packages_with_unresolved_victim_name`), `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch`, and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` came back clean. Victim name ("Ronald Dump") consistent across `game_overview`, `detective_script`, `master_context`, and `evidence_cards`. Full read of `detective_script` plus all 5 characters' `background`/`secret`/`relationships`/round scripts/`final_statement`/`rumors`/`accusations` found no bracket leaks, no chain-of-thought, no secret/background inversions, and no narration-person issues — motive/method/opportunity chain in `master_context` cross-checks cleanly against every character's own script content.

**Found:** Mike Millingdon's (the murderer) `final_statement` — the confession script read aloud during the reveal — ended with a stray, unmatched closing single-quote (`...I'm sorry for what it cost me to finally do it.'`) with no corresponding opening quote anywhere in the ~250-word passage. Hand-corrected first as an apparent one-off.

**Jonathan asked whether this was a one-off or worth building something for.** Rather than guess, ran a corpus-wide check using a strict heuristic (field ends in terminal punctuation immediately followed by a quote character, AND has no legitimate opening quote — this corpus's own convention, a quote preceded by start-of-string/whitespace/`(`/`:` — anywhere earlier in the field, which excludes the many *correctly* quote-wrapped dialogue lines used throughout this corpus, e.g. `rumors` gossip lines and the murderer's `accusations` deflection lines). Result: **51 live rows across ~30 already-delivered, paid packages**, concentrated almost entirely in 3 fields: `introduction` (25 hits), `final_statement` (19 hits), `reveal_confession_guilty`/`reveal_confession_accomplice`/`final_innocent` (7 combined). Not a one-off.

**Root cause:** all 3 heavily-affected fields are the LAST field in their respective JSON output schema in the Make.com Child (Unified) blueprint's per-character generation calls — `introduction` closes "Call 1: Context," `final_statement` closes "Call 3: Round Scripts," `reveal_confession_guilty`/`reveal_confession_accomplice` close "Call 4: Guilty Scripts"/"Call 5: Accomplice Scripts." `round2/3/4_script` (not last-field-of-a-call) showed zero true hits under the same check, confirming the pattern tracks field position, not content type. Likely mechanism: "Call 3: Round Scripts" carries an explicit pacing instruction — *"write each round, verify the comma and closing quote are present, then move to the next round"* — meant as JSON-formatting guidance (confirm the string is properly terminated before moving on), but phrased ambiguously enough that the model appears to sometimes interpret "closing quote" as a literal character to add to the narrative content itself, right as it wraps up the last field before closing the JSON object.

**Fixed same session, per this ADR's standing rule for genuinely fixable, recurring prompt/instruction gaps:**
1. All 51 live rows across the 5 affected fields hand-corrected via a single verified, narrow UPDATE per field (trimmed the single trailing stray character where the strict pattern matched); corpus re-checked to 0 remaining hits.
2. New detector shipped and applied to remote same session: `list_packages_with_dangling_quote_mark()` (`supabase/migrations/20260925000000_add_dangling_quote_mark_detector.sql`), using the same strict heuristic validated above. Returns 0 rows post-fix.
3. Prompt fixed at the source in a new blueprint version, `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)44-DanglingQuoteFix.blueprint.json`: (a) added a new global rule, "NO STRAY QUOTATION MARKS," to the shared `content_coherence_rules` block that appears identically at all 8 LLM call sites across both mystery-style branches (detective and character), explicitly stating a field's content must never be quote-wrapped or end in an unmatched quote, and that any "closing quote" instruction elsewhere refers only to the JSON string delimiter, never a literal character in the prose; (b) reinforced the specific ambiguous pacing instruction in "Call 3: Round Scripts" to spell out "closing double-quote (the JSON string delimiter... never a literal character added to the prose itself)." JSON-validated. **Imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-09-25.**

**Flagged, then resolved — no guardrail warranted (separate issue, same sweep):** the victim, "Ronald Dump," reads as a fairly transparent parody of a real, still-living political figure. Raised this as a possible content/brand-risk judgment call, since it's more pointed than the generic corrupt-billionaire-villain archetype most packages use. Checked `mystery_packages.user_conversation` to see whether this was customer intent or model improvisation: the customer explicitly co-built this exact concept in the concept chat — name, "billionaire financier turned politician," the healthcare-policy and public-cruelty characterization — and approved the full preview before hitting "Generate Mystery." Jonathan's call (2026-09-25): no guardrail needed here — he's supportive of this kind of creative latitude as long as the generated content matches what the customer actually asked for, which the concept-chat transcript confirms it does. Standing principle for future sweeps: don't flag a "pointed" or edgy villain/theme as a brand-risk concern by itself — check `user_conversation` for customer intent first, and only surface it if the generated content diverges from what the customer approved.

### Key files (Addendum 55)
- `mystery_characters` — 51 rows hand-corrected across `introduction` (25), `final_statement` (19), `reveal_confession_guilty` (3), `reveal_confession_accomplice` (3), `final_innocent` (1), spanning ~30 packages
- `supabase/migrations/20260925000000_add_dangling_quote_mark_detector.sql` — new `list_packages_with_dangling_quote_mark()` detector; applied to remote via Supabase MCP 2026-09-25, verified live (0 rows post-fix)
- `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)44-DanglingQuoteFix.blueprint.json` — new blueprint copy fixing the ambiguous "closing quote" instruction at its source; **imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-09-25**

## Addendum 56 (2026-09-26): Two same-day purchases — "El Zasca Final" and "The Last Lesson Of Professor Vaingloryus" — each had one real, distinct defect; also resolves a GitHub health-check `needs_review` flag and gives the first live data point on v44's dangling-quote fix

New-Purchase sweep on two packages: "El Zasca Final" (package `9513886e-ce1a-4bea-8492-117fc58eafb3`, conversation `2bba9639-d88d-4352-a4ac-e88b1aa6c1bb`, $19.99, `ffernandezricardo@gmail.com`, purchased 2026-09-26, `mystery_style='detective'`, `script_type='both'`, `player_count=11`, `has_accomplice=true`, Spanish) and "The Last Lesson Of Professor Vaingloryus" (package `dd0b0ec3-0123-4f2b-a365-754f199cb61f`, conversation `02ab4fef-4b76-49e9-ac80-f49d053f9aad`, $19.99, `drewnanncy@gmail.com`, purchased 2026-09-26, `mystery_style='detective'`, `script_type='full'`, `player_count=14`, `has_accomplice=false`, English). Both post-v44. All four standard detectors and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` run for both; character counts matched `player_count` (11 and 14) for both.

**GitHub health-check flag resolved (El Zasca Final):** a scheduled health-check comment flagged this exact conversation as `needs_review` in the last 7 days. Traced via `auto_remediation_log`: one row, `defect_class='empty_character_content'`, `action='regenerate_character:Justin Timberlake'`, `outcome='escalated'` ($0.15, 2026-09-26 16:30) — the auto-remediation attempt gave up and flagged for human review. Verified directly: package `generation_status` is `completed`, `package_completion_blocking_defects()` is clean, and Justin Timberlake's content is fully present and coherent across every field (lengths comparable to all 10 other characters; introduction and secret read cleanly in Spanish and tie correctly to his approved concept — a secret cabaret alter-ego, "Carmen de Mairena Jr.", matching the customer's own concept-chat description). The package evidently completed via a later retry independent of the escalated remediation attempt. Acknowledged in `acknowledged_health_alerts` (detector `needs_review`) — content is correct as delivered, no patch needed for this flag.

**El Zasca Final is a wild customer-designed celebrity-mashup cast** (real living celebrities Belén Esteban — the victim — Britney Spears, Justin Timberlake; real deceased historical/religious figures Fidel Castro, Margaret Thatcher, Pope John Paul II, Mother Teresa; fictional characters from Titanic, The Matrix, and Buffy). Checked `user_conversation`: the customer explicitly designed and approved every character and plot beat in the concept chat. Per the standing principle from Addendum 55, not re-flagged as a brand-risk concern — this is customer-approved creative latitude, not model improvisation, even though it uses real people's actual names (a step further than Addendum 55's fictionalized-name parody).

**Found and fixed — El Zasca Final, tú/usted register mismatch (2nd occurrence of the Addendum 13 bug class):** the detective's own reveal monologue is consistently formal ("ustedes," "señoras y señores," "usted" addressing the accomplice Juan Pablo II) throughout, except one line addressing the murderer: *"Trinity. La asesina de Belén Esteban **eres tú**."* — informal, breaking the register used everywhere else in the same speech, including two sentences later for the accomplice. Fixed to `es usted` to match. This is a different specific locus than Addendum 13 (which was about a character's own branch headers vs. their own introduction) — here it's the detective's cross-suspect address consistency within one monologue — but it's the same underlying bug class (no detector catches T-V register drift; requires reading the actual dialogue). Per the checklist's own instruction not to decide solo: **this is now a 2nd confirmed occurrence of T-V register drift — surfacing to Jonathan whether it's worth a structural fix (deciding register once per-package and threading it through every call) versus continuing to catch it by hand each sweep.**

**Found and fixed — Professor Vaingloryus, cross-field evidence-ownership mismatch (new instance of the recurring cross-field-mismatch bug class, cf. Addenda 33/47/48):** the Round 2 evidence item ("The Grievance Ledger") was described two contradictory ways. `detective_script`: *"recovered... from Professor Vaingloryus's private chambers... his own handwriting."* `evidence_cards`: *"kept privately by Professor Quillbrook."* Checked `master_context`: `evidenceProgression.round2.item` is deliberately ownership-neutral, but the separate `namedItems` array explicitly canonizes `"name": "Quillbrook's ledger", "owner": "Professor Cornelius/Cornelia Quillbrook"` — a decades-long record Quillbrook kept of Vaingloryus's wrongdoing, not a confession in Vaingloryus's own hand. `evidence_cards` correctly reflected the canonical owner; `detective_script` invented a conflicting origin independently. Fixed `detective_script`'s Round 2 passage to match the canonical Quillbrook-owned framing. **This is at least the 4th documented instance of the cross-field-mismatch class** (title mismatch in Addendum 48, blackmail-amount mismatch in Addendum 47, secret/background inversion in Addendum 33, now evidence-ownership here) — surfacing to Jonathan whether the accumulating count now justifies an LLM-judge-based semantic-consistency check (needs his sign-off per the no-paid-API-without-permission rule) versus continuing to catch this by hand, per the checklist's existing "don't decide solo" instruction for this bug class.

**Dangling-quote-mark detector: first live data point post-v44.** `list_packages_with_dangling_quote_mark()` returned one hit on Vaingloryus — `introduction:Professor Ivy/Ivo Ravensmoor` — despite this package generating fully after v44's 2026-09-25 import. Hand-corrected and re-verified. One occurrence out of many generated fields (14 characters × multiple fields each) is a large reduction from the pre-fix baseline (51 rows/~30 packages), consistent with an explicit "don't do X" instruction reducing but not fully eliminating a probabilistic LLM habit — not evidence the fix failed to land. No action taken beyond the standard fix-and-re-verify; the detector is doing its job. Worth continuing to watch via the standard checklist, not worth a fresh investigation on one instance.

### Key files (Addendum 56)
- `mystery_packages.detective_script` — 2 rows hand-corrected: El Zasca Final (`9513886e-ce1a-4bea-8492-117fc58eafb3`, tú/usted fix) and Vaingloryus (`dd0b0ec3-0123-4f2b-a365-754f199cb61f`, ledger-ownership fix)
- `mystery_characters.introduction` — 1 row hand-corrected (`dd0b0ec3-0123-4f2b-a365-754f199cb61f`, Professor Ivy/Ivo Ravensmoor, dangling-quote-mark)
- `acknowledged_health_alerts` — 1 row added for `9513886e-ce1a-4bea-8492-117fc58eafb3` resolving the GitHub health-check `needs_review` flag

**Follow-up, same day — Jonathan's call on the two "surface, don't decide solo" items above:** (1) structural fix for T-V register drift, yes; (2) LLM-judge semantic-consistency check — initially yes in principle, then reconsidered once scoped. Structural register fix implemented same session — see below.

**LLM-judge check: proposed, scoped, and declined in favor of a cheaper fix that closes the same gap.** Before building anything that spends money automatically (per the global no-paid-API-without-permission rule), asked where it should run: on-demand during sweeps, automatic on every generation, or a scheduled batch — each with a rough cost estimate (~$0.10-0.50/package for a comprehensive Sonnet pass; automatic/scheduled at current volume would run $3-75/month ongoing). Jonathan pushed back on the premise: since manual sweeps already involve reading content and cross-referencing for contradictions, what would a dedicated tool add that a sweep doesn't already catch? Correct challenge — the honest answer is the tool wouldn't add a new *capability*, it would add *coverage*: this checklist's own manual step only spot-checks 2-3 characters out of a typical 11-14+ cast, so 8-12 characters get zero cross-field scrutiny per sweep, and every cross-field-mismatch bug found so far (Addenda 33, 47, 48, 56) was caught in content that happened to already be read closely, which is a selection-biased sample — no way to know what's being missed in the unsampled characters. Given the real gap was sampling, not a missing capability, Jonathan's call: **widen the manual read to the full cast instead of building a paid tool.** Checklist step 6 updated accordingly (`CLAUDE.md`) — closes this open item at zero new ongoing cost.

**Structural register-consistency fix, implemented in two new blueprint versions (not yet imported):**
1. `temp-files/MM Live - Parent70 (Register Consistency Fix).blueprint.json` — adds a new `<register_consistency>` instruction block to both detective_script generation call sites (initial + retry), directly targeting the bug found in this addendum: the detective's own voice must commit to ONE register (formal by default) for its entire script, applied uniformly to every suspect including the murderer and accomplice at the reveal — never switching between suspects or sections. Includes an explicit self-check instruction ("re-read your own reveal before finishing").
2. `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)45-RegisterConsistencyFix.blueprint.json` — adds a new "REGISTER CONSISTENCY" rule to the shared `content_coherence_rules` block (same 8 call sites patched for Addendum 55's quote fix), targeting the original Addendum 13 bug shape: a character's own introduction establishes their register, and every other field generated for that same character (round scripts, final statement, confession/reveal branches) must match it.

Both are JSON-validated, ready for Jonathan to import. **Not yet imported into Make.com.**

## Addendum 57 (2026-09-27): "L'eredità Del Silenzio" — first Italian-language sweep under this ADR; package clean apart from one dangling-quote hit; predates the register-fix import, so not yet a real test of it

New-Purchase sweep on package `2b8d5cd9-cd70-4de1-bee7-7dcaecc7c10f` (conversation `18f27ad6-c5d1-4ee6-9ff2-9ba25714f729`, $19.99, `flavia_valente@hotmail.it`, purchased 2026-09-27, `mystery_style='detective'`, `script_type='full'`, `player_count=7`, `has_accomplice=false`, Italian — first Italian package swept under this ADR). Generated 2026-09-27 04:45 UTC, **before** Jonathan imported the two structural register-consistency blueprints from Addendum 56 (`Parent70`, `Child v45`) later that same day — so this package's generation used the pre-fix blueprints throughout, same as every prior sweep.

All four standard detectors and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` run. One hit: `list_packages_with_dangling_quote_mark` flagged `final_statement:Suzanne`. Hand-corrected and re-verified clean — consistent with the expected pre-v44/pre-import baseline, not a new concern.

**Widened full-cast read (per Addendum 56's checklist change) — all 7 characters read in full**, not just a 2-3 sample. Cross-checked `master_context` (murderer/victim/motive/evidence chain), `detective_script`, and `evidence_cards` against every character's `background`/`secret`/`introduction`/`final_statement`. Register checked throughout (Italian tu/Lei distinction): the detective and all 7 characters consistently use the informal/neutral plural "voi" address, with the reveal's arrest line cleverly avoiding a direct singular tu/Lei choice by staying in third-person framing ("Suzanne... è in arresto" rather than "tu sei" or "Lei è"). No register drift found — but since this predates the fix import, a clean result here doesn't confirm Parent70 works; it's simply a well-formed pre-fix generation. **The real test is the next Italian, German, French, Portuguese, or Dutch purchase generated after the import** — watch for that.

**One near-miss, considered and cleared, not a defect:** `evidence_cards`' Round 4 item describes the attendance register as "kept by the studio's custodian" ("tenuto dalla custode dello studio") — and the custodian is Suzanne, the murderer. This isn't stated in `master_context`'s `namedItems` (which only covers 3 objects — the fake Vermeer, Leone's papers, the whisky bottle — and doesn't include this register at all) or in `evidenceProgression.round4.item`, so it's an invented detail, matching the shape of Addendum 56's ledger-ownership bug. Checked whether it creates a real logical contradiction the way that bug did: it doesn't — the murderer-flaw mechanism relies entirely on a witness's contradicting testimony ("someone saw her leave the study, not the kitchen"), not on the register's own entries about her, so attributing the register's upkeep to the custodian (a plausible, in-character detail — it's literally her job) doesn't undermine the solution logic. Documenting this as a worked example of the distinction the widened full-cast check is supposed to draw: an invented cross-field detail is only a defect when it actually contradicts something canonical or breaks the plot mechanism, not merely because it wasn't explicitly specified upstream.
