## Addendum 67 (2026-09-30): "The Last Invitation" — a 2nd occurrence of Addendum 29's garbled reveal-line bug, plus hard evidence the v44 dangling-quote fix isn't landing as well as Addendum 56 assumed

New-Purchase sweep on package `c14de0f2-0a98-4475-9578-4a10c810fc2f` (conversation `14e18f71-6810-4acb-8e13-1af8acf9b414`, $19.99, `sydneylj3@gmail.com`, purchased 2026-09-30, `mystery_style='detective'`, `player_count=8`, `has_accomplice=false`, English, well post-ADR-0074). All five standard detectors, `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch`, and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` run; character count (8) matches `player_count`. Full 8-character cast read in full (per the Addendum 56 standard), plus the complete `detective_script`.

**Found and fixed — 2nd confirmed occurrence of the Addendum 29 garbled-reveal-line bug, 3 weeks apart.** `detective_script`'s climactic REVEAL section read: *"Let the record show: **Jay DeWald's** killer stands revealed... Detective Inspector Marlowe places Jay under arrest for the murder of **Gabe DeWald**."* Jay is the murderer; Gabe DeWald is the victim. "DeWald" is the victim's own surname — confirmed Jay carries no surname anywhere in her own `background`/`relationships`/`secret` (she's explicitly "a longtime member of the friend group," not family), so "Jay DeWald" isn't a legitimate alternate identity, it's the exact same fusion-of-murderer-first-name-onto-victim-surname shape as Addendum 29's "Fernande Cole's killer... murder of Nathan Cole" (package `5849f5a2-b7ef-4cb1-9cf4-b240a03e734a`, 2026-09-08) — almost certainly the same underlying mechanism (the model's parallel-construction habit for naming the victim bleeding into the murderer's name mid-sentence at the one spot in the whole package where both names sit close together). Severity is high despite being one sentence: this line is read aloud to the entire table at the game's climax and makes it briefly sound like the murderer was the one killed. Fixed via a single targeted, exact-match `replace()` scoped to this `package_id` ("Jay DeWald's killer stands revealed" → "Gabe DeWald's killer stands revealed"); re-verified the old substring is gone, the new one is present, and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` is still null.

**Considered building a detector for this bug class, concluded it's genuinely harder than it looks — surfacing to Jonathan rather than deciding solo.** Per the Addendum 38/ADR-0131 test: impact is unambiguously severe-and-obvious (yes), but detection is not actually cheap-and-deterministic the way the dangling-quote fix was. A detector needs the victim's full name and the murderer's name reliably extracted first, and this codebase's own existing `package_victim_mismatch_candidate()` helper (backing `list_packages_with_victim_mismatch`) already shows how fragile that extraction is in practice — it needs 3 fallback regexes against `game_overview`'s free-form prose and still returns `NULL` (silently, by design) when none match. Checked it against this exact package: none of its 3 patterns actually match this `game_overview`'s opening sentence ("On a storm-battered October night, Gabe DeWald threw himself an 'apology tour' reunion..."), so a detector built on the same extraction approach would have missed this specific instance too. Building a reliable version would mean solving victim/murderer-name extraction more robustly first — a bigger investment than the bug-fix itself, for a defect that's shown up twice in ~3 weeks (not zero, but not yet a clear pattern frequency either). Flagging for Jonathan's call: invest in better name extraction to support this detector, or keep catching it via the full-cast manual read (which does catch it — both occurrences were found that way) and wait for a clearer frequency signal.

**Found and fixed — live post-v44 dangling-quote hit, Samantha's `introduction`** (stray trailing `'` after "...Let's figure out what actually happened, together." — no matching opening quote). Trimmed via `regexp_replace(introduction, '''$', '')`, re-verified clean.

**Investigated the post-v44 trend per the standing "worth a fresh look" note (this is now the 4th post-v44 sweep to hit this detector: Addenda 56, 57, 66, and this one) — the data no longer supports "a large reduction from baseline."** Queried every package created since v44's 2026-09-25 import (10 total) and checked each against `list_packages_with_dangling_quote_mark()`, including re-checking packages whose hits had already been hand-fixed in earlier addenda (by re-deriving from each addendum's own writeup, since fixed rows no longer show up live). Result: **5 of the 10 post-v44 packages have had at least one hit** — Vaingloryus (1, fixed in Addendum 56), L'eredità Del Silenzio (1, fixed in Addendum 57 — itself generated *before* that day's later register-fix import, so a pure v44-only data point), Ghost In The Uplink (3, see below), Emma's Honky Tonk (3, fixed in Addendum 66), and this package (1). That's a 50% package-hit-rate, not a rare straggler, and directly contradicts Addendum 56's read of its one early data point ("a large reduction... not evidence the fix failed to land").

**Also found — 3 live, previously-undetected hits in "Ghost In The Uplink" (package `4f9babf8-c691-4987-b2bf-850e92f6522d`), sitting unfixed since 2026-09-27.** This package was swept once already (Addendum 59, triggered by an unrelated customer complaint about a *different* package that happened to also touch this one for a webhook-timeout watch and the Adebayo-Finch name-truncation bug) but that sweep never ran the dangling-quote detector against it at all. Found via this addendum's corpus-wide trend check, not a routine sweep. Confirmed via `right(field, 40)`: `reveal_confession_guilty` for Bram/Briar Solano and Odalys/Odell Voss, and `reveal_confession_accomplice` for Vex/Vexa Castellan, each ending in an unmatched `'`. This package was already delivered to a paying customer (`is_paid=true`) 3 days before this fix. Fixed all 3 via the same narrow `regexp_replace` trim, re-verified `list_packages_with_dangling_quote_mark()` returns 0 rows for both this package and Ghost In The Uplink.

**Root-caused why Ghost In The Uplink's hits went unfixed for 3 days: this detector has no auto-remediation wiring at all.** Checked `supabase/functions/*/index.ts` for any reference to `dangling_quote` or `package_completion_blocking_defects` calling it — none. Unlike the accomplice-role and culprit-accusations detectors shipped in Addendum 66 (wired into `package_completion_blocking_defects()`, so a hit holds the completion gate and reaches the existing auto-remediation/health-check alert path), `list_packages_with_dangling_quote_mark()` has only ever been a manual-sweep-time check since Addendum 55 shipped it — a hit is invisible to every automated system and only gets caught if a human happens to run this exact query against this exact package. **Surfacing to Jonathan: the fix itself is a trivial, zero-cost, deterministic string trim (no LLM call, no ambiguity — a trailing quote with no opening match is unambiguously wrong) that passes the Addendum 38/ADR-0131 "cheap+severe-ish → build now" test on the cheap-detection half at least; the open question is whether it's worth wiring `package_completion_blocking_defects()` (or a lighter-weight dedicated auto-fix cron, since this needs a trim not a regeneration) so this class stops depending on sweep timing/luck to get caught**, the same gap this exact addendum just demonstrated concretely (3 days, one paid delivered package, zero automated detection).

### Key files (Addendum 67)
- `mystery_packages.detective_script` — 1 row corrected (`c14de0f2-0a98-4475-9578-4a10c810fc2f`, "Jay DeWald's killer" → "Gabe DeWald's killer")
- `mystery_characters.introduction` — 1 row corrected (`c14de0f2-0a98-4475-9578-4a10c810fc2f`, Samantha, dangling-quote-mark)
- `mystery_characters.reveal_confession_guilty` — 2 rows corrected (`4f9babf8-c691-4987-b2bf-850e92f6522d`, Bram/Briar Solano, Odalys/Odell Voss)
- `mystery_characters.reveal_confession_accomplice` — 1 row corrected (`4f9babf8-c691-4987-b2bf-850e92f6522d`, Vex/Vexa Castellan)
- No migration, no detector shipped this session — both open questions (garbled-reveal-line detector feasibility, dangling-quote auto-remediation wiring) surfaced to Jonathan rather than decided solo

## Addendum 68 (2026-10-01): second line of defense shipped in `adapt-mystery-create` — a corrupted accomplice-role cast can no longer enter the Remove-a-Character reassignment flow at all

Follow-up to Addendum 65/66's `GuestDropoutPanel.tsx`/`isReassignRole()` finding: `package_accomplice_role_mismatch()` closes the gap going forward at generation time, but only re-validates a package at its own completion transition — it can never retroactively protect an already-completed package, and (as Addendum 3 of ADR-0069, found the same session, demonstrates) the trigger that runs it can itself regress silently. Rather than rely solely on the upstream gate, added a direct sanity check inside `adapt-mystery-create` itself, at the exact point a murderer/accomplice removal is accepted: before treating the request as eligible, count how many characters in the full cast (already loaded in memory for the existing headcount guard, so this is a zero-cost addition) share that exact `character_role`. If it's not exactly 1, reject the whole batch with a distinct `role_data_inconsistent` error rather than silently proceeding into the expensive, invasive reassignment rewrite (`adapt-mystery-apply` rewrites `detective_script`, evidence cards, and invents a new culprit) against a cast that's already structurally wrong — which would compound the corruption rather than just fail safely.

Confirmed the frontend (`GuestDropoutPanel.tsx`) already degrades gracefully on an unrecognized error reason (falls through to a generic `createFailed` toast, no crash) — no frontend change needed; per this function's own header comment, the frontend gate is a UX convenience, the backend is the real check.

Deployed via `supabase functions deploy adapt-mystery-create` and verified live (`get_edge_function`: version 7, guard code present, `verify_jwt: true` preserved).

### Key files (Addendum 68)
- `supabase/functions/adapt-mystery-create/index.ts` — role-count guard added, deployed live (v7)
- No DB migration — this is application-layer defense-in-depth, not a new detector

## Addendum 69 (2026-10-01): New-Purchase sweep — "The Night The Storm Hit" — AI recap heading leaked verbatim into the customer-facing title, fixed at source

Jonathan pasted the purchase notification and flagged the title himself ("The Night The Storm Hit — Full Final Recap... that's probably not supposed to be there"). Conversation `39ced5e9-56d7-4fef-bee5-a3731e44768f`, package `2b06bcef-2d57-496d-b6e3-2a84bad104c1` — 14-character, `detective`-style, `has_accomplice=true`, English, post-v44.

**Root cause.** `conversations.title` and `mystery_packages.title` were both the literal string `"The Night The Storm Hit — Full Final Recap"`. Traced it to `user_conversation`: this customer's concept chat ran long (3 days, ~270 messages, several revision passes), and at some point the assistant wrote its own closing summary headed `# THE NIGHT THE STORM HIT — FULL FINAL RECAP`. `src/utils/titleExtraction.ts`'s FIRST-PASS header regex (`/^#\s+(.+?)\s*$/m`) captures the entire H1 line verbatim by design (a deliberate prior fix — see the comment above it — to stop a restrictive character class from dropping titles with commas/ampersands). `isLikelySectionHeader()` only checks whether a title *starts with* a handful of section words ("questions", "overview", "premise", ...) — nothing in the pipeline strips a trailing descriptor. The corrupted title was written client-side in `MysteryChat.tsx` on the first qualifying AI message, then passed through unchanged by `mystery-webhook-trigger` (`title: conversation.title || ...`) into the Make.com parent payload and back into `mystery_packages.title` — this is a pure passthrough, not a separate Make.com title-generation bug.

**Fix (cheap, deterministic, severe — built immediately per the Addendum 38 test, same as Addendum 45).** Added `stripMetaSuffix()` to `titleExtraction.ts`: strips a dash-separated trailing segment (`— Full Final Recap`, `— Recap`, `— Summary`, `— Overview`, `— Draft`, case-insensitive) from the extracted title before `formatTitle()`, applied in all three extraction passes (header, label, bold-text). Deliberately gated on a dash separator rather than any separator — colon-separated subtitles are a legitimate, common pattern in this corpus (`Dead On Arrival: The Blackwood Victory`, `Murder At The 19th Hole: The Viagha Affair`) and must not be touched. Verified against those and a handful of synthetic cases before shipping.

**Corpus check.** Regex-scanned every `conversations.title`/`mystery_packages.title` for the same dash+meta-word suffix shape — this package was the only hit in the entire corpus. Genuine one-off, not a backlog; no further live rows needed fixing.

**Live data fix.** `conversations.title` and `mystery_packages.title` both updated directly to `"The Night The Storm Hit"`.

**Rest of the sweep: clean.** All five standard detectors (`meta_text_leak`, `victim_mismatch`, `unresolved_victim_name`, `dangling_quote_mark`, `role_tag_leak`), `package_completion_blocking_defects()`, and `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch()` all returned empty. Victim (Miranda Whitlock), murderer (Naomi Petrakis), and accomplice (Callum Ashford) names consistent across `game_overview`, `master_context`, and `detective_script`. Evidence cards (planner page, cracked crystal, first-aid tin/supply list) match the detective script's round-by-round reveals exactly. Full 14-character cast read (background/secret/relationships/all four rounds/final statement/accusations) found no cross-field contradictions — the overlapping subplots (Della Voss 2008 cover-up, Grant's drink-spiking, Wren's kleptomania and the stolen watch, Theo's ring, Rowan's curse) are deliberately interlocking, not conflicting, and match the customer's own stated theme request ("a lot of sideplots going on... evidence was tampered with"). One detail that looked like a contradiction on first read — Naomi's own `round4_script` gives a bare "knife" excuse while three other characters' scripts quote her specific "knife while cutting a lime" story — turned out to be a corroborated 3-witness clue (Danielle, Theo, and Vivienne all independently report the identical "knife + lime" line, which is the planted tell the detective calls out at the reveal), not an inconsistency. `host_guide`/`preparation_instructions`/`timeline`/`hosting_tips` are null, same as every other package in the last 10 — confirmed deprecated/consolidated columns, not a gap.

### Key files (Addendum 69)
- `src/utils/titleExtraction.ts` — `stripMetaSuffix()` added, applied in all three extraction passes
- `conversations.title`, `mystery_packages.title` (package `2b06bcef-2d57-496d-b6e3-2a84bad104c1`) — corrected live to "The Night The Storm Hit"
- No migration, no new detector — the fix is a client-side regex change plus a one-time corpus check that found no backlog

## Addendum 70 (2026-10-01): closing the two open items from Addendum 67 — dangling-quote self-heal shipped, reveal-line name-fusion detector shipped, prevention-at-source blueprint fix drafted

Jonathan's direct ask after Addendum 67: "let's build fixes so none of these things happen again — shouldn't [the garbled reveal line] have been self-healed or avoided entirely?" Addressed both open items from that addendum, plus the root-cause prevention Addendum 67 itself didn't attempt.

**1. Dangling-quote-mark now self-heals.** `list_packages_with_dangling_quote_mark()` was refactored to call a new shared helper, `package_dangling_quote_mark(_pkg)` (ADR-0131 single-source-of-truth pattern, same shape as `package_victim_mismatch_candidate`/`package_accomplice_role_mismatch`), which is now also wired into `package_completion_blocking_defects()` — a hit holds the completion gate and reaches the existing health-check/auto-remediation path instead of depending on sweep timing. `auto-remediate-packages` gained a real deterministic handler, `handleDanglingQuoteMark` (free, no LLM): parses the detector's `field:character_name` sources, strips exactly the one trailing stray quote character via a JS regex that mirrors the SQL detector's own match precisely, writes it back through the existing `runGatedAttempt` re-detect-gate/revert machinery (same pattern as `handleSelfDirectedQuestions`/`handleTemplateArtifacts`). Deployed via `supabase functions deploy` (CLI, not the MCP inline-content path per standing practice) — confirmed live content matches source and `verify_jwt: true` was preserved.

**2. New detector for the reveal-line murderer/victim name-fusion bug — `package_reveal_name_fusion()` / `list_packages_with_reveal_name_fusion()`, wired into the completion gate, deliberately NOT auto-remediated.** Victim name is extracted from `master_context`'s `victimProfile.name` via regex — the same pattern already proven live in `auto-remediate-packages`' own `getVictimName()` helper, validated here against 15/15 recent packages (far more reliable than `package_victim_mismatch_candidate`'s `game_overview`-prose fallback, which doesn't even match this package's own `game_overview` shape). One SQL portability catch while building this: Postgres's regex engine caps bounded repetition at 255 (`{0,400}?` from the JS version throws "invalid repetition count(s)"; `{0,255}?` is the max that works) — not documented anywhere obvious, found by bisecting a minimal repro.

Before shipping, ran the detector against the full corpus and found 6 raw candidates, which forced two false-positive guards that weren't obvious from the 2 instances already in hand: (a) a title ("Dr.", "Professor," etc.) mistaken for a first name — "Dr. Splice" and "Professor Vaingloryus" are legitimate references to a victim who happens to share the murderer's own title, not fusions; (b) a surname the murderer legitimately shares with the victim as an established family name — "Festa Fatal"'s "Júnior Nacife" is genuinely his own name (the surname appears on 7 other cast members' own `character_name`), not a bug, even though his own `background`/`relationships` text never happens to state it directly (the first guard I tried — checking the murderer's own content for the surname — would have missed this; checking the surname against the *whole cast's* `character_name` column is what actually closes it). After both guards: 1 real hit remained, a 3rd confirmed occurrence, previously unknown — **"Ghosts Of The Past: A Halloween Reunion Murder"** (package `8b0729ac-9709-4ab3-9e56-b5121af048b2`, delivered 2026-07-28, predates even Addendum 29): *"Did you help **Riley Blackwell** take **Reese Blackwell**'s life?"* — Riley is the murderer, Reese Blackwell the victim. Fixed via the same narrow exact-match `replace()` as the other two instances.

**Why this one is detection-only.** Tested the obvious deterministic fix — swap `<murderer-first> <victim-surname>` for the victim's full name — against both this instance and Addendum 67's own: correct for "Jay DeWald's killer" → "Gabe DeWald's killer" (exactly right), but produces "Did you help **Reese Blackwell** take Reese Blackwell's life?" for this one — nonsense, implies the victim helped kill herself, because this sentence's correct fix is to *remove* the fused surname, not swap it. The right repair depends on surrounding sentence structure, which varies by instance. This is the same "no mechanical fix → escalate, don't guess" shape this codebase already treats as detection-only elsewhere (`identity_conflict` has an identical shape: a completion-gate check with zero auto-remediation handler). A human picks the right surgical edit each time; the gain here is that it's no longer invisible between sweeps.

**3. Prevention at the source — blueprint fix drafted, not yet imported.** All 3 confirmed occurrences sit in detective_script's `## THE REVEAL` section (the one place the murderer's and victim's names are already in close narrative proximity by design). Added a new `NAME-FUSION GUARDRAIL` paragraph to that section's prompt text, in all 4 routes (detective/character × murder/intrigue — same 4-route shape as every prior Parent-blueprint fix in this ADR), explicitly naming the exact failure shape with the real "Jay"/"Gabe DeWald" example so the model has a concrete anti-pattern to check against, plus an explicit self-check instruction. Built via `temp-files/build-parent-v71.py` (same scripted-not-hand-edited pattern as every prior multi-route fix, to guarantee identical wording across all 4), anchored on the `CRITICAL: Output ONLY the markdown above...` line already shared byte-identically across all 4 REVEAL prompts — confirmed present exactly once per route before patching, confirmed the guard landed exactly once per route after. JSON-validated. Saved as `temp-files/MM Live - Parent71 (Reveal Name-Fusion Guardrail).blueprint.json`. **Imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-10-01.** Not yet confirmed as the scenario the production webhook actually calls, not yet validated against a real generation (same open items as every prior blueprint import in this ADR) — the next detective-style purchase is the real test; the `reveal_name_fusion` detector shipped in this same addendum is what will catch it live if the fix didn't fully land, same post-import-watch pattern as the dangling-quote v44 fix (Addendum 55/56).

**Verification before considering this closed.** Ran both new checks (`package_dangling_quote_mark`, `package_reveal_name_fusion`) via `package_completion_blocking_defects()` against all 98 packages from the last 60 days — zero errors, exactly one hit, and that hit (`missing_round_content`) is a pre-existing, unrelated defect class, confirming no false positives or crashes introduced on live data. Re-ran both advisory RPCs corpus-wide post-fix: 0 rows each (all 3 known reveal-fusion instances and all then-live dangling-quote instances are fixed).

**Not independently live-tested end-to-end** (no access to the actual `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` value in this session to invoke the deployed function directly) — confidence instead comes from: the deployed function's content byte-verified against the reviewed source, the handler reusing the exact `runGatedAttempt` machinery already proven correct for `self_directed_questions`/`template_artifact`, and the underlying SQL transform validated directly against the real corpus. The next live dangling-quote hit (the 5-of-10 post-v44 rate from Addendum 67 means this should be soon) is the real first end-to-end test — worth a quick check next sweep that `auto_remediation_log` actually shows a `fixed` row for `dangling_quote_mark` rather than relying on this writeup's confidence alone.

### Key files (Addendum 70)
- `supabase/migrations/20261001010000_add_dangling_quote_autoheal_and_reveal_name_fusion_detector.sql` — `package_dangling_quote_mark()` (new shared helper), `package_reveal_name_fusion()`/`list_packages_with_reveal_name_fusion()` (new, detection-only), both wired into `package_completion_blocking_defects()`; `list_packages_with_dangling_quote_mark()` refactored onto the shared helper
- `supabase/functions/auto-remediate-packages/index.ts` — new `dangling_quote_mark` defect class + `handleDanglingQuoteMark` deterministic handler, wired into the dispatch loop; deployed live (CLI), `verify_jwt: true` preserved
- `mystery_packages.detective_script` — 1 row corrected (`8b0729ac-9709-4ab3-9e56-b5121af048b2`, "Riley Blackwell take Reese Blackwell's life" → "Riley take Reese Blackwell's life")
- `temp-files/build-parent-v71.py`, `temp-files/MM Live - Parent71 (Reveal Name-Fusion Guardrail).blueprint.json` — prevention-at-source prompt fix, all 4 routes; **imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-10-01**


## Addendum 71 (2026-10-01): "A Feast For The Dying" sweep: 4 leaks + 1 fact inversion fixed, meta-text detector widened, a second paid package found with the same leak

New-Purchase sweep on "A Feast For The Dying" (conversation `1c5a07a5-f249-40dc-bcd9-08d9f7a65ec5`, package `f25f316e-59ff-48f9-99eb-0cefadf8aea3`, slip-style, 6 characters, no accomplice, post-ADR-0074 so Sonnet 5). Paired with a `needs_review` generation-issue alert listing `meta_text_leak.character.Steward Iona/Ionas Blackwell` and `dangling_quote_mark.introduction:Brother/Sister Edmund Vale`.

**Findings (full 6-character cast, detective_script, evidence_cards and master_context read):**
1. Steward Blackwell `final_innocent`: "Wait, I need to close this properly." followed by a verbatim duplicate of the whole section. Trimmed to the first copy. This is what the alert flagged.
2. Percival Ashgrove `final_innocent`: trailing "Wait — I need to review that formatting before finalizing." **The detector did not catch this one**: the pattern was `wait, i need to` (comma only) and this leak used an em dash. Found by manual read.
3. Duskwood `reveal_confession_guilty`: a stray trailing fragment ("Caught them. End of lies, and end of a tyrant - some debts no apology can repay."). Removed.
4. Vesper `round2_innocent`: inverted the blood-debt ("a kindness I once extended when I saved his get") against `master_context` and her own other branches (Aldric saved *her* get). Corrected. A cross-field fact inversion, caught only by reading.
5. Vesper `accusations`: one deflection tip lost its bullet marker (cosmetic, fixed). Percival's confession said "Last night" for a same-night event; changed to "Tonight".
The dangling-quote flag on Edmund Vale's introduction was already gone when the sweep ran; later confirmed in `auto_remediation_log` as an Addendum 70 self-heal success (see follow-up).
All five standard detectors + `package_completion_blocking_defects()` returned empty after the fixes; package flipped `needs_review` → `completed` (conditional on the blocking-defects check being null).

**Detector gap, fixed same session (cheap + deterministic + severe, per the Addendum 38 test).** Migration `20261001020000_widen_meta_text_leak_detector.sql`: (a) `wait, i need to` → `wait\s*[,—–-]+\s*i need to`; (b) `package_meta_text_leak()` now also scans `reveal_confession_guilty`/`reveal_confession_accomplice`, which it never looked at. Corpus check before shipping found exactly one other live instance: **"Operation: Thirty & Murdery" (`b8428a57-1c2b-4bf1-881c-98c8436be6a9`), a paid package from 2026-08-25**, `reveal_confession_accomplice` of Indigo/India Mercier had a "Wait, I need to correct that last piece..." leak plus a duplicated confession (one copy hardcoding a murderer's name). Live for ~5 weeks. Fixed by keeping the clean second copy. No other duplicated-section rows exist corpus-wide (checked `## THE REVEAL`/`## FINAL STATEMENT` repeat counts across all 2,507 characters).

**Follow-up (same session, Jonathan's call):**
- **Prevention at source: `Child48-EmitFieldOnce` blueprint** (`temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)48-EmitFieldOnce.blueprint.json`, built by `temp-files/build-child-v48.py` from Child47; diff vs Child47 is exactly the 8 prompt texts, +684 chars each). All 8 prompts already had a `<no_chain_of_thought_in_output>` block, but it only covered mid-field self-corrections ("Wait, this is wrong", "Let me recalculate"). The real failure mode here is different: the model *finishes* a field, appends "Wait, I need to close/review/correct...", then re-emits the whole section. New rule: emit each field exactly once, stop at the last paragraph, a section heading may appear at most once per field, never comment on own formatting/names. **Imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-10-01; not yet validated against a real post-import generation.**
- **Self-heal gap closed in `auto-remediate-packages` (v18, deployed via CLI, verify_jwt true preserved, live content verified):** it carries a hand-synced copy of the detector regex (`PROSE_LEAK_RX`) with the same comma-only `wait, i need to`, so an em-dash leak the widened detector now flags would not have been routed to delegation. Widened to match, and added `reveal_confession_guilty`/`reveal_confession_accomplice` to `CHARACTER_ARTIFACT_FIELDS` (`regenerate-child-content` already supported both). Together with the prompt fix this means a recurrence is both less likely and self-healing via the existing ADR-0099 regenerate path. Not live-tested end-to-end against a real leak. **Why Blackwell's leak had not self-healed (investigated after wrap-up question):** `auto_remediation_log` shows the `template_artifact` strip correctly refused (embedded leak, not strippable), then the ADR-0099 delegation to `regenerate-child-content` logged `invoke_failed` at 18:00 and 18:05, then `skip:attempt_cap` at 18:10, so it needed a human. Context: over the last 21 days 4 delegations logged `invoke_failed`, and 3 of those 4 were followed by a successful regeneration on the same package, so this is intermittent, not systematic; Blackwell's package just used up its attempts on two consecutive failures. **Root cause of the invoke failures not found** (the MCP log query couldn't read edge-function logs this session). If it recurs, the cheap candidate fix is to not count `invoke_failed` (call never reached the regenerator) against the attempt cap. Not built: too few instances. Also corrects the earlier note on the dangling-quote flag: the log shows Addendum 70's self-heal DID fix Edmund Vale's introduction at 18:13 (`strip_dangling_quote:introduction`, outcome `fixed`), the first confirmed live success of that handler. The package's missing Round 2 evidence image was also auto-regenerated (system spend, $0.16, same cron).
- **Deliberately not built:** a deterministic "trim at the marker, keep the second copy" handler. Needs a per-case call on which copy is canonical (trivial for the 2 instances seen, not proven general), and the regenerate path already covers the class. 2 instances in ~5 weeks does not justify more.
- **Older packages (8 pre-2026-04 matches of the widened detector): Jonathan declined to examine them.**
- **Why so many unrelated defects in one package:** they were different bug classes (self-correction leak x2, stray fragment, fact inversion, formatting, tense). Only the leak class is cheaply detectable and now has prevention. The fact inversion (Vesper) is the semantic-contradiction class already ruled manual-only (Addendum 56); the rest are one-offs below the build threshold (Addendum 38 test).

### Key files (Addendum 71)
- `supabase/migrations/20261001020000_widen_meta_text_leak_detector.sql`: applied live via MCP `apply_migration`
- `supabase/functions/auto-remediate-packages/index.ts`: `PROSE_LEAK_RX` widened, reveal fields added; deployed v18
- `temp-files/build-child-v48.py`, `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)48-EmitFieldOnce.blueprint.json`: prompt fix, imported 2026-10-01
- Data fixes: 6 `mystery_characters` rows in `f25f316e-…` (Blackwell, Percival, Duskwood, Vesper) + 1 row in `b8428a57-…` (Mercier); `mystery_packages.generation_status` for `f25f316e-…`

## Addendum 72 (2026-10-02): New-Purchase sweep — "The Crown's Shadow: A Festival Of Secrets" — first generation on Parent72/Child48; quote self-heal fired again; 3 text slips fixed by hand; innocents naming the murderer flagged, not fixed

Conversation `a80910cf-bdea-4d7d-ad23-f0ec64dd933b`, package `c829079c-ff20-439c-bd84-109ff89a64b5` (detective style, pointForm, 8 characters, no accomplice, created 2026-10-01 23:55 UTC). Triggered by a `needs_review` alert (`dangling_quote_mark.introduction:Lady Arabella Bellrose`).

**Parent72 verified live.** This was the first package generated after Parent72 (ADR-0133) was imported. Part 2 completed and `master_context` holds both halves (`evidenceProgression`, `hostBriefing`), `detective_script`, `game_overview` and `evidence_cards` all present and consistent with each other. Removing the duplicate Part 1 injection did not change what downstream modules receive.

**The alert was legitimate and already healed.** Arabella's `introduction` ended in a stray `'` (`...mine included.'`). Addendum 70's handler stripped it at 00:13 UTC (`strip_dangling_quote:introduction`, outcome `fixed`, $0), about 18 minutes after creation, and the status flipped back to `completed`. All six sweep detectors and `package_completion_blocking_defects` return clean.

**This is the second post-fix quote hit, and both are `introduction`.** The other was Edmund Vale's in `f25f316e` (Addendum 71). Both are a single trailing `'` after the closing sentence of the profile call's `introduction`. Of 6 packages since 2026-09-30, 2 needed it; the heal log only started 2026-10-01 18:13 UTC, so the pre-heal rate is unknown (the detector shows nothing currently dirty for earlier packages). Child48 was live for this one. **Root cause not found; no prompt change made.** Cost to ship is zero while the self-heal works, so this was left alone; if it keeps recurring, the place to look is the node 401/501 `introduction` schema template, since neither hit was in any other field.

**Full-cast read (all 8, prose and pointform spot-checked against the canonical Part 1 facts and the three evidence cards).** Names, amounts (40,000 crowns), weapon, victim and alibis agree across fields. Fixed by hand (5 field edits, exact-match replaces, verified one row each):
- Elara, `round3_script`: "a apothecary's" -> "an apothecary's".
- Elara, `round4_script` + `round4_script_pointform`: the cordial gap was described as "between the music starting and the Duchess's confrontation wrapping up"; every other character and the Round 4 evidence place it **before** the music started. Now "before the music started, after the confrontation wrapped up".
- Seraphina, `round4_script` + `round4_script_pointform`: "three Valedore attendants" -> "Frostmere attendants" (Valedore is the rival alliance partner; the rest of her text says her own attendants).

**Flagged, not fixed (needs Jonathan):**
1. **Innocents name or point straight at the murderer before the reveal.** Elara's and Nicholas's `final_statement` name Miriam Quill outright; Elara's and Nicholas's Round 4 scripts cite Miriam's "shawl" alibi and the "M.Q." page; Penelope, Iridessa, Arabella and Rowan point at "the one with archive/record access" without naming her. The child prompt tells suspects/red herrings to write "an emotional defense and theory about who really did it", and every child call sees the full `master_context`, so the theory is the correct answer. Corpus proxy (60 days, detective-style): 148 of 716 innocents' `final_statement`s (21%, 40 of 65 packages) contain the murderer's first name. A name mention is not the same as an accusation, so this overstates it, and detecting accusation vs. mention needs judgment (fails the Addendum 38 cheap-and-deterministic test). Not fixed per-package because the fix is regeneration (paid) or a rewrite by hand across the cast. This is also the concrete cost of the ADR-0133 open question about giving the child the full `master_context`.
2. **Nicholas's secret contradicts his own script.** The secret says he has "told no one" where the Crown is (not even Penelope); his Round 2 script says "yes, the bread chest, go ahead and laugh" and his relationships say he spent the evening "muttering over bread chests" with Iridessa. One character, low severity.
3. **`description` format is inconsistent**: 3 of 8 are first person, 4 carry the `## CHARACTER DESCRIPTION` header and 4 don't. Cosmetic.

**Separate health-check alert, "Trick, Treat, Dead" roster-extraction anomaly: false positive, not a regex regression.** Conversation `89738524-8954-4254-a290-63ec0a1be502` (unpaid, 2026-10-02 00:32 UTC): the customer chose 4 players (the product minimum) but wrote "only 2 people will play", and the AI agreed and produced a 2-character cast under a "Character List (2 players)" header. The parser extracted both names correctly; `MIN_ROSTER_SIZE = 4` in `_shared/rosterExtraction.ts` then discarded the roster, which the anomaly detector reads as "header present, nothing parsed". Nothing changed. Open question for Jonathan: should the detector skip headers claiming fewer than 4, and should the concept chat steer a 2-person game toward 4 characters (the product range is 4-35)?

### Follow-up (2026-10-02, same day, after Jonathan's review)
- **Crown's Shadow made whole by hand (no regeneration, $0).** Removed the pre-reveal spoilers from Elara's and Nicholas's text: Elara's `final_statement` no longer names Miriam or her motive and her Round 4 no longer attributes the shawl errand to Miriam; Nicholas's `final_statement` no longer names Miriam, no longer knows the private confrontation was about "founding records", and no longer genders the culprit ("her"); all mirrored in the pointform fields. Nicholas's secret ("told no one") now says he has not told Penelope, matching his own Round 2 script (`secret` and `secrets` kept in sync). Standard applied: innocents may argue from the evidence cards and what was said aloud in the rounds, but may not name the culprit, state their private motive, or know what the private confrontation was about. Left alone on purpose: questions aimed at Miriam (a game mechanic, and the "M.Q." card makes her an obvious target) and "shawl" callbacks (the detective's own Round 4 script introduces the word). `package_completion_blocking_defects` still clean afterward.
- **No detector for the by-hand slips.** The a/an typo has 0 other hits in 1,171 recent characters (word-list check), the timing and wrong-kingdom slips are semantic contradictions (Addendum 56/38: no cheap deterministic detection, subtle impact). Revisit only if they recur.
- **Why Jonathan got an alert for a package that then self-healed:** `WORKER_RECOGNIZED_PREFIXES` in `notify-generation-issue` (the ADR-0065 grace-period allowlist) lists only `meta_text_leak`, `self_directed_question`, `victim_mismatch`, `identity_conflict`, `slip_culprit_leak`. `dangling_quote_mark` (Addendum 70) and `narration_person_mismatch` (Addendum 45) both have worker handlers but were never added, so those alert immediately instead of waiting out the 35-minute window. Same paired-predicate drift shape as ADR-0131. **Fix not yet shipped; awaiting Jonathan's go-ahead.**
- **Where the "theory about who really did it" instruction lives:** node 409 (detective-style `finalStatement`, suspect/red herring clause) and node 509 (slip-style `finalInnocent`); 513's is the murderer's own deflection ("a plausible theory about who else could have done it") and is correct. Slip-style cannot leak because the culprit is drawn at the table and is not in `master_context`; detective-style leaks because the culprit is fixed there. The detective's own Final Statements script only asks for "your final defense, your last plea of innocence", so nothing depends on the theory in detective-style. Tighter corpus proxy (murderer's name near an accusation cue in an innocent's `final_statement`, detective-style, 60 days): 67 of 716 (about 9%), vs 21% for any mention. **Proposed, not built:** Child49 changing node 409's clause to defense-only plus one innocent-ignorance rule (suspects and red herrings do not know who the murderer is and must not name them or their private motive/alibi). Round 4 scripts also leaked (Elara, Nicholas) with no "theory" instruction in play, so the rule is needed in addition to the clause change.
- **Trick, Treat, Dead: nothing changed, on hold by Jonathan's call.** Separate unpaid customer; the 2-character cast is what the customer settled on in chat. No fix until the customer confirms what they want; a confirmation email was drafted for Jonathan to send (not sent).

- `auto_remediation_log` row `9d41b346-…` (the quote heal)

## Addendum 73 (2026-10-02): Child49 stops detective-style innocents from naming or knowing the murderer; 2-player games confirmed unsupported

**Child49 (`temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)49-InnocentIgnorance.blueprint.json`, built by `temp-files/build-child-v49.py` from Child48).** Follows Addendum 72's finding. Two edits, node 409 only (the detective-style round/final call):
1. `finalStatement` clause for SUSPECT/RED HERRING changed from "an emotional defense and theory about who really did it" to a defense of their own innocence only, with an explicit ban on naming or theorizing about the murderer.
2. New `INNOCENT IGNORANCE` bullet in `content_coherence_rules`, active only for suspect/redHerring: they do not know who the murderer is, must not name/accuse them, must not state their private motive, concealed alibi or the content of a private confrontation as known facts, and may only draw on their own history, what was publicly visible, the evidence cards, and what is said aloud in the rounds.

Why both: Crown's Shadow's Round 4 scripts leaked (the culprit's concealed alibi, the confrontation topic) with no "theory" instruction in play, so changing the clause alone would not have been enough.

Why 409 only: detective-style fixes the culprit in `master_context`, which every child call sees; slip-style (5xx) draws the culprit at the table, so its innocent "theory" cannot leak. Node 513's murderer-side "plausible theory about who else could have done it" is the culprit's own deflection and stays. Nodes 401/405 (profile, rumors) were left alone: no leak was observed there, and rumors about the murderer's visible movements are legitimate.

Diff against Child48: exactly node 409's prompt text (+910 chars) and the blueprint `name`. The internal `name` had been stale at "Child (Unified)40" since v41 (earlier builders' `replace()` silently no-op'd), so Child49 sets it explicitly.

**Imported into Make.com by Jonathan 2026-10-02; not yet verified on a real generation.** No test generation run (it would call the paid API). Verification once imported: after the first detective-style package, read the innocents' `final_statement`s and Round 4 scripts, and re-run the proxy query (murderer's first name within 80 characters of an accusation cue in a suspect/redHerring `final_statement`; baseline 67 of 716, about 9%, across 65 detective packages in the prior 60 days). Risk to watch: innocents' finals becoming flat or repetitive now that the "theory" is gone.

**2-player games are not supported (checked, not run end to end).** Prompted by "Trick, Treat, Dead" (unpaid customer, chose 4 players, said only 2 would play, chat settled on a 2-character cast). Evidence from code and prompts, three independent places assume at least 4:
- `mystery-ai` accepts only 4-35 for the declared count; a 2-person "additional detail" slipped past because the declared count was 4.
- `_shared/rosterExtraction.ts` `MIN_ROSTER_SIZE = 4` discards a 2-character roster, so the checkout preview is empty and `mystery-webhook-trigger` falls through to its legacy scan and then the Claude fallback (not traced further).
- Part 1 requires the murderer to have at least 2 Hostile relationships and 2-3 other characters who could have left the same evidence; Part 2's funnel is Round 2 5-6 suspects, Round 3 3-4, Round 4 2-3, with 2-3 red herrings and evidence "implicating 4-6 characters". None of that is satisfiable with one other character.
Likely failure if someone paid for it: fabricated characters up to the declared 4 (the ADR-0118 shape) or a roster/completion mismatch. Jonathan's design point, recorded: even if it generated, with two suspects played by the two guests the innocent player knows the other is guilty, so it only works as a performance for non-participants.
- **Decision: nothing changed for that customer.** On hold until the customer replies; Jonathan sends the confirmation email (draft given in chat, not sent). **Possible follow-up, not built:** have concept chat state the 4-character minimum when a customer says fewer than 4 people will play, instead of agreeing to a smaller cast.

## Addendum 74 (2026-10-02): the checkout roster-anomaly alert no longer fires on unpaid concepts that state a cast smaller than the product minimum

**Trigger:** the 6-hourly health check commented on the GitHub issue about "Trick, Treat, Dead" (`paid=false`). Jonathan's question: how do we stop alerts about unpaid mysteries?

**Why not "only alert on paid":** this detector (ADR-0130 Addendum 2) runs on the pre-purchase checkout page, so every legitimate hit is unpaid by construction. The ADR-0130 incident itself blocked would-be buyers before payment. Filtering on `is_paid` would have switched the detector off entirely. The fix has to target the false-positive shape.

**Root cause (this instance):** `isRosterExtractionAnomaly` flagged any assistant message with a character-list header when zero characters came back. A 2-character cast under "## Character List (2 players)" parsed fine, then `MIN_ROSTER_SIZE = 4` discarded it on purpose (the product supports 4-35; see Addendum 73), so it looked like "header present, nothing parsed".

**Change:** `isRosterExtractionAnomaly` (in `_shared/rosterExtraction.ts`) now ignores a header whose own stated count is below `MIN_ROSTER_SIZE`, using the existing `extractStatedRosterCount`. A header with no stated count, or a stated count of 4 or more, with nothing parsed, is still an anomaly, so the real regression shape is unchanged. New offline test `scripts/__tests__/rosterAnomaly.test.mjs` (11 cases, no network, added to `npm run test:roster`; confirmed it fails against the old predicate and passes against the new one). The `.gitignore` needed a `!scripts/__tests__/rosterAnomaly.test.mjs` line (the `*.mjs` rule would otherwise have silently excluded it).

**Deployed:** `extract-concept-roster` only, as v8 (`verify_jwt` still `false`, matching `config.toml`). Live source re-downloaded and confirmed equal to the repo for both `index.ts` and the shared file. Deliberately did NOT redeploy `mystery-webhook-trigger` or `mystery-ai`: they bundle the shared file but never call this function, so their behavior is unchanged, and redeploying the payment-critical generation trigger for no behavioral gain was unnecessary risk. Consequence: their bundled copy of the shared file differs from the repo until the next ordinary roster deploy (`scripts/deploy-roster-functions.sh`) syncs it; harmless for now.

**Data:** deleted the single `roster_extraction_anomalies` row for that conversation (id `eddfe28a-11c0-400b-8fb0-9d5557dbe648`, conversation `89738524-8954-4254-a290-63ec0a1be502`, player_count 4, detected 2026-10-02 00:46:41 UTC). It was the only row the table had ever held, and without deleting it the next health checks would have re-reported it for up to 24 hours. The `acknowledged_health_alerts` table could not be used: it is keyed by package and this conversation never had one.

**Not done / still open:** a 3-character concept with a different header wording that omits the count would still alert (the count is only read from the header). Not pursued: no such case seen. Concept chat still does not state the 4-character minimum when a customer says fewer than 4 will play (Addendum 73).

## Addendum 75 (2026-10-02): concept chat now states the 4-character minimum

Closes the open item from Addendums 73 and 74. **Problem:** "Trick, Treat, Dead" picked 4 players on the form and added "only 2 people will play" as a detail. The chat explained the mismatch, then agreed to "just 2 players" and wrote a 2-character concept the product cannot generate (chat range gate checks only the declared count; roster extraction discards casts under 4; Part 1/Part 2 need 4+ suspects). The customer reached a blank checkout preview with nothing explaining why.

**Change:** `supabase/functions/mystery-ai/index.ts` gets one more unconditional `CRITICAL:` guardrail appended to the system prompt, the same mechanism and placement as the existing ones (round count, 50-character ceiling, live-crime, investigator roles), so it applies to every branch including the dominant `MYSTERY_FREE_PROMPT` path that is not editable from this file. It tells the model: every mystery needs at least 4 characters; if the user says fewer than 4 will actually play ("only 2 people", "just me and my partner"), do not design, agree to, or list a smaller cast; explain kindly that it is a group deduction game and that with 2 players the innocent one would know who is guilty; offer to keep 4 characters and invite a couple more guests (video call is fine); if they still insist, say the tool cannot build one that small; never write a character list with fewer than 4 entries.

**Deployed:** `mystery-ai` v187, `verify_jwt` still `false`. Live source re-downloaded and equal to the repo. Before editing, confirmed live == repo; the only difference in the bundled shared parser was the (unused here) anomaly function. Syntax-checked with esbuild and ran `npm run test:roster` first.

**Tested live 2026-10-02, with Jonathan's explicit approval (3 real calls to `mystery-ai`, read-only: the function writes nothing to the database; estimated cost 15-25 cents, actual token usage not returned).** (1) The exact Trick, Treat, Dead opening ("4 players... only played by 2 people"): stated the 4-character minimum, did not agree to a smaller cast, offered 4 characters plus two more guests by video call, said plainly the tool is not built for 2 players. (2) Pressure turn ("just 2 of us, date night, make it a 2-character mystery"): held firm ("I can't build a 2-character (or even 3-character) mystery"), gave two options (invite two more, or save it for a bigger group), no cast list. (3) Control (normal 4-player 1920s speakeasy request): normal behavior, clarifying questions only, no new refusal. No reply contained a `Character List` header. Not tested: the "4 characters but each of us plays two" phrasing and non-English chats; the first real customer covers those. What to watch for: the model refusing too eagerly (for example someone who says "4 characters but only 2 of us are playing and we will each take two roles"; the guardrail tells it to offer options, not to refuse that) or still agreeing to a small cast. If it misfires, the lever is the wording of the guardrail in the same file.

**Not done:** no change to the setup form, which still lets a customer type any "additional details"; the guardrail is the only line of defense, which matches how the other product limits work.

## Addendum 76 (2026-10-02): New-Purchase sweep - "Paradis Perdu : Meurtre Au Resort Emeraude" (French, detective, 12 characters, no accomplice) - name fusion + leaked self-correction in the reveal; Parent71 guardrail did not fully hold; six more defects found by the full-cast read

**Trigger.** `needs_review` alert, `structuralDefects: reveal_name_fusion.murderer=Laura,victim_surname=Beaulieu,victim_full=Tommy Beaulieu`. Package `a4cf30d3-2a46-4e2a-8a6e-bcad32c4d408`, conversation `55235299-2ba2-433d-837a-81a466a6896b`, purchased 2026-10-02 11:36 UTC, after both the Parent72 (carries Parent71's guardrail) and Child49 imports.

**1. The alert did not self-heal, by design.** `package_reveal_name_fusion()` is detection-only (Addendum 70): the right edit depends on the sentence. The 5-minute held-package sweep only re-checks; it released the package within minutes of the manual fix. The sentence was "l'arrestation de Laura Beaulieu - pardon, de Laura - pour le meurtre de Tommy Beaulieu": the fusion, plus the model correcting itself inline. Fixed with an exact-match `replace()` to "l'arrestation de Laura pour". The widened self-correction meta-text detector (Addendum 71) did not catch it ("pardon, de" is not in its pattern); the fusion detector was the only thing that did.

**2. Parent71's NAME-FUSION GUARDRAIL did not fully prevent it.** This is the first detective-style generation after the import, and it recurred (4th confirmed occurrence overall, after 2026-07-28, 2026-09-08, 2026-09-30). Same field, same `## THE REVEAL` section, a new shape (self-correction instead of a clean fusion). Decided same day: not another hardening round, a one-sentence swap (see item 2a).

**2a. Parent73 (`temp-files/MM Live - Parent73 (Name-Fusion Construction Rule).blueprint.json`, built by `temp-files/build-parent-v73.py` from Parent72; both gitignored). Imported into Make.com by Jonathan, 2026-10-02.** One change, THE REVEAL prompt in all 4 routes: Parent71's guardrail is replaced by a construction rule. Hypothesis (one example, untested): Parent71's "re-check every sentence before finalizing" can only surface as visible self-correction in a one-pass output, which is exactly the "— pardon, de Laura —" seen here, and its example spelled out the fused tokens. The new rule drops the re-check and the example and keeps the murderer's and victim's names out of the same sentence in the closing arrest line. Verified structurally: only the 4 reveal texts differ from Parent72, old wording absent, new rule present once per route. **Not yet validated against a real generation**; the next detective-style purchase is the test, and `reveal_name_fusion` remains the safety net. Deliberately not done: widening the self-correction detector to "pardon" (noisy across 13 languages; the fusion detector already caught this case), a deterministic auto-fix (Addendum 70), and wiring the detector into the paid regenerate path (needs Jonathan's sign-off; revisit only if it recurs after Parent73).

**3. Full-cast read (12/12 characters, detective_script, evidence cards, master_context) found six more defects no detector covers.** Fixed in one script (`temp-files/paradis-perdu-fixes-2026-10-02.sql`, gitignored; applied by Jonathan in Supabase Studio after four MCP `execute_sql` writes were declined; verified live: leftover-string check 0, gate null, status `completed`):
- English section headers on 5 characters (Charles, Florence, Melyna, Rob, Thomas Lainess), Rob's including "When asked about your feelings toward la victime". Translated to the French wording the other 7 characters already used.
- Frederique: round/final headers in tu ("TON SCRIPT", "TA DECLARATION FINALE") against vous in his own introduction (the Addendum 13/56 class). Normalized to vous.
- Jacob: stray markdown backticks around quoted phrases (4 places). Stripped.
- Thomas Henry: hidden "divert suspicion" text said rivals' motives were stronger "que celui de Laura", an innocent referencing the murderer's motive. Changed to "que ceux des autres invites".
- Round 2 evidence card: "tenu par Tommy lui-meme comme journal personnel" contradicted the detective script ("Ce n'est pas l'ecriture de Tommy") and `namedItems` (Tommy's secret carnet was stolen, so a burned journal of his is incoherent); last sentence was garbled. Aligned the card to the detective script.
- Rob: secret said he confronted Tommy "bien apres le bal" while every other field has Tommy cutting ties right after it; moved to "peu avant" (secret and background).
- `game_overview`: "onze compagnons" vs twelve guests.

**4. First live read of Child49 (Addendum 73).** No innocent named the murderer or stated her private motive or concealed alibi. One residual: the Thomas Henry line above, which sits in the red-herring "divert suspicion" block, not obviously covered by Child49's node 409 clause. Sandrine's rumor about Laura holding a villa key is a legitimate visible observation and was left.

**5. Corrects Addendum 66's "confirmed one-off" on the English-header leak.** Corpus check (60 days, non-English characters detected by a pronoun heuristic): English headers on 39 characters across 6 packages: three fully German (Sep 1, 10, 18), three partial (El Zasca Final 1/11, I Dodici Sigilli 1/13, this one 5/12), the last three inside one week. Applying the Addendum 38 test: detection would need a package-language signal that is not stored, and the impact is cosmetic, so **no detector**; recorded so the "one-off" label is not relied on. Backticks: 1 character in 1 package, no detector.

**Left alone, deliberately.** (a) The detective's Round 3 and the evidence card put the poison in Tommy's rum reserve while the reveal, Laura's confession and `master_context` put it in his champagne coupe; the cast's own text mixes both, so fixing it means rewriting many fields; low severity. (b) Sandrine's round scripts have no `##` headers: 499 characters across 60 packages share that format, it is the normal variant. (c) Thomas Henry and Thomas Lainess have first-person descriptions/backgrounds while others are third-person; cosmetic.

**Process note.** Four MCP write attempts after the first (one 40-line UPDATE, then smaller ones) were declined; the SQL was handed over as a file instead, and a first-run error (`evidence_cards` is `jsonb`, not text) was fixed in the file before the successful run. Originals were backed up in the session scratchpad before any bulk change.

**Customer.** Drafted a short French note listing the fixes in plain terms; sent by Jonathan.

## Addendum 77 (2026-10-02): New-Purchase sweep - "Blood On The Mead-bench" (English, detective, 8 characters, no accomplice) - a `<FILL>` token in the murderer's confession, French pointform that will not converge (4th case), a false-positive `identity_conflict`, innocents leaking the reveal's last beat (Child49 did not hold), and the first documented use of the validation-trigger bypass

**Trigger.** `needs_review` alert on package `fe0a6738-503b-4bee-b82f-3de1ab327718`, conversation `6b337314-865d-406e-9191-d3683a79360c`, purchased 2026-10-02 14:58 UTC (after the Child49 import, 07:45 UTC). Gate defects: `pointform_language_mismatch` (Aelfgifu the Steward, Bjorn) and `identity_conflict.father` (Osric, Wulfstan). Detectors otherwise clean. `auto_remediation_log` showed the worker had already tried and hit its attempt cap: `empty_character_content` regen ($0.15, escalated), the `identity_contamination` delegate to `regenerate_child_content` (generation_error, reverted, $0.40), and two pointform regenerations (both "still flagged", $0.25). About $0.80 of paid regeneration logged; no paid calls were made in this session.

**1. French pointform on English prose, non-converging: 4th case** (after Marigold, Onszi, Natalie Maine-coon). Source prose for both characters verified English, `generate-pointform-summaries` (Sonnet 5, language instruction present, no language hint in the user prompt) returned French twice more, including invented words ("belebene", "medhu"). No root cause found, same as Addendum 63. Fixed by hand-writing English bullets for all 14 fields (7 x 2), the precedent from Addendum 63; no further API spend.

**2. `identity_conflict.father` is a false positive.** Osric ("the way my father taught me") and Wulfstan ("our father", about the victim's sibling) each refer to their own, different father; neither is cross-character contamination. Corpus check: this detector has flagged exactly two packages since 2026-06-01, the real July "brother" contamination and this one, so it is the first false positive in four months. **No detector change** (single occurrence, and a one-off patch to a security-style detector is the patch-chasing Addendum 31 warns against); recorded so a second occurrence is recognized.

**3. Release mechanism, documented for the first time.** A plain `UPDATE ... status = 'completed'` is undone: `trg_validate_package_characters` runs the gate on every transition into `completed` and re-holds the package while any defect remains. `heal_completed_packages()` (2-minute cron) and `promote_complete_packages()` only release when the gate returns NULL, and nothing demotes a package that is already `completed`. For a confirmed false positive the bypass is one transaction that disables only that trigger, flips the status guarded on "gate's sole remaining defect is exactly the known false positive", and re-enables the trigger before COMMIT (trigger changes are invisible to other sessions until commit, so no other package skips validation). `trg_z_notify_package_ready` stays active, so the customer's ready email sends on commit. Used here (ready email 2026-10-02 17:06:19 UTC). Side effects to expect: the failed first flip likely re-fired a `notify-generation-issue` alert, and because the gate still flags the package, `list_packages_with_identity_conflicts()` will keep listing it.

**4. A literal `<FILL>` token ended the murderer's `final_statement`** (the confession read aloud at the climax). First occurrence in the whole corpus (all guest-facing fields checked); not in the Child blueprint, so a model artifact. No detector caught it (`package_meta_text_leak` has `[insert `-style patterns but no `<TOKEN>` pattern). Stripped. **Detector built and applied the same day (Jonathan's go-ahead):** `package_meta_text_leak()` gained a case-sensitive `token_rx` = `<[A-Z][A-Z0-9_ ]{2,30}>`, tested with `~` (not `~*`) so lowercase markup like `<b>`/`<br>` never trips it. Migration `20261002180000_add_placeholder_token_to_meta_text_leak.sql`, applied via MCP `apply_migration` after diffing the live function against the repo (identical). The two field concatenations were moved into a LATERAL; verified equivalent to the prior definition by running old and new logic side by side over all 248 packages (0 mismatches, the same 8 flagged both ways), and corpus-validated first: zero angle-bracket tokens of any kind in 2,780 text units after the one known hit was repaired. Live checks: synthetic `<FILL>` flags, `<b>`/`<br>`/`<div>` do not, `[insert ` still flags, both recent packages' gates unchanged, advisory list still 0. It is the single source for both the gate and the advisory list, so nothing else changed. **Worker behaviour, checked rather than assumed:** `auto-remediate-packages`' free strip only removes lines matching its own artifact patterns, so a placeholder hit finds nothing to strip, returns no plan, and escalates; it never reaches the paid `regenerate-child-content` fallback. That is the intended outcome (a hand strip takes seconds), so no worker change or deploy.

**5. Child49 did not hold (second live read).** Bjorn, Wulfstan and Osric each referenced the Steward's corridor-and-jug alibi gap in Round 4, before the reveal (the reveal's own last clue), and Bjorn's hidden "deflect" block told him to steer toward it. Several innocents also point at the Steward by name in Rounds 2-3. With Addendum 76's Thomas Henry line, that is two live reads showing innocents still know the culprit's concealed detail. Either Child49 is not the scenario the production webhook runs (never confirmed, Addendum 73) or its INNOCENT IGNORANCE rule is too weak. **Confirmed live:** Jonathan confirmed Child49 is the running Make scenario, so the rule itself is too weak. **Root cause found (read from the Child49 blueprint and this package's `master_context`):** node 409's inputs are `{{142.master_context}}`, the character's description, and chat excerpts (no detective script). `master_context` carries the author's solution notes as named fields, `murdererFlaw` and `murdererAlibiGap` ('A guest recalls the Steward stepping briefly toward the stillroom corridor... dismisses as fetching a fresh jug, though no one actually saw a new jug arrive'), plus `whyOthersEliminated` and `murderMethod.*`. The leaked Round 4 lines paraphrase `murdererAlibiGap` almost word for word. Child49's INNOCENT IGNORANCE rule lets characters use 'what was publicly visible' and 'what players say aloud during the rounds'; because the notes phrase the gap as something a guest *recalls*, the model reads it as publicly known, so the rule's own exception is the loophole. Rumors (nodes 401/405, deliberately left alone in Addendum 73) show a related pattern here: four of seven innocents hint at the Steward's ledger shortfall, which is the murderer's private motive. **Built and imported (Jonathan, 2026-10-02): Child51.** Child50 (`MM Live - Child (Unified)50-SolutionNotesOffLimits`, built by `temp-files/build-child-v50.py`) was built first as node 409 only: it names the solution-note sections of master_context (the murderer-related parts of murdererSelection, murderMethod, motiveDistribution, timelineFramework and evidenceProgression, the murderer's own wrongedEachCharacter entry, and all of deceptionGuidelines, confessionGuidance, accompliceDetails and hostBriefing) as off-limits even when a note describes a guest recalling or witnessing something, uses the corridor/jug sighting as the worked example, drops 'what was publicly visible at the Festival' (a hardcoded Crown's Shadow leftover) and 'what players say aloud during the rounds', and restricts the character to their own history, background, relationships, position and alibi plus the evidence cards' own text. Jonathan then asked what including rumors would do. **Measured first:** across 72 detective packages in 90 days the murderer is a rumor target about 3.6 times per package against 2.3 for the average other character (about 1.6x; at least 2x in 25 of 72), an upper bound on leakage because hostile relationships also drive targeting. **Key finding:** node 405 generates both `rumors` and `accusations`, and Bjorn's leaked hidden 'deflect suspicion' block is an `accusations` field, so Child50 alone would never have reached it. **Child51** (`MM Live - Child (Unified)51-RumorsSolutionNotesOffLimits`, `temp-files/build-child-v51.py`, built on Child50) adds the same ban to node 405 with a rumor-specific clause: rumors and accusations may still target any eligible character including the murderer, and may be wrong, petty or malicious, but may rest only on what anyone at the table could observe, never on a hidden motive, movement or secret that appears only in the solution notes. Child51 vs Child50 differs in exactly one field (node 405 text), vs Child49 in exactly two (405 and 409); slip-style nodes 5xx deliberately unchanged (their culprit is drawn at the table, not in master_context). Jonathan imported **Child51 only** (it contains both changes; Child50 was never imported). **Not yet validated against a real generation**: the next detective-style purchase is the test. Check innocents' rumors and hidden accusation blocks for the murderer's secret, skimmed money, hidden movements or the alibi gap; rumors targeting the murderer from visible behaviour are fine and expected. Known trade-off: dropping the 'what players say aloud' allowance means innocents react less to each other and more to their own situation and the evidence. Fixed in this package by hand (three prose passages, Bjorn's deflect bullet, and the matching pointform lines).

**6. Low-severity polish, fixed.** Hild: Round 3 admits walking past the stillroom, Round 4/final said she "did not go near" it, now "into". Ragnar/Freydis: Ragnar said he stayed "beside" Freydis while she performed at the far end; he is now on the back benches in front of her. Osric: stopped naming the Steward (his listed ally) in Round 3.

**Left alone.** Wulfstan calls Cynewise a "young cousin" while Cynewise calls herself the thegn's child (cast-wide kinship muddle; fixing it means rewriting relationship text across sheets). Osric's rumor about the Steward's ledgers (reads as ordinary gossip). Slashed dual-gender names/pronouns (by design).

**Process.** All fixes delivered as SQL files in `temp-files/` (gitignored: fixes, polish, release) and run by Jonathan in Supabase Studio. First run failed on `multiple assignments to same column "round4_script"` (my generator emitted two assignments for one column; merged into a nested `replace()` and every UPDATE re-checked for duplicates). Originals were not separately backed up this time because every fix is an exact-match `replace()` or a full-field set from the pointform regeneration, and the pre-fix text is in `auto_remediation_log.before_value` for the reverted attempt and this ADR.

**Health check.** `.github/workflows/health-check.yml` runs every 6 hours with a 30-day window and raises a red line for any `identity_conflict` hit, so this false positive would have nagged for a month. Added the same by-id exclusion the Bollinger package already has (both jq expressions, comment explains why, YAML re-validated), so nobody has to remember it. The exclusion should be removed if the detector is ever tightened.

**Customer.** The standard ready email went out automatically on release (17:06:19 UTC); Jonathan sent the follow-up note listing the fixes.

- No code or migration change.


## Addendum 78 (2026-10-03): New-Purchase sweep - "El Último Brindis De Laia" (Spanish, slip style, 10 characters) - the English token "until" looping through confessions, stray markup, a who-read-whose-cards inversion, a self-naming accomplice confession, vosotros/ustedes drift; two detector extensions shipped; a 10-package backlog found

**Package.** Conversation `e6c75429-ff85-4e56-bf45-06ea1c70ccd9`, package `32ec66a4-0ed3-4068-a10f-867ce852e45a`, USD 19.99, purchased and delivered 2026-10-02 (ready email 21:24 UTC), so every fix below went live to a customer who already had the package. Post-ADR-0074, post-v44. `mystery_style='character'`, `has_accomplice=false` on the form, but the approved concept chat names **Jordi as murderer and Edu as accomplice** (see "Left alone").

**What the standing detectors said.** All six scoped detectors and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` returned clean. Every real defect below was found by reading all 10 characters in full (every field that matters, plus a programmatic scan of all 52 text columns), which is the Addendum 56 full-cast read paying for itself again.

**1. Stray-token glitches (new bug class, fixed + detector).**
- The English word `until` appears mid-sentence in 6 fields across 5 characters, sometimes as a loop (Dani Zhou's guilty confession ends `por eso until until until until until until until until until until until acabé quitándole la suya`; Claudia has 3 in a row), sometimes singly where a Spanish word belongs (`No until que vi su cara...`). The 3 cases that were singles are fixed by rewriting the clause; the loops by deleting the run.
- Also: `</final>` closing Ignasi's final statement, `</document>` closing Aleix's, a ``` code fence closing Jordi's guilty confession, a Cyrillic `алgo` in Ramón's round 3, English `more` (Claudia) and `last` (Laura) inside Spanish sentences, a trailing curly `”` on Edu's accomplice confession, `ultimamente`/`duerve` typos, and four Aleix innocent-branch fields (round 2/3/4 and final) with no `## RONDA n` / `**SI ERES INOCENTE**` header at all.
- **Corpus check (done before calling it a one-off, per the standing lesson):** the `until` class is live in **3 earlier delivered Spanish packages** (El Último Brindis De Bellanotte 5 characters, Veneno En La Medianoche 4, El Zasca Final 2; the Zasca ones were in a package swept on 2026-09-26 and missed because no detector covered it) and the Cyrillic class in 2 more (Costa Del Karaoke, Villa Limoncello). Spanish-only so far, which fits a Sonnet 5 repetition artefact on Spanish prose, not a prompt gap, so there is no prompt fix to make.
- **Detector (Addendum 38 test: cheap + deterministic, severe on a read-aloud confession, so built now):** migration `20261003100000_add_stray_token_patterns_to_meta_text_leak.sql` extends `package_meta_text_leak()`, the single source for `package_completion_blocking_defects()` and `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()`, with (a) case-sensitive closing tags `</[a-z]{2,20}>`, code fences and any Cyrillic letter and (b) `until` only when the same text unit has no common English function word and does have a common foreign one (so English packages and mixed English/Spanish titles are untouched). Verified by creating the new body under a temp name and comparing it with the live one over **every package**: 0 hits removed, exactly 5 added, all real (Bellanotte, Veneno, Zasca, Costa Del Karaoke, Villa Limoncello), 0 false positives; the temp function was dropped. The worker's free strip does not match these patterns, so a hit escalates (free, no paid regeneration).
- **Stray quote:** migration `20261003100100_dangling_quote_mark_also_catches_double_quotes.sql` extends `package_dangling_quote_mark()` (Addendum 55) to a trailing straight or curly **double** quote with no opening double quote anywhere in the text. The original single-quote branch is kept verbatim (strictly additive). Corpus: 6 delivered packages carry the same shape (5 inside the 2026-04-01 window; Windsor Christmas Masquerade, 2025-07, is outside it). Edu's `”` was the only one in this package and post-dates v44.

**2. Cross-field contradiction: who read whose cards (fixed).** `master_context`, the customer's concept and Aida's own secret/introduction all say **Aida read Laia's tarot** and Laia tied threads from it. Aida's round 2 innocent, round 2 guilty, guilty reveal confession and final innocent had it inverted ("Laia me leyó las cartas", and a garbled "Lo que até cabos esa noche con las cartas"), and two pointform bullets copied the inversion. Rewritten to match; her accomplice round 2 had an ambiguous subject ("sacó las cartas") fixed to "saqué".

**3. Self-naming accomplice confession (fixed).** In this package every accomplice confession is written around Jordi as the killer, which matches the customer's approved concept and the ADR-0125 pattern (accomplice branches slot into the canonical killer scenario). Jordi's own accomplice confession therefore said "Sabía lo que Jordi pensaba hacer" and "como hizo con Jordi" about himself (the Addendum 45 shape, which `narration_person_mismatch` does not see because it is a third-person self-reference, not a person-switch). Rewritten with "esa persona" in the text and 5 matching pointform bullets. Dani Zhou's pointform also said Laia "la amenazaba" (Dani is male); fixed.

**4. Detective script (fixed).** The opening said Laia "sopló las velas" "hace apenas unas horas", but `master_context`, the overview and every character have her collapse mid-"cumpleaños feliz", minutes after the toast, before any candles. Now "hace apenas unos minutos, ... en mitad de su propia canción de cumpleaños".

**5. Register drift (fixed).** Nine fields over four characters slid from vosotros into formal ustedes: Ignasi (6 fields, 8 spots: "Sigan mirando", "créanme", "decirles", "no se los voy a negar"), Aida's introduction (`ustedes imaginen`, `como todos ustedes`), Edu ("de verdad queréis ..., miren" in one sentence), Jordi ("Miren a su alrededor"). Normalised to vosotros, the register each character otherwise uses. The detective's own voice is consistent (ustedes throughout). **I did not check whether Child45/Parent70 (the Addendum 56 register fix) are imported;** if they are, this is a live miss and worth a fresh look.

**Applied.** One atomic `DO` block of 49 exact-match edits (each must match exactly one row or the whole thing aborts) via `supabase db query --linked`, `temp-files/el-ultimo-brindis-de-laia-sweep-2026-10-03.sql` (gitignored). Only text columns changed; no status change, so no ready-email trigger fired. Pre-fix text is in the session's row export and in this addendum. Re-verified: all six detectors, the blocking-defects function, the new patterns and a full re-scan of every text column are clean; the 35 changed fields were re-scanned, and the Aleix header inserts and Jordi's rewritten confession were read back.

**Left alone, on purpose.**
- All 10 accomplice confessions name Jordi as the killer and Jordi's and Aleix's guilty confessions name Edu as the accomplice. This matches the customer's approved concept (Jordi + Edu) but `master_context.accomplicePairings` pairs every character with someone else, and the game is slip style (killer drawn at the table). If the table draws a different pair the confessions will not line up. Pre-existing product behaviour (ADR-0125), not a defect in this package; noted because the customer explicitly chose a killer.
- Relationship text vs matrix: Jordi lists Aleix and Claudia as allies (matrix: Hostile, Neutral); Ramón lists Edu as an ally (matrix: Hostile; Edu lists Ramón as a rival); Claudia lists Ignasi as both ally and rival; Aida has a duplicate Jordi entry; Sergi lists Laura as an ally (Neutral). A garbled sentence in Laura's round 3 innocent ("ninguna de ellas era yo sola la que tenía las manos libres") and the first-person/second-person header drift ("SI SOY/SI ERES CULPABLE") on Dani and Ramón's guilty branches. All low severity.
- Evidence cards, `host_guide`/`timeline` (null, localized client-side) and `master_context` cross-checked against the detective script: consistent.

**Backlog repaired (same day, Jonathan's call: everything in the last couple of months).** 20 exact-match edits in one atomic transaction across 9 packages via `supabase db query --linked` (`temp-files/stray-token-backlog-repair-2026-10-03.sql`, gitignored): `until` removed or the clause rewritten in Bellanotte (Amelia 20x, Filemón, Fulgencio, Marquesa Hortensia, Tristán), Veneno En La Medianoche (Arlett x2, Celestina, Federico, Isadora; one was a half-English clause) and El Zasca Final (Fidel, Rose); Cyrillic letters inside `baropstelling` (Villa Limoncello) and `karaoke` (Costa Del Karaoke); one stray trailing double quote each in Champagne & Crocodile Tears, Multiverse Gala, The Final Cut, Coral Cove and Costa Del Karaoke. Two of the `until` repairs are inferences (Federico: `no until la confusión` became `no aproveché la confusión`; Tristán: `nunca until esta noche` became `nunca, hasta esta noche,`). The first context pull showed only the first match per field, so Celestina's second `until until until` was caught by the post-repair detector run and fixed separately. Verified after: `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak` and `list_packages_with_dangling_quote_mark` return 0 since 2026-04-01 and a scan of every character column of every package since 2026-08-01 finds no `until`/Cyrillic. Not touched: the 2025-07 Windsor Christmas Masquerade stray quote (outside the window) and 8 older packages the meta detector lists only when the window is widened past 2026-04-01 (older, pre-existing patterns).

**Relationship text vs matrix (measured after Jonathan asked; first numbers corrected twice the same day).** The Child prompt tells the model allies must be `Friendly` and rivals `Hostile` per `master_context.relationshipMatrix`, but nothing enforced it and no detector checked it. Final measurement, by the new detector over the 103 packages completed since 2026-08-01 (1,238 characters): **44 packages (43%), 100 characters (8%) list a Hostile character as an ally or a Friendly one as a rival**; about a third of characters list a Neutral one. (Two earlier figures in this addendum, 160 of 434 characters and then 43 characters in 29 packages, came from throw-away scripts that mis-split non-English headers or skipped characters they could not name-match; the detector covers every character.) Root cause is the prompt: it demands 2-3 ally entries that "must be Friendly" and 2-3 rivals that "must be Hostile", but 43% of matrix rows have fewer than 2 Friendly cells and 70% fewer than 2 Hostile, so the model pads. Decision, plan and the Child52 fix: **ADR-0134**.

**Post-import watch.** The Parent73/Child51 test was **not** exercised: this is a slip-style package (Parent73 is the detective closing line; Child51's solution-notes rule concerns a fixed culprit, and the slip nodes were deliberately unchanged). Nothing in the innocents' rumors or accusations hinted at a hidden motive beyond the shared rumor targets, but that is not a clean test. The watch continues for the next detective-style purchase.
