**Update 3 (2026-10-07, the detector timeout fixed and made non-fatal).** Reproduced and root-caused the `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak` statement timeout from Update 2; both halves of the fix shipped.
- **Measured cause.** The worker's 30-day call took 7.9 s against the 8 s `statement_timeout` that `service_role` inherits through PostgREST (`authenticator` carries `statement_timeout=8s`; `service_role` has no setting of its own), so it died whenever the database was slightly slower. Two things made it slow: (1) the function called `package_meta_text_leak(mp.*)` twice per package (SELECT list and `WHERE ... IS NOT NULL`), and (2) inside it the word-loop pattern `\m(\w{2,})\M(\s+\1\M){4,}` uses a backreference and costs about 5.7 s over the 30-day character text (isolated measurement), paid only for packages under 7 days old, so a busy week makes the detector slower, which matches the three failures after a day of many orders. The other patterns cost 50 to 100 ms each; the main alternation about 2 s on characters. No index helps (the work is regex over text; the row scan is 57 packages).
- **Fix 1 (SQL, `supabase/migrations/20261007120000_meta_text_leak_detector_evaluate_once.sql`, applied with `apply_migration`).** The detector evaluates `package_meta_text_leak` once per package through `CROSS JOIN LATERAL (SELECT ... OFFSET 0)`; `OFFSET 0` stops the planner from inlining the call back into both places. Signature, columns, filter, window, ordering, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path` and the `service_role`-only grant are unchanged. **Result: 7.9 s to 3.9 to 4.8 s on the same window (noisy; roughly half). That is headroom, not a guarantee: cost still grows with the number of packages under 7 days old and with the 30-day window.** Further cuts were not taken because they change what the detector sees (a shorter loop window or a pre-filter) or need a stored per-package result; revisit if it climbs back above about 6 s.
- **Equivalence evidence.** Old and new definitions run side by side on live data for the 30-day window: identical rows, 0 only in either direction (1 hit, "El Último Trago", 4 character sources). The new live function over the default window (since 2026-04-01) hashes to the same value as the pre-change output (md5 `bcf0ce5acd58af19e01aa245698d4855` over `package_id` plus sorted sources). `package_meta_text_leak` itself was not touched, so per-package output is identical by construction; the weak point of this proof is that only one package currently hits, so it shows the filter and ordering are unchanged more than it exercises many hit shapes.
- **Fix 2 (worker, `auto-remediate-packages` v26, deployed with the CLI, `verify_jwt` stays true).** `supabase/functions/auto-remediate-packages/detector-guard.ts`: each class sweep now goes through `sweep(rpc)`, which runs the detector (and the needs-review filter) inside `runDetectorGuarded`; a failure is recorded, logged (`auto-remediate detector skipped: <rpc>: <message>`) and that class returns no rows, and the run continues with the other classes. The response gains `detector_failures`, and the log line gains the count. One alert email per run (Resend, same pattern as `rescue-unstarted-orders`, best effort, not sent on `dry_run`) lists every failed detector. **Deliberately NOT guarded:** the two re-detect gates (`stillFlagged` and the confession-class re-check). "Could not check" must never read as "no longer flagged", so those keep throwing and the fix is reverted and escalated as before. Per-row handler errors are unchanged (still fatal to the run), out of scope here.
- **Alert noise trade-off.** A detector that is down for good would email every 30 minutes; accepted because a silent skip is the worse failure and the run no longer dies, so it is visible but harmless. If it ever becomes noisy, dedupe by `rpc` like `alert-dedupe.ts`.
- **Test.** `scripts/__tests__/detectorGuard.test.mjs` (added to `npm run test:roster`, un-ignored in `.gitignore`): a timeout is recorded and returns no rows, later detectors still run, one alert body per run, and `index.ts` has only the two re-detect `callDetector` calls outside `sweep`. No Deno available locally for a type check; the CLI bundle succeeded and the live check is the next cron run.
- **Spend.** 0 USD: no Anthropic, review or regenerate call; the only calls were SQL and the deploy.
- **Live confirmation (07:43 UTC, first full cron run on v26).** `auto-remediate: 0 fixed, 1 escalated, 0 failed, 2 skipped, 0 detector failures, $0 spent`, cron `succeeded`; the 07:40 held-package run on v26 was also clean. A fourth timeout hit the OLD code at 07:30 UTC ("auto-remediate fatal: ... statement timeout"), before the deploy; no `fatal` line since. `auto_remediation_log` has 0 new rows and 0 USD after the deploy. Not yet seen live: a detector actually failing (the skip-and-alert path is covered by the offline test only).

**Update 4 (2026-10-07, same day: Update 3 was not enough margin; word-loop regex made cheaper).** Jonathan received the new alert email at 10:13 CEST (08:13 UTC): `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak ... statement timeout`. The Update 3 worker guard did its job (run continued, 1 detector failure, one email; the 08:00 run also skipped it), but the detector itself still timed out intermittently: after the evaluate-once fix it measured 3.9 s, then 6.5 s at 08:42, against the 8 s limit. So Update 3's "roughly half" was real but load-sensitive (shared CPU), not enough headroom.
- **What was left.** About 2 to 2.5 s of the call is the word-loop regex over packages under 7 days old, which a narrower window cannot shrink (7-day window alone: 2.65 s). Timing the pattern variants on the last 7 days of character text: original `\m(\w{2,})\M(\s+\1\M){4,}` 1.5 s; `(?:\s+\1\M){4}` 0.39 s; fully unrolled 0.31 s; all 3 hits.
- **Fix (`supabase/migrations/20261007140000_meta_text_leak_loop_regex_cheaper.sql`, applied).** In `package_meta_text_leak`, `(\s+\1\M){4,}` becomes `(?:\s+\1\M){4}`, by exact-text replacement on the live definition with an assert of exactly one occurrence (same approach as `20261004140000_meta_text_leak_closing_label.sql`). Only that function carries the pattern (checked across all public functions). It is only used as a boolean `~*`, and "4 or more repeats" matches exactly the texts "4 repeats" matches, so results are identical.
- **Equivalence evidence (stronger than Update 3's).** Patched copy vs live function over ALL 259 packages: 0 differing, 9 packages hitting (not just the one current hit). Detector over the default window still hashes to the pre-change md5 `bcf0ce5acd58af19e01aa245698d4855`. Grants unchanged (`service_role` only).
- **Result.** The 30-day detector call is a steady 2.2 s (runs: 2277, 2221, 2225, 2229 ms), against 7.9 s originally and the 8 s limit. Most of what remains is the main alternation `rx` on character text (about 2 s); cutting it further would need a stored per-package result or a narrower window, neither taken.
- **Not touched, next closest:** `list_packages_with_pointform_language_mismatch` takes 4.9 s on the same window (others: 1.9 s identity conflicts, 1.7 s self-directed questions, all else under 1 s). Not changed because it has not failed; if it appears in a detector-failure email, narrow its worker window first (7 days measured 1.2 s) or apply the same kind of per-pattern timing.
- **No worker change, no deploy.** v26 stands. 0 USD.

**Update 5 (2026-10-07, same day: still failing after Update 4; held run narrowed, one retry on timeout).** A third alert email arrived at 10:50 CEST (08:50 UTC): the same `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak` statement timeout, **four minutes after Update 4's migration was applied (08:46:09 UTC)**. So Update 4 did not by itself end it. Failures so far, all on the same detector: 01:45, 07:05, 07:13 (before any fix), 07:30 (old code, fatal), 08:00 and 08:13 (after Update 3, guard worked), 08:50 (after Update 4, guard worked). Runs between them were clean.
- **What the evidence says, and what it does not.** Alone, the 30-day call is a steady 2.2 s (Update 4); the timeout needs 8 s, so something makes it about 4x slower at the moments it fails. At 08:50:00 ten cron jobs fire in the same second (`heal_completed_packages` and `sweep_incomplete_packages` 1.7 s each, `auto-remediate-held-packages`, two review jobs, `rescue-unstarted-orders`, three stuck-package sweeps); `:00` is similar and `:13` is the full run. `pg_stat_statements` shows `heal_completed_packages()` and `sweep_incomplete_packages()` with a mean of about 240 ms but a max of about 6.3 s (they also run the leak scan per package), and PostgREST calls with a max of 7.98 s, which fits "same work, slower under concurrency". **That is a strong correlation, not a measured proof: I did not capture the failing moment itself.** The failing 08:50 call was the 5-minute held run (it fails 12 s after the cron tick, i.e. on its first detector, `template_artifact`).
- **Why the held run is the lever.** `auto-remediate-held-packages` runs every 5 minutes (288 times a day) and called six detectors (`identity_contamination`, `slip_culprit_leak`, `template_artifact`, `missing_role_branch_content`, `pointform_language_mismatch`, `narration_person_mismatch`) over the full 30-day window, only to throw away every row that is not a `needs_review` package. Measured alone on the 30-day window those six cost roughly 1.9 + 0.05 + 2.2 + 0.05 + 4.9 + 0.9 s, about 10 s of database CPU every five minutes, the largest recurring load of the detectors and the one that lines up with the other cron jobs.
- **Fix 1 (worker, `auto-remediate-packages` v27).** `runWindowDays` in `detector-guard.ts`: when `only_needs_review` is set the window is at most 7 days (`HELD_RUN_WINDOW_DAYS`); an explicit narrower `window_days` still narrows, an explicit `backfill_days` still wins, and the half-hourly full run keeps 30 days. A held package older than 7 days is therefore only picked up by the full run, not by the 5-minute one (measured on the 7-day window: meta-text-leak 2.65 s before Update 4, pointform 1.25 s; 18 packages instead of 57). The re-detect gate uses the same window as the sweep, so a fix and its check stay consistent.
- **Fix 2 (worker).** `runDetectorGuarded` retries once, and only for a message containing "statement timeout", before skipping the class and counting the failure. Other errors are not retried, and the re-detect gates are still unguarded on purpose. The retry costs up to one more detector call in the rare case; the function has 280 to 300 s.
- **Test.** `scripts/__tests__/detectorGuard.test.mjs` now 10 checks: retry then recover records nothing, two timeouts record once after exactly two attempts, a non-timeout error is not retried, and the window rules (full 30, held 7, narrower request wins, backfill wins).
- **Not done, and why.** No change to `heal_completed_packages`/`sweep_incomplete_packages` or the cron minute offsets (the other contenders; staggering is the next lever if the alert recurs, and moving the held job off `:00/:10/:50` is a one-line cron change). No SQL change in this update. If an alert arrives after v27, the next step is to capture the slow moment (`pg_stat_activity` snapshot while the full run executes) rather than guess again.
- **Spend.** 0 USD.

### Addendum 88 (2026-10-08): "Death And Dumplings At Madwimmin House" - first live purchase on Parent80/Child60, `master_context` empty again, 0 of 5 characters (cause NOT settled)

**Package** `67c9afa5-a3cb-467f-9196-3d28afe4c8cf`, conversation `58188c80-d99a-4b10-8008-a5e3611060bb`, slip style (`character`), 5 players, no accomplice, point-form, English, paid 19.99 on 2026-10-07 21:03 UTC (Stripe payer alinalynfox@gmail.com, account chavi.l.fox@gmail.com). Held in `needs_review`, no ready email sent (`ready_email_sent_at` null), customer saw the "team has been notified" message.

**Evidence (all read-only, 0 USD):**
- The input was fine: the approved message (`fe927e57`, 4,507 chars, a full concept with a header) plus 2 later messages = 5,467 chars reached the Parent (`mystery-webhook-trigger` log, webhook 200 "Accepted"). This is NOT the Montero delta-concept cause.
- Two Parent80 executions, both `status 1` (success), 39 operations, about 320 s each: 21:04:06 UTC (`850980d931734f36803661f64b158bed`, the purchase) and 22:53:25 UTC (`18a3c4a55da342cfb5ea6ecd1be4617e`, the customer's retry, `generation_attempts` `claimed`). Both ended with `master_context` '' (0 chars), `game_overview` = the AI request "The master_context for this mystery arrived empty" (525 chars), and 0 `mystery_characters` rows. `detective_script` (8,291 chars) and `evidence_cards` WERE saved, so the `textResponse` read works for modules 5004 and 15118; they are generic (no victim or cast names), consistent with having been generated against an empty master context. The live blueprint (fetched through the Make API) is identical to `temp-files/MM Live - Parent80 (Read textResponse).blueprint.json` (100 modules, no mapper differences).
- `master_context` is saved by module 158 as `{{165.textResponse}}{{4010.textResponse}}` (Master Doc Part 1 and Part 2, `max_tokens` 32000, Resume error handler on both). 4010's prompt and 15118's prompt also embed `{{165.textResponse}}`. For the field to be '' both modules returned an empty `textResponse`.
- The Child scenario ran 5 times per attempt with 7 operations and about 16 s each (healthy: 30 operations, about 150 s) and wrote no character rows. The 10-minute and 20-minute `auto-remediate` rounds each escalated 5 `regenerate_character` calls (0.15 USD each, outcome `escalated`, cap reached).
- The same two modules read `content[].text` in Parent79 and produced a 51,637-char `master_context` for Montero Manor at 06:37 UTC on 2026-10-07. Parent80 (imported 07:27 UTC) changed only the read expression. These two executions are the first and second runs since the import.
- The gate did its job: `package_completion_blocking_defects` returns `missing_core_content.game_overview_is_ai_request` and `meta_text_leak.package`; `list_packages_with_missing_core_content` lists it. Nothing was released and no email went out. A `master_context` that is empty on its own is still not a gate condition (it is only caught through the overview text).

**Two explanations fit and they cannot be told apart from here:** (a) `textResponse` comes back empty for 165 and 4010 (the two 32,000-token modules) although the model answered; (b) both calls failed (HTTP 400, content filter, timeout) and the Resume handler returned its empty bundle, which reports `status 1`. Make's API exposes only the execution summary, not module output, so the deciding evidence is the module 165 and 4010 output of execution `850980d9...` in the Make UI (`stop_reason`, content block types, `usage.output_tokens`, any error bundle). Per Addendum 87 and ADR-0146: ask for the module output before theorising, and do not reword.

**Not done, and why:** no re-fire (paid, needs Jonathan's yes, and a third run on the same Parent80 would likely repeat); no Parent81 (a blueprint change built before the module output is seen would be a guess, and ADR-0146 warns that a wrong field name reads empty silently); no customer message sent.

**Side finding fixed in this session:** commit `a2dbfce` had truncated this ADR (554 KB to 2.5 KB, 1,851 lines deleted) and `8ab5a5f` built on the fragment; restored in `840b9c9` from `5c57802` plus Updates 4 and 5, byte-identical to the vault copy. A scan of the last 60 commits touching `docs/` and `CHANGELOG.md` found no other large deletion.

**Update 1 (2026-10-08, cause found from Jonathan's Make execution view).** Module 158 input shows `master_context: ""`. Module 165 output: `content[0]` is `type: text` with the complete Part 1 JSON, thinking tokens 0, about 8,000 output tokens, `stop_reason` empty, no error; module 4010 is the same. So both calls succeeded and `{{165.textResponse}}{{4010.textResponse}}` resolved blank: explanation (a) is right, explanation (b) (failed calls, content filter, timeout) is ruled out, and Sonnet 5.5 itself is not at fault (the answer is complete and consistent). `textResponse` resolved correctly for 5004 and 15118 in the same run, so it is unreliable per module, not globally; why is unproven (the empty `stop_reason` hints that the 32,000-`max_tokens` calls take a different path in the Make module). Parent79's `content[].text` read worked for 165/4010 (no thinking block), and failed for 5004 when a thinking block came first, so each read fails in a different case.
- **Fix (ADR-0147, built, not imported).** Parent81/Child61: every read is `{{ifempty(N.textResponse; ifempty(N.content[].text; N.content[2].text))}}` (84 + 8 reads, path-level diff verified).
- **Package still broken and still held.** The overview, materials, detective script and evidence cards (and the evidence images) were all generated against an empty master context, so the repair is a whole-package re-fire after Parent81 is imported (paid, needs Jonathan's yes). Not done: any partial repair, any customer message.
- **Not built yet:** a Make-side stop after module 158 on an empty `master_context`, and a DB gate condition for it. `master_context = ''` was only caught indirectly through the overview text.

**Update 2 (2026-10-08, re-fired on Parent81/Child61; sweep complete).** Re-fire 07:02:48 UTC (Jonathan: "OK imported let's refire"), generation 07:02:51 to 07:08:09, ready email 07:14:38 UTC. The fix held (ADR-0147 Addendum 1).
- **Held once, healed free.** The gate held it on `dangling_quote_mark.final_innocent:Clarice/Claris Reyes` (a trailing `'`); `auto-remediate` stripped it at 07:13:09 (0 USD). This is a live hit AFTER blueprint v44, so the v44 prompt fix did not fully land; one sighting, the heal covers it, no new work, watch for a second.
- **Reviewer (0.2346 USD):** 3 findings, all applied before release: Toni description "I mean," (real), Clarice `final_guilty` "honest about what I can be" (real), Toni `round4_guilty` word order (debatable). All three read correctly in context after the edit.
- **Full-cast read (5 characters, every branch incl. guilty, rumors, accusations, confession, detective script, overview, materials, evidence cards, pointforms present and English):** one miss by hand, Mo/Moe accusations tip "Share the chore wheel and the pantry door." (garbled, fixed to "Downplay", exact-match edit, 1 row). Wax, thermos tape, stubby pencil are all canonical (in `master_context` and the evidence cards), not invented. Relationship matrix contradiction NULL; the slip, role-tag, victim, meta-text and missing-core-content detectors all clean; reveal and closing name no one (Parent76 held); confessions name no other cast member. `human_verdict` recorded (2 real, 1 debatable), miss and sweep rows inserted, scoreboard row added.
- **Not exercised:** non-English, accomplice, detective style (Parent73/Child51/Parent77/Child58 items), gender-neutral victim.

**Update 3 (2026-10-08, follow-ups shipped, ADR-0148).** Jonathan chose the database-side guards over a Make-side stop (Parent82 declined, reasons in ADR-0148). Shipped: the gate check `missing_core_content.master_context_empty` (new packages only, 0 existing packages newly flagged) and the recovery guard in `notify-generation-issue` v42 (no character re-fires without a usable `master_context`, one 0 USD log row, immediate alert). The 10 futile re-fires on this package (1.50 USD logged) are the case it prevents. Customer note sent by Jonathan the same day. Sweep checklist impact: step 3 now also expects `missing_core_content.master_context_empty` to stay empty.

**Update 6 (2026-10-13, Update 5 reduced but did not end the timeouts; the two slowest detectors capped at 14 days).** Re-checked six days later from the function logs (last 24 h, 300 runs): 0 fatals, but `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak` timed out 11 times, the Update 5 retry recovered 8, and 3 runs still skipped a detector (02:43 and 06:35 UTC on 10-13 for meta-text-leak, 13:14 on 10-12 for `list_packages_with_pointform_language_mismatch`, the 4.9 s detector flagged in Update 4). About 3 alert emails a day. So Update 5 made the run robust, which is what the guard was for, but not quiet; the 35 clean minutes recorded on 10-07 was too short a sample to call it fixed. Plan chosen with Jonathan: narrow the scan, since cost scales with the number of packages scanned.
- **Change (worker `auto-remediate-packages` v28, no SQL).** `detectorSinceIso` in `detector-guard.ts`, applied inside `callDetector`: for exactly two RPCs, `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak` and `list_packages_with_pointform_language_mismatch`, the window is capped at 14 days (from 30). Every other detector keeps its window, the held run's 7 days is already narrower, and a window wider than the standard one (an explicit `backfill_days`) is left alone. The re-detect gates go through the same `callDetector`, so a fix and its check use the same window.
- **Trade-off.** A meta-text leak or pointform language mismatch in a package older than 14 days is no longer auto-healed. Those packages were already seen by the new-purchase sweep, the reviewer-before-release pipeline and the 14 days of runs before it (48 runs a day), so the lost coverage is for something that survived all of that. An old package with such a leak found by hand is repaired by hand or with `backfill_days`.
- **Expected effect, not yet measured.** Packages scanned fall from 57 to roughly 30 for these two (about half the work). The honest limit: this halves the load, it does not remove the contention that makes a 2.2 s query exceed 8 s. If skips continue, the next steps in order are: a `pg_stat_activity` snapshot during a full run, then staggering the cron minutes (ten jobs fire at :00/:10/:50), then a stored per-package verdict so unchanged packages are not rescanned.
- **How to check later (free):** `select count(*), countIf(event_message like '%detector skipped%'), countIf(event_message like '%timed out, retrying%') from logs where source='function_logs' and event_message like '%auto-remediate%'` over 24 h. Baseline before this change: 300 runs, 11 retries, 3 skips.
- **Test.** `detectorGuard.test.mjs` (11 checks): the two heavy RPCs capped at 14 days, other RPCs untouched, a narrower window kept, a backfill kept, a garbage timestamp passed through. Spend 0 USD.

**Update 7 (2026-10-14, the 14-day cap did not help; the email now fires only when one detector keeps failing).** Jonathan came back from a long weekend to about a dozen identical "detector failed" emails and asked whether they still mattered. Logs for the roughly 26 hours since Update 6 (v28): about 340 runs, 0 fatals, 15 timeout retries (before: 11), 6 skipped detectors in 3 clusters (before: 3 skips), so about 3 emails a day, unchanged. The skips were no longer limited to the two capped detectors: `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak` (3), `list_packages_with_pointform_language_mismatch`, `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch` and `list_packages_with_self_directed_questions` (1 each), three of them within two minutes at 07:43 to 07:45 UTC. That points at contention at the shared cron minutes, not at one slow query, which is the limit Update 6 already named. Nothing was actually wrong: no fatals, the other classes ran, the only cost is a free heal delayed by up to about 30 minutes.
- **Decision (Jonathan: "yes let's do that"):** stop emailing on a single skipped detector. Staggering the cron minutes (the Update 6 next step) was offered and not chosen now; it stays the fix if skips ever need to go down rather than just go quiet.
- **Change (worker `auto-remediate-packages` v36, deployed with the CLI, `verify_jwt` true confirmed; migration `20261014200000_detector_failure_log.sql`, applied by the MCP).** Each skip is written to the new `detector_failure_log` table (`rpc`, `message`, `alerted`, `created_at`; RLS on, no policies, service role only). An email goes out only when the SAME detector has failed 4 or more times in 3 hours, and at most once per detector per 6 hours (rows are marked `alerted`). Rows older than 7 days are deleted on each run. If the bookkeeping itself fails, the worker falls back to emailing on every skip rather than hiding a problem. A dry run writes and sends nothing. `detector_failures` in the response and the log line are unchanged, so skips stay visible in the logs.
- **Not changed:** the retry, the 14-day cap, the re-detect gates (still unguarded on purpose), and the fatal path (`auto-remediate fatal` still returns 500 with no email; the cron shows it as failed).
- **Test.** `scripts/__tests__/detectorGuard.test.mjs`, 12 checks: a single skip never alerts, three failures do not, four do, the cooldown suppresses a repeat, other detectors in the same run are unaffected. The DB bookkeeping path is not covered offline.
- **How to check later (free):** expect 0 alert emails for isolated skips. `select rpc, count(*), count(*) filter (where alerted) from detector_failure_log group by 1;` after a day (about 6 rows from 3 clusters, 0 alerted) and the 24 h log query from Update 6 for the skip count. A first real alert means one detector failed 4 times in 3 hours: look at the cron load first, then that detector. Delete this note after one clean day.

**Update 8 (2026-10-15, 24 h measurement after Updates 6 and 7; a heavier-load finding that is not fixed).** Logs for the last 24 h on v36 (which still contains the retry, the held 7-day window and the 14-day cap): 356 runs, 0 fatals, 14 timeout retries, 4 skipped detectors, against the pre-Update 6 baseline of 300 runs, 11 retries, 3 skips. **The 14-day cap made no measurable difference** (Update 7 said the same on a shorter window); the retry recovers most timeouts and the new alert rule keeps isolated skips quiet, which is the outcome Jonathan chose. Skips keep landing on small detectors too (`dangling_quote_mark` is 73 ms alone and still timed out once at 09:44 UTC on 10-14), which confirms the cause is contention at the shared cron minutes, not any one query.
- **New observation, cause not investigated.** `pg_stat_statements`: `heal_completed_packages()` (2-minute cron, calls `package_completion_blocking_defects`) and `sweep_incomplete_packages()` (2-minute cron) have a mean of about 680 ms and a max of 17 to 18 s over 11.6k to 11.8k calls each; on 2026-10-07 the mean was about 240 ms and the max about 6.3 s. Each now accounts for about 8,000 s of database time. The PostgREST detector calls average 2.7 to 3.1 s over about 5.4k calls. `net._http_response` housekeeping (pg_net) is another 15,000 s total, max 31.6 s. Between 10-07 and 10-15 the completion gate and detector set grew (Addenda 93, 96, ADR-0155, ADR-0161 additions), which is the likely reason, but I did not confirm it, and I did not change anything.
- **If the skips ever need to go down rather than just stay quiet,** in order: (1) profile `heal_completed_packages` and `sweep_incomplete_packages` first (they run every 2 minutes and grew 3x), (2) stagger the cron minutes (ten jobs fire together at :00/:10/:50), (3) a stored per-package verdict so unchanged packages are not rescanned. Do not shorten more windows: Updates 5 and 6 showed that does not help.
- **Status of the thread:** closed as "accepted, monitored" per Update 7. No code change in this update. 0 USD.

## Addendum 89 (2026-10-12): New-Purchase sweep - "Compleet Overzicht - de Tijdcapsule Van Klas '96" (NL, detective style, 7 characters, no accomplice) - found unswept six days after delivery; the customer's explicit climax (the "Daanus" running clue, "Hij is dus écht een gladjanus!", Dina's stairs memory) was in no character sheet

**Trigger.** Jonathan pasted the purchase notification on 2026-10-12 to confirm an older order "was indeed complete" (no generation alert had ever fired). Conversation `f2dd8b07-d46f-4fb7-9778-70be4ff738fc`, package `01c497a2-e20a-48f6-8b9a-e06e54b56ebe`, purchased 2026-10-06 20:03 UTC, completed and ready email sent 20:16:52 UTC (reviewer 20:14:33 to 20:16:52, about 2 minutes). First live Dutch package since Child53 (language pack) was imported.

**Complete: yes.** `generation_status` completed; 7 of 7 characters with every detective-style field; `detective_script`, `evidence_cards`, `materials`, `game_overview`, `master_context` (59k) and the three evidence images present; `package_completion_blocking_defects` NULL. `host_guide` NULL and `final_innocent`/`reveal_confession_*` NULL are normal for detective packages (checked against every paid detective package since 09-25). No alert fired because nothing failed, so the order had simply not been swept. Generated BEFORE Parent77/Child58 (imported 20:35 UTC) and Parent79 (20:50 UTC) and before Parent80, so it carries none of their fixes.

**Reviewer (read first, before any edit, so recall is valid):** 11 findings, 6 auto-applied (all read correctly in context) and 5 left open. All 11 real (verdicts recorded). Misses by the reviewer: 8 (below). Recall 11 of 19 (58 percent).

**Defects fixed (27 exact-match replaces in one transaction, each asserted to hit exactly one row; pointform twins kept in sync):**
1. Round 4 evidence card named the murderer ("nog aan Daan moest stellen", ADR-0035 violation) and contradicted the timeline (Bas had already asked): now "aan iemand heeft gesteld over de camera en de la". The advisory `evidence_culprit_spoiler` had hit it; the reviewer flagged the same quote only as a wrong fact.
2. Detective script Round 2 cued the Round 2 card after describing Round 1 clues (card is Mila's school-newspaper clipping): clipping added before the cue. Round 3 cued the Round 3 card after Bas's notes (card is the darkroom developing slip, the notes are the Round 4 card): slip described before the cue. The Round 3 half was a reviewer miss.
3. Detective voice: formal "u" in the opening changed to "jullie"; "of [Google NotebookLM]" and "pairs met één forensische afbeelding" (mixed-language slips) corrected; the reveal's "Het was zijn bijnaam" (ambiguous, reads as the father's) made explicit; the arrest line "voor de aanval op Bas" changed to "voor de aanval van drie dagen geleden" (murderer and victim in one sentence, expected because the package predates Parent77).
4. Tom's `round3_script` and `final_statement` named "Daan en Anna" (he cannot know; hints at the solution); Mila's `round2_script` used the Round 3 notes finding (reviewer finding) and her `round2_questions` said Tom "gelegd" the photos (he found them); Anna's rumor "neerlegden" and question "met de foto's" (same fact); Anna's introduction had a stray combining accent ("hé́l"); Anna's `accusations_pointform` was empty (hand-written).
5. **Missing customer requirements (Jonathan approved the authored additions):** the master context lists as user requirements that Daan is called "Daanus" occasionally all game, that someone shouts "Het is dus Daan!" and Kevin answers "Hij is dus écht een gladjanus!", that Dina recalls her father saying "Die gladjanus..." from the stairs, and that a friend confirms the nickname. "gladjanus", "Daanus" and "trap" appeared in zero character fields; only the detective reveal mentioned them, as if already said. Added one italic "Klimaxmoment" line (and pointform bullet) to the accusations of Kevin, Dina and Bram (Bram's worded so his sheet does not reveal the culprit), and "Daanus," as a vocative in six Round 3/4 questions (Tom x2, Bram x2, Kevin, Mila).

**Left alone (decisions):** (a) the English scaffolding in `detective_script` and `evidence_cards` headings: it is by design in every non-English detective package and the card printer parses the English headings (`evidenceCardUtils`, ADR-0035/0129); not translated. (b) The Round 4 evidence image carries garbled English handwriting ("Monday Morning routing; D chame Tom 3451!"): an image-model text artefact on an English prompt; a fix is a paid regeneration, not asked for. (c) 6 reviewer-applied fixes accepted as they stand.

**Dutch or wider? (Jonathan's question).** Mostly wider.
| Item | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| English scaffolding in detective script and card headings | wider, by design | present in nearly every non-English detective package since 08-01 (ES, FR, IT, DE, PT) |
| Mixed-language slip in the card intro sentence | wider | the Italian "I Dodici Sigilli Di Villa Blackwood" (09-30) has "Each card pairs con un'immagine forense" |
| Round 4 card names the murderer | wider | the detector hits 3 English packages too (Whitfield 09-21, Ashworth 09-06, Gold 08-14); 4 hits in about 75 detective packages; single-name murderers match on the first name |
| Arrest line with murderer and victim | wider, timing | all 10-06 packages (EN too) have it; from 10-09 the line is generic ("for the crime committed in this house tonight"), so Parent77 held (a) |
| Formal "u" in the detective voice | unclear | one instance; the earlier cases (Addendum 13, 56) were German and Spanish |
| `accusations_pointform` empty | wider, normal | 398 characters in 54 packages since 09-01 |
| Character headers and register | held | all headers Dutch and canonical, no formal "u" in any character (hand regex; `lang_audit.py` has no Dutch register pattern) |
| Customer-required climax missing from the characters | wider | not language-related; first confirmed instance |

**Decisions on new detectors (ADR-0103 Addendum 38 test: cheap and deterministic, and severe and obvious?).** (1) Card-intro-sentence slip (`Each card pairs` not followed by `with one forensic image`): cheap, but low impact (a host-facing display note); deferred, 2 occurrences, no detector. (2) Customer-required phrases absent from every character: needs a parse of the approved concept, so fuzzy; severity high for the customer; first occurrence, so stays manual; a repeat should trigger a look at an "unresolved user requirements" check in the reviewer or Parent. Not built.

**Misses logged** to `package_review_misses` (8 rows) and a `package_review_sweeps` row written; scoreboard row added.

**Post-import watch items touched.** Language pack (Child53/ADR-0135): held for characters on the first Dutch package. Detective-style Parent73/Child51 items not evaluated (package predates Parent77/Child58).

**Not done / open for Jonathan.** The customer received this on 10-06 and may have seen the Round 4 card spoiler and the missing climax; no outreach made (nothing in the data says they complained). The vault changelog is missing the 2026-10-08 entries (not touched here).

**Update 1 (2026-10-12, same day: evidence images regenerated; Jonathan skipped customer outreach).** Jonathan asked for the image text to be fixed and said outreach is not needed (a short optional note was drafted for him instead). All three evidence images carried garbled English text (Round 2 a newspaper headline, Round 3 and Round 4 handwriting), not just Round 4 as first noted. Regenerated through `generate-evidence-images` (Replicate Flux 1.1 Pro, about 0.04 USD per image, 8 images in total including re-rolls, about 0.32 USD, Jonathan's explicit go-ahead; no Anthropic spend). Prompts now say no letters, words or numbers and keep any writing out of frame or face-down: Round 2 two faded school portraits on a torn paper scrap, Round 3 a darkroom logbook strip with illegible pencil scribbles, Round 4 a crumpled lined sheet lying face-down. The first attempts made Round 2 and Round 4 worse (Flux invents readable fake English when asked for "newspaper" or "handwritten note"), so the working recipe is: depict the object without its text. Originals kept in the session scratchpad only; the storage path is overwritten in place, so a browser may show the old image for up to an hour (Supabase cache). Also the failed Pages deploy for 2819b0e was a transient npm registry 404 on the freshly published `@typescript-eslint/scope-manager@8.71.2` tarball (`npm install` step; the docs-only commit changed no site content and the previous deploy stayed live); the tarball served fine minutes later and a fresh `gh workflow run deploy.yml` (not a re-run) was started.

**Why this was not alerted, and what the data says (Jonathan's question).** No generation note is by design: `notify-generation-issue` fires only when a package is held or stuck; this one passed the gate, the reviewer ran, and it was released with the ready email, so every signal said "fine". Of the 14 paid packages released since the review-before-release pipeline went live (2026-10-04), 10 were released with open reviewer findings (about 83 of about 121 findings left open); `REVIEW_DIGEST` is off, so nothing tells anyone. Auto-apply covers `single_generation_slip`, `wrong_fact`, `cross_field_contradiction` (languages en, es, fr, de, it, pt, nl); it never applies `secret_leak`, `language_slip` or `pronoun_drift`, does not cover Danish or Swedish packages, and left 3 package-document findings of covered classes open on this package for a reason not yet diagnosed (my hand edit overwrote the note on those rows). Judged precision is 95 to 100 percent in every category of `review_performance`, so the open findings are mostly real. Recall measured against hand sweeps is 33 to 91 percent by category (detective cross-field 33, secret leak 62, wrong fact 55, slips 91), and it cannot see an absent requirement.

**Update 2 (2026-10-12, later: customer quirks and the climax cue added; Jonathan's go).** The new requirements-coverage probe (ADR-0150) found customer-specified details absent from every sheet that the hand sweep had missed (all confirmed in the database): Tom's filler sayings (only "ik zeg maar zo" was used), Bram's camping story (the noises that were reebokken), Kevin's "Pinnen? Haha sorry, ik bedoel: wil je dat aangeven?" slip and his "truck" remark to two women, and Anna's fine for public drunkenness. Hand-written into the owners' introductions and scripts in their voices (11 edits, pointform bullets kept in step; backup of all seven characters in the session scratchpad): Tom intro "Ja, het is ook mijn weekend" and "ping ping en ling ling", round 2 "Poepiedie papiedie", round 3 "Skidadle skidoodle"; Bram intro; Kevin intro (two); Anna intro. A second probe then showed that no sheet told any player to shout "Het is dus Daan!" (Kevin's cue only reacted to it), so Mila's accusations now say to call it first if she concludes Daan is the culprit. **Left out on purpose:** Tom's "jawuhl meine Führer" (a Nazi-parody quip from the customer's list); the probe still reports it as missing, correctly. Jonathan can add it with one edit. Probe cost 0.21 USD; after the edits the gate and the meta-leak, pointform-language, empty-pointform and narration detectors are clean. The reviewer-side recall result for this package stays 58 percent (the probe did not exist when it ran).

**Update 3 (2026-10-12, closing).** Jonathan sent the customer a Dutch note (informal "je", routine-quality-check opening, "email me directly" closing; reprint the Round 4 card and the character sheets). Parent83 was imported and verified live (ADR-0151). Leftovers decided: the two older August packages that name a culprit on a card stay as delivered, and Tom's "jawuhl meine Führer" line stays out (reasons in ADR-0151). Machinery added this session: ADR-0149 (daily open-findings email, language_slip, Danish and Swedish, retroactive apply 40 findings), ADR-0150 (requirements coverage, second pass), ADR-0151 (full-name culprit check, pronoun_drift, Parent83). Open findings on the Danish, Swedish and French packages are all closed.

## Addendum 90 (2026-10-12): New-Purchase sweep - "Champagnens Skygge" (DA, slip style, 17 characters, accomplice) - the customer's two name changes never reached the character sheets; German and English labels in 17 Danish sheets

**Trigger.** Jonathan pasted the purchase notification plus a "Generation Display Issue" alert (status `needs_review`, 17 of 17 characters) on 2026-10-12. Conversation `df706106-f2fa-4f46-9723-aef3cf2fa95d`, package `b18f6a86-feef-4669-9d21-e7b2e7ed0828`, purchased 2026-10-09 08:41 UTC (customer jla@corit.dk), generation 66.6 minutes, reviewer 09:46:00 to 09:48:25, released and ready email 09:48:25 UTC. Generated with Parent81/Child61 (after Parent80, Parent79/Child59), before Parent83.

**Complete: yes. The alert was stale.** By the time of the sweep `generation_status` was `completed`, `needs_review_at` NULL, `package_completion_blocking_defects` NULL, all 17 characters with every slip-style field (4 empty pointforms per character are the legacy `round*_script_pointform`/`final_statement_pointform`, normal), `detective_script`, `evidence_cards` (3 cards, 3 images), `materials`, `game_overview`, `master_context` (119k, non-empty: Parent81's fallback read held on a 17-character package) present. Why it was held at the moment of the alert cannot be recovered (the hold marker is cleared on release). All eight scoped detectors (meta text, victim mismatch, unresolved victim, dangling quote, role tag leak, slip reveal names cast member, missing core content, narration person) were clean. Parent76 held (the detective reveal and closing name no one), Parent74 held (no `[Closing`), the register item is moot (Danish has no formal/informal split in use), Parent83 and the Parent77/Child58 victim items do not apply (slip, no named victim, `keyMomentPositions` absent).

**Reviewer (read first, before any edit).** 32 findings written at release, all medium; they sat unapplied until the retroactive apply of 2026-10-12 17:42 to 17:54 UTC (ADR-0149/0150 session), which is before this sweep, so the reviewer's output pre-dates my read and recall is valid. Read in context: 30 real, 2 debatable. 14 of 32 are Danish article and gender slips ("en hul", "mit hukommelse", "en tyveri") and about 11 more are garbled or non-word phrases ("bårert", "tabte sig" for "gik vild", "tager buket"); one hit a German header, on Emil only. **A reviewer-applied fix was itself wrong:** both Dagny findings replaced "har vist en vejen" with "har vist en vejen ind", still ungrammatical; hand-fixed to "vist en vej ind". Recall: 32 found, 11 distinct misses (below), about 74 percent.

**Defects fixed by hand (every replace asserted to hit its expected count, in transactions, backup of all 17 rows in the gitignored `temp-files/backup-champagnens-skygge-2026-10-12.json`):**
1. **Customer's requested renames not applied (high).** The chat ended with the customer asking for a new surname for Agnes Weber and for "Bedstemor Signe Holm" not to be a grandmother; the AI answered "Agnes Munk" and "Signe Holm", the customer generated. `master_context` (`userRequirements`, `characterDetails`), the relationship matrix and 15 other sheets say Agnes Munk and Signe Holm, but `extracted_characters` kept the first full-list names, so Agnes's own sheet (name, description, background, introduction, introduction pointform) said "Agnes Weber" and Signe's sheet was titled "Bedstemor Signe Holm". Fixed in `mystery_characters` (6 field edits plus 2 names) and in the `extracted_characters` snapshot. No other table keys on the name (`character_assignments` uses ids).
2. **Language slips in section labels (medium, 17 of 17 sheets touched).** German "Zur Person" on 8 background sections (the reviewer fixed Emil's only), "KARAKTERHINTERGRUND" x2, "VERBÜNDETE / RIVALEN & FEINDE" on 6 sheets, "DIT GEHEIMNIS" (Margot), English "Why This Matters:" (Dagny), the wrong-gender secret header "DIT HEMMELIGHED/DIT HEMMELIGE" on 12 sheets (Danish "hemmelighed" is common gender: DIN HEMMELIGHED), "SÅ DU ER USKYLDIG" on Thorkild's four innocent branches, three typo headers (AFSLÆRINGEN, AFSLUTNINGEN, AFSLøRINGEN), "Sådan mødte figuren offeret". Text columns and the `secrets` jsonb copy and the `relationships` JSON string were all updated; a final scan finds no German or English label left.
3. **Round 4 header "RUNDE 4: LEJLIGHED" (46 fields) changed to "MULIGHED"** (Jonathan's yes; "lejlighed" reads as "flat", the other 17 fields already said MULIGHED).
4. **Secret leak.** Jazz-Jo's accomplice branch (and its pointform bullet) said Ingrid's mother quietly pays her debt, which Ingrid's and Dagny's secrets say nobody knows. Replaced by "som altid har en ny skandale og en ny regning i hælene".
5. **Confession clears another cast member.** Dagny's `reveal_confession_guilty` said "Ingrid vidste intet. Hun skal ikke bære skylden", but culprit and accomplice are drawn at random, so Ingrid can be the accomplice and her own confession contradicts it. Sentence (and pointform bullet) removed. This is the open decision of Addendum 81 Finding 3 in a new shape (clearing instead of implicating a named helper); the other 15 confessions name cast members only as people they pointed at, which stays true for any draw.
6. **"i aftes" (last night)** in Dagny's introduction (and pointform) and in Ingrid's rumor about Signe; the theft is tonight in the detective script and `master_context`. Holger's "Den nat" and Ingrid's "i nat" are fine and left.
7. **`game_overview` named five hidden secrets** (Jazz-Jo's debt, the Count's gambling debt, the Captain's delivery, Elise's affair, Margot's smuggler past) and that Thorkild is only posing as a guest. Rewritten to public information only (ADR-0151 does this for new packages; Jonathan's yes to apply it here).

**Checked and left alone (decisions).** (a) Detective script, cards and materials: coherent, English scaffolding headings by design. (b) Characters refer to clues that are in `master_context` but on none of the 3 printed cards (tinktur and gaze, kassebrev, kapsel and rem, chalk mark, shadow with a tray, perfume on the banister): the 3-card limit is true of every package since 09-20, so it is corpus-wide design, not a defect of this one; noted for the roadmap (the host cannot show what the scripts discuss). (c) Mixed grammatical person across sheets (Ingrid, Holger, Agnes and Margot's secret in third person, Emil, Jazz-Jo and others in second, Dagny and Signe in first): style, not a fact. (d) Header wording variants with the same meaning (ANKLAGER/ANSKYLDNINGER, SLUTERKLÆRING/AFSLUTTENDE ERKLÆRING, "DIN tilståelse" vs "DIN TILSTÅELSE"): left; Danish has no language pack (ADR-0135), which is why they drift. (e) Four characters name Jazz-Jo's debt to dangerous people as a rumor: it is in the customer-approved concept text itself. (f) "Jazz-Jo: nogen bad mig forlænge et nummer" appears in his innocent branch (an unfalsifiable clue); left, ambiguous.

**Root cause and what to do about it.** The customer's rename was a post-approval edit expressed as a delta ("alle de øvrige 15 karakterer ... er intakte"). ADR-0069 re-captures the approved message only when a later message re-states a roster of a different shape; a delta that restates two names does not trigger it, and ADR-0069 explicitly deferred "prose-only drift". The Parent (Make) reads the whole conversation after the snapshot (ADR-0059), so `master_context` is right; the roster handed to Child (names) comes from the snapshot and is not. This is the same family as the Vellacourt stale cast (Sept) but the cast was complete here, only two names stale. **Detector test (Addendum 38): not cheap and deterministic enough yet.** A comparison of the matrix names in `master_context` with `mystery_characters.character_name` over all packages since 08-01 found this package and 6 others, but those 6 are noise (a victim who is not playable, nicknames, titles), so a first-occurrence detector would be wrong 6 times out of 7; a repeat should trigger a narrower version (a master_context name with the same first token as exactly one sheet name and different surname). The ADR-0150 requirements item in the reviewer should flag it on a future package (the customer's renames are in `userRequirements`); not probed here (paid). **Prevention candidate** (a Make or edge-function change, Jonathan's call): apply `userRequirements`-style renames to the roster before Child runs.

**Post-import watch.** Parent81/Child61: `master_context` non-empty on a 17-character package, held. Parent76 and Parent74: held. Reviewer volume: 32 findings for 17 characters (about 1.9 per character, against about 0.9 for the slip baseline in `docs/autonomy-scoreboard.md`), mostly Danish grammar; one package, so a data point, not a conclusion. Generation time 66.6 minutes for 17 characters (the customer waited; no baseline for that cast size recorded).

**Not done / open for Jonathan.** (1) Review-table bookkeeping (verdicts for the 32 findings, 11 misses, the sweep row) was declined as an MCP write; it is in `temp-files/champagnens-skygge-review-bookkeeping-2026-10-12.sql` for a manual run, then `select * from review_performance;`. (2) The customer has had the package since 10-09 and may have printed the old names, the grandmother title and the German/English labels; no outreach was made (a draft note is in the session reply). (3) A Danish language pack for headers (ADR-0135) would stop the header drift for the next Danish order. (4) The evidence-card/script clue mismatch in (b) is a roadmap item, not fixed.

## Addendum 91 (2026-10-13): New-Purchase sweep - "Murder On The Silver Nightingale" (EN, detective style, 12 characters, accomplice) - materials list written in Spanish on an English package, and the same on 4 other English packages since 2026-10-09

**Trigger.** Jonathan pasted the purchase notification on 2026-10-13 (four days after delivery). Conversation `af5faa29-ed3d-450c-8e19-3593f57ce126`, package `178de25c-d7b8-4e02-9210-4e63298614e9`, purchased 2026-10-09 15:49:49 UTC (customer logan.a.welsh@gmail.com), generation 25 minutes, released and ready email 16:15:06 UTC. Generated with Parent81/Child61, after Parent79/Child59 (Sonnet 5.5) and before Parent83 (imported 2026-10-12).

**Complete: yes.** `package_completion_blocking_defects` NULL, 12 of 12 characters with every detective field and all pointform twins (accomplice and murderer carry the extra `accusations_pointform`), `master_context` 68k with `timelineFramework.keyMomentPositions` present (Parent77/Child58 watch item d), `detective_script`, 3 evidence cards, `game_overview`, `materials` present. All scoped detectors clean (meta text, victim mismatch, unresolved victim name, dangling quote, role tag leak, slip reveal, missing core content, relationship matrix, evidence culprit spoiler, reveal name fusion). `host_guide` is NULL, as it is for detective packages generally.

**Reviewer (read first).** Ran before release on 2026-10-09: 6 findings, 3 applied (a garbled Juliet introduction sentence, a garbled Round 3 evidence significance, "put behind it"), 3 open (Vivian's Round 3 question and Felicity's Round 4 question both presume Juliet's hidden confrontation with Reginald; the materials list is Spanish). All 6 real (verdicts recorded). No `+p2` findings. **One applied fix needed repair:** the Round 3 evidence significance now said "the vial and the single napkin set apart on the brandy service tray", which puts the vial on the tray although the card says both were recovered from a waste bin; reworded by hand to "the vial and the napkin, which matches the single napkin set apart on the brandy service tray".

**Full-cast read (12 of 12), cross-checked against `master_context`, the detective script and the cards.** Parent77/Child58 and Parent73/76 controls all held: the arrest sentence names no victim and no "for the murder of"; no innocent's Round 2 or 3 script points at a role by name (Round 3 scripts say the poison needed "someone who knew the stores and the service", which is the detective's own deduction from the card); alibi witnesses agree in both directions (Felicity and Simone, Marcus and Harry, Beatrice and Nikolai, the head table four); the register is English throughout; all 12 `relationships` blocks match the matrix. Pointform exists for every script and is in English.

**Fixed by hand (every replace asserted to hit exactly once, one transaction):**
1. **`materials` in Spanish on an English package (medium).** 7 bullets ("Música ambiente de los años 30", "Copas de brandy y bandeja de servicio", ...). Translated to English by hand (this was reviewer finding b3db43ce, left open because the reviewer does not auto-apply `PACKAGE DOCUMENTS`). See "Corpus" below: this is a recurring regression, not a one-off.
2. **`game_overview` named hidden motives (medium).** "Percival Okafor was bled over murky dealings. Antonia Rolewicz dreaded the exposure of her title, and the train's own physician and head steward were not spared either" tells every guest that the physician (accomplice) and the head steward (murderer) were squeezed, plus Bartholomew's confided secret. Rewritten to public information only (Juliet jilted in public, Beatrice's unease, Nikolai unexplained). Pre-Parent83 package, so this is the ADR-0151 class, not a new one.
3. **Two reviewer findings applied by hand** (Vivian Round 3 question, Felicity Round 4 question): both asked Juliet about a private talk with Reginald that only Juliet, Beatrice and the steward could know. Replaced with the reviewer's suggested wording.
4. **Two borderline leading questions softened:** Nikolai to Juliet in Round 2 ("What did you want to say to him when you were finally alone?") and Marcus to Juliet in Round 4 ("before you reached his door"). Both presume Juliet met Reginald. Debatable; changed because the edit is free and removes any chance of a hint.
5. **Josie's `relationships` lacked Harry Dunmore.** The matrix has Josie-Harry Friendly both ways and Harry's sheet lists Josie as an ally. `package_relationship_matrix_contradiction` checks for contradictions, not omissions, so it was NULL. Added one ally paragraph.

**Checked and left alone (decisions).** (a) Percival's "I was the last to speak with him" (sheet, final statement and `master_context.victimLastSeen` all say so) while Juliet's secret has her confront Reginald in the compartment corridor afterwards: the canonical timeline itself is ambiguous, and Percival could not know; left. (b) Beatrice's secret has Reginald approach Juliet and the two walk off, Juliet's has her slip out and confront him: compatible enough, left. (c) The Round 4 card shows the initials "S.D." beside the brandy tray: Simone Delacroix is the only S.D. in the cast, but it is the intended Round 4 clue, not a name copied from `master_context`. (d) Bartholomew is "they" in his description and "a man of business" in his own speech: the customer-assigned player is James; harmless.

**Unresolved, needs Jonathan.** The customer's brief calls Marcus Finch's real identity and reason for being aboard "the secret identity twist at the heart of the mystery". Nothing in the package says who he really is or what his purpose is: his secret, his final statement and `master_context` all say only "a purpose I meant to finish" and "a cover I built". Harry (journalist) gets a concrete twist; Marcus does not. This is a requirement the reviewer's REQUIREMENTS item (ADR-0150) did not report (none of the 6 findings was a requirement item). A fix needs a creative decision (who Marcus is) and edits to Marcus's background, secret, final statement and rumors from others; recorded as a miss, not edited.

**Corpus (Addendum 38 test applied).** The `materials` list is the output of the four "Themed Materials (R0..R3)" Make modules, whose only language rule is "Always respond in the same language as the master_context provided below". A scan of every package created since 2026-10-04 (stopwords per language) finds Spanish materials on **five English packages, all created since 2026-10-09 15:49**: this one, "Blackthorn Manor: A Victorian Murder Mystery - Final Concept" (10-09), "Dead Man's Saddle" (10-10), "The Last Slice: A Cake Swap To Die For" (10-11) and "Lip Sync For Your Life (and Death)" (10-12). English packages before that (Vellacourt, Last Thanksgiving, Montero Manor, Death And Dumplings) and one after ("Emma's Honky Tonk", 10-09 18:30) had English materials; Spanish packages and the French, Dutch, Danish and Swedish packages are correct. So it is intermittent (5 of 7 English packages in four days), started after Parent81/Child61 were in place, and is not tied to style. Cause not established: Make execution output is UI-only and the prompt gives the model a very weak language anchor (no explicit language, only a pointer at a long JSON). **Detector test:** a stopword comparison of `materials` against `game_overview` is cheap, deterministic and the impact is a host's printed shopping list in the wrong language (obvious, but only to the host who reads it), so by Addendum 38 a detector should be built now; the heal is a translation of 5 to 8 bullets (about 0.01 USD, needs a yes, or free by hand). Not built in this session: it waits on Jonathan's call (below).

**Bookkeeping.** `package_review_findings.human_verdict` set on all 6; 6 rows in `package_review_misses` (overview leak, evidence wording side effect, Josie omission, two debatable questions, Marcus twist; the wording side effect is not counted in recall); one row in `package_review_sweeps`. Recall for this package: 6 found of 11 distinct defects (about 55 percent); the 5 not found are the overview leak, the Josie omission, the two debatable questions and the Marcus twist. The evidence wording repair is a side effect of an applied fix, logged but not counted as a miss.

**Post-import watch.** Parent81/Child61: `master_context` non-empty (68k), held. Parent77/Child58 (a)-(d): held. Parent73/76: held. Reviewer volume 6 findings for 12 characters (0.5 per character), the lowest of the recent English detective packages (baseline about 13 per package in ADR-0142). Parent83 was not in this package; the overview leak confirms it was needed.

**Not done / open for Jonathan.** (1) Marcus's identity (above). (2) The other four packages with Spanish materials were not touched (bulk change across customers; they need the same translation). (3) Whether to build the `materials` language detector and a Parent84 prompt change that names the target language explicitly in the four Themed Materials modules.

**Update 1 (2026-10-13, same session, Jonathan: "1. yes", "2. your reco, best guess from the initial convo").**
- **Marcus Finch's twist written.** From the customer's brief (a "retired detective" cover; "who he really is and why he's really on this train"; an unfinished purpose; Reginald probing the pretense): Marcus is a working private inquiry agent hired by a family Reginald once ruined to recover Reginald's ledger of secrets before Vienna. Edited his `secret` and `secrets`, `final_statement` and its pointform, and one sentence in `master_context` (so a regeneration keeps it). The family is deliberately unnamed, so no new suspect or fact is introduced; his alibi, observations and denials are untouched. Jonathan's instruction was to guess, so this is a guess; it is easy to change.
- **Four other packages' materials translated by hand** (one package per call; a single call bundling all four plus the Marcus edit was declined by the permission layer): Lip Sync For Your Life (and Death) `a03fcc0e`, Dead Man's Saddle `aa767a77`, Blackthorn Manor `0bc4a72a`, The Last Slice `20ab2a21`. Each update matched exactly one row by its Spanish first bullet.
- **Detector built (migration `20261013120000_materials_language_mismatch_detector.sql`, applied):** `list_packages_with_materials_language_mismatch(_since)`, with `package_materials_language_mismatch(pkg)`, `detect_text_language` and `text_language_scores`. It takes the language of `game_overview` (the stopword method of `_shared/languageDetect.ts`, 40+ words) and flags the package when another language scores at least 0.08 on `materials` and at least twice the overview's own language. The first version used the reviewer's strict thresholds and missed a 70-word Spanish list (short prop lists have few stopwords); the relative version scores the old Silver Nightingale list es 0.37, en 0.00. Corpus run, every package since 2026-01-01: 2 hits, both real: a TEST package (French list, English overview) and **"Morte No Topo: O Ultimo Brinde De Vivienne" (`57acfc47`, paid, 2026-07-07): Portuguese package with an English props list**, i.e. the reverse direction, so the defect is older and wider than the October run. Not touched (ask first). It is NOT in `package_completion_blocking_defects`: a hold needs a heal, and the only heal is a translation (paid, or by hand), so it is a sweep-time and daily-check detector for now.
- **Parent84 built, not imported:** `temp-files/MM Live - Parent84 (Materials Language Anchor).blueprint.json`, identical to Parent83 except the four Themed Materials modules (15115, 15119, 15123, 15127) end with a mandatory LANGUAGE CHECK: write in the language of the prose inside `master_context` (naming two example fields), never Spanish by default, do not mix, do not translate. Diff against Parent83 is 5 changed lines (the four prompts and the name). Whether this fixes the intermittence is unproven: the cause (why a model given an English `master_context` answers in Spanish) is not visible from the server, so the post-import test is the next 3 English purchases (CLAUDE.md note added).
- **Open:** translate "Morte No Topo" (Portuguese) on a yes; import Parent84 (Jonathan); consider adding the detector to the daily open-findings email.

## Addendum 92 (2026-10-13): New-Purchase sweep - "Blackthorn Manor: A Victorian Murder Mystery - Final Concept" (EN, detective style, 11 characters, accomplice) - held in `needs_review` for four days because an innocent journalist was stored as a second accomplice

**Trigger.** Jonathan pasted the purchase notification plus the "Generation Display Issue" alert on 2026-10-13. Conversation `4b53b965-0230-4faf-bf9b-54dfad225b9e`, package `0bc4a72a-bfc7-452e-b0aa-d1c248c3f80d`, purchased and generation started 2026-10-09 18:03 UTC (customer ajross532@gmail.com), `needs_review` since 18:10:21 UTC the same day, `ready_email_sent_at` NULL. The customer has therefore had nothing for four days (party date in the brief: 2026-10-30). Generated with Parent81/Child61, before Parent83 (imported 2026-10-12).

**The block.** `accomplice_role_mismatch.has_accomplice=true,accomplice_role_count=2`. Two characters carried `character_role = 'accomplice'`: Pamela 'Pam' Ridgewood and Jacqueline 'Jackie' Winthrop. The approved concept ("received the poison via a separate accomplice"), `master_context.accompliceSelection` (Pam), the detective script reveal (arrests Sheila and Pamela) and Jackie's own sheet (accusations stub of 189 characters like every innocent, `final_statement` an innocent denial, `master_context` `isRedHerring: "true"`, no `*_accomplice` branch fields) all say Jackie is an innocent red herring. Only the role label was wrong, so the gate was right to hold the package and the fix is one field, not a regeneration. Cause of the mislabel is not visible from the server (the Parent's character list is the likely source; Jackie's secret was also written in accomplice style, see below). Corpus check since 2026-08-01: no other paid package has two accomplices, so this is a first sighting, not yet a class (Addendum 31 bar).

**Sweep, full cast (11 of 11) read against `master_context`, the detective script and the three evidence cards.** Scoped detectors all clean (meta text, victim mismatch, unresolved victim name, dangling quote, role tag leak, missing core content, materials language, which was translated earlier today under Addendum 91). `host_guide` NULL as for every detective package. Relationship blocks match the matrix (Caroline omits two Friendly ones, Jackie omits Wendy, omission only, left). Alibi witnesses agree in both directions (settee: Thorne, Trudy, Caroline; card table: Lex, Kat, Wendy; back hall: Anna, Chain, Pam). Register English throughout; pointform present for every script.

**Fixed by hand (one transaction, every replace asserted to hit exactly one row):**
1. **Jackie `character_role` accomplice to redHerring.** `package_completion_blocking_defects` is NULL afterwards; `has_accomplice` stays true (Pam is the only accomplice).
2. **Jackie's secret named the accomplice (high).** "Your consistent goal is to protect the person behind the note. Pam has long been hostile to you, yet she once did you a quiet kindness ... you never turn on Pam or on anyone she is shielding." The same secret says she only saw a note on a side table, so she cannot know who is behind it, and her player would have been told the accomplice's identity. Rewritten so her goal is to keep the note out of the conversation.
3. **Caroline's Round 4 witness line lacked the clue the reveal depends on (medium).** The reveal solves Sheila by "one guest swore never to have left the fire, and yet described a chill from the cellar stairs"; Sheila's own script says "if I spoke to Caroline of a chill from the cellar stairs", but Caroline's script only said "a chill that clung to her". Added "from the cellar stairs" to Caroline's script and its pointform so the clue is actually spoken in the game.
4. **`game_overview` named hidden secrets (medium).** "The forgetful Dr. Thorne hides an unreported death. Wendy's miracle tonics are fraudulent. Anna is a quiet girl in white with dangerous dealings." Rewritten to public information (pre-Parent83 package, the ADR-0151 class, same as Addendum 91 item 2).

**Checked and left alone (decisions).** (a) Anna's background and relationships say Chain delivered the vial "to Pam Ridgewood" and Chain's secret says he handed it to the housekeeper; their players therefore hold a trail to the accomplice. This is the intended Round 3 red herring trail (the concept makes the poison trade a fair clue) and each is told to deny it; debatable, not a leak of the murderer. (b) Several innocents (Thorne, Trudy, Sheila, Chain) say "ask the person who holds the keys", which points at Pam's role through a fact the evidence card makes public (the key tag); left. (c) Wendy's secret says her tonic bottle "was found near the scene" and no other field repeats it; the concept lists the tonic as a red herring, so left. (d) Evidence card 2 mentions a "signed confession clipped to one page" that no sheet uses; left. (e) Anna is a 17-year-old with a poison trade, as the customer specified.

**Reviewer.** Did not run: the package never reached `reviewing` (it was held by the gate), so `package_review_findings` has no rows. After release the cron will review a package that already contains the fixes above, so this sweep is NOT recorded in `package_review_sweeps` (it would measure the residual, not recall, per the protocol).

**Release.** The status flip to `completed` (which fires the paid reviewer through `reviewing` and then the customer's ready email) is a separate step that waits on Jonathan's yes.

**Open decisions for Jonathan.** (1) Release now. (2) Whether to add a heal for this exact shape (a second `accomplice` role when the master_context names one): a deterministic free heal would be "demote every accomplice-role character whose name is not `accompliceSelection.character` to redHerring", but it is one occurrence so far, so no heal or detector is built yet.

**Update 1 (2026-10-13, Jonathan: "Yes release and then let's draft an additional note to apologize").** Released: status set to completed at ~08:46 UTC, held in `reviewing` for about 2 minutes, released and ready email sent 08:48:02 UTC. The reviewer found 3 issues, all applied and all real (verdicts recorded): Lex's Round 3 "could not have said which door leads down to the wine" contradicted his Round 4 admission that he has wandered into the cellar; Thorne's "unlabelled bottle ... with a paper label" contradicted itself; Jackie's Round 3 question said "a handwritten hand". Two of the three were in the text I had read and passed over (Thorne's, Lex's), so the full-cast read missed 2 of 3 small slips here; the sweep is not recorded in `package_review_sweeps` because the reviewer ran after my edits. An apology note to the customer was drafted in chat (not sent).

**Update 2 (2026-10-13, found by the independent ADR-0155 eval).** My item 2 (Jackie's secret) edited `secret` only; her `secrets` array (the column every other character keeps equal to `[secret]`) still held the old text naming Pam. Synced `secrets := [secret]` for that one row (asserted to hit 1 row); afterwards 0 of 11 characters have `secrets` different from `secret` and none contains the old sentence. Lesson for the sweep checklist: when hand-editing a secret, update both `secret` and `secrets`.

## Addendum 93 (2026-10-13): New-Purchase sweep - "Dead Man's Saddle" (EN, slip style, 6 characters, no accomplice) - one unfilled "choose for yourself" instruction in a secret; a host who also wants to play is steered away by the guide

**Package** `aa767a77-4f18-4739-ac4e-a1654f5a0131`, conversation `b78aeba8-4ab0-4064-9ac8-353ae0757933`, paid 2026-10-10 16:06 UTC, generated in about 6 minutes in one attempt, released by the reviewer at 16:12:32. Predates Parent83/84/85 (imported 2026-10-12 and 10-13).

**Detectors.** All of the New-Purchase set run scoped to this package (meta text, victim mismatch, unresolved victim name, dangling quote, role tag leak, slip reveal names cast member, missing core content, materials language) returned nothing, and `package_completion_blocking_defects` was NULL. The relationship matrix agrees both ways for all six characters.

**Reviewer.** One finding, `materials` language_slip (props list in Spanish). Real (verdict recorded). The list was already English when the sweep started: someone translated it by hand after release, so the finding was still `open` but nothing was left to do. The materials-language detector therefore stayed silent for a package it would have caught earlier.

**Full-cast read (6 of 6).** One real defect that nothing caught: Jessica Calloway's `secret` (and the `secrets` array) ended its first paragraph with the unfilled instruction "Choose for yourself what this secret is, in keeping with your character, so long as it is a private shame or wrong...". The customer's approved concept only ever said "her secret" and `master_context.wrongedEachCharacter` repeated it without content, so the Child handed the gap to the player. One occurrence in the whole corpus (ILIKE over `mystery_characters.secret`), so per the Addendum 31/38 test no detector is built (detection would be cheap but the impact is low: the player can still play the character and her other sheets already stay vague about the secret). Recorded in `package_review_misses` (single_generation_slip) and `package_review_sweeps`; the reviewer ran before any edit, so recall is valid (1 of 2 real defects found by the reviewer, 0 of 1 secret placeholder).

**Fix (Jonathan: "Yes go ahead", 2026-10-13).** Replaced that one sentence by hand with a concrete secret that does not touch the ledger clue (the evidence is deliberately "points to no one"): after a bad breeding season she quietly borrowed against the ranch's bucking-stock contracts without her father's knowledge and kept the loan papers from him; Jed saw them on her desk. It is consistent with her scripts ("Jed never asked me for a dime", "I won't tell you what he held over me"). Updated `secret` and `secrets := [secret]` in one pass (Addendum 92 Update 2 lesson), asserted equal afterwards, 0 blocking defects.

**Customer messages.** Oct 10 "failed to generate" / "won't generate, refund": the generation succeeded (one attempt, completed in 6 minutes), so this was most likely the browser showing a failure while the page waited; function logs from that day are gone, so unconfirmed. The customer wrote on Oct 12 that she had the mystery and liked it, so the refund request is treated as withdrawn. Oct 12: she wants to be host and also play a character without learning the other scripts, the clues or the killer. For a slip-style game this works (the detective script says "Whoever drew the Culprit slip" and never names anyone; the evidence "Host Only" notes point at no one), but the host guide says the opposite ("In character-based mysteries, you do NOT participate as a suspect" under Option 1; Option 2 needs generated audio) and its checklist tells the host to skim every character's secret. Jonathan answered her himself with the slip-draw instructions. The guide change is ADR-0157.

**Open.** None for this package.

**Update 1 (2026-10-13, Jonathan's calls after the sweep).** (a) The customer was NOT told that Jessica's secret changed after delivery (she received the package on Oct 10 and may have printed or sent the sheets; whoever plays Jessica could hold the old "choose for yourself" text). Jonathan's call: leave it, she has not replied and may not; send the standard "package updated" note only if she writes back. (b) The Oct 10 "failed to generate" mails: not investigated further (logs gone, package generated fine, customer later said she liked it). (c) Reply to her Oct 12 hosting question was sent by Jonathan as drafted; the guide change is ADR-0157 (commit a02b99c, key count corrected in cff060f). (d) The detective-style version of ADR-0157 (host also plays) is handed to a separate tab with a kickoff and eval prompt (vault: 01_Projects/Mystery-Maker/kickoff-detective-style-host-also-plays-2026-10-13.md). (e) Non-English host-guide strings from ADR-0157 stay without native-speaker review; accepted, no action. (f) Corpus check for a possible "unfilled instruction" detector: the narrow phrases "choose for yourself / decide for yourself / in keeping with your character / up to you to decide / your choice of" matched 0 of 2,769 character rows after the fix (1 before it), whereas broad phrases ("invent a", "make up a") match many legitimate in-character lines and must not be used. No detector built yet; see the open question to Jonathan.

**Update 2 (2026-10-13, Jonathan: "ok built it with the heal").** Built the detector and the heal for the "instruction handed back to the player" class. (1) `package_meta_text_leak()` gained an `instruction_rx` marker (choose for yourself | decide for yourself | in keeping with your character | up to you to decide/choose) tested against `secret + background + relationships + description` of each character only (migration `20261013120000_meta_text_leak_player_instruction.sql`, applied as a guarded in-place edit of the live body that aborts unless each anchor occurs exactly once). Spoken fields are excluded on purpose: the same words are ordinary dialogue there and a false hit costs a paid regeneration. Because `package_completion_blocking_defects()` already uses this predicate, a hit holds the package. (2) The heal needed a hand-kept mirror (`supabase/functions/auto-remediate-packages/player-instruction.ts`, called from the character loop in `handleTemplateArtifacts` before `stripArtifactLines`): without it the strip found nothing to strip in prose like this, the handler returned "nothing to do", and the package would have sat held with no regeneration. With it the field goes to `delegateToRegenerator` (regenerate-child-content, about 0.15 USD per character, which re-runs the SQL detector and reverts on a persisting defect). Deployed with the CLI as version 32 (live code was identical to HEAD first; `verify_jwt` stays true). (3) Verification: unit test (`npm run test:metaleak`, 14 cases incl. the real Jessica text, spoken-field immunity, and the broad phrases deliberately not matched); a rolled-back transaction on a TEST package showed the phrase flagged in `secret` and `background`, not flagged in a spoken `introduction`, baseline clean, nothing left behind; corpus: 0 of 2,769 character rows match after the Jessica fix; the detector's hit set is unchanged (1 package before and after) and the 14-day window the cron uses takes about 1 s (the full-corpus scan measured 13.4 s, 22.6 s and 10.6 s across three runs, so the one slow reading was noise, not the change). (4) NOT verified: the heal has not run on a real hit (a live test would cost about 0.15 USD on a package that has the defect, and none exists). Known risk: if the Child omits a secret the brief never defined, the regeneration may repeat the placeholder; the regenerator then reverts and the attempt cap escalates, so the failure mode is an alert, not a bad write. (5) Not done on purpose: the Child prompt fix (a brief that never defines a secret should make the Child invent one) needs a Make import and the class is 1 in 2,769; revisit on a second sighting.

**Update 3 (2026-10-13, close of session).** The customer replied "Ok great. Thanks so much" to the hosting email and did not mention the changed Jessica sheet. That met the condition in Update 1(a) only in the narrowest sense, and Jonathan's call was to leave it: no "package updated" note is sent about Jessica's secret. If she later asks about a sheet, send the standard note then.

## Addendum 94 (2026-10-13): New-Purchase sweep - "Lip Sync For Your Life (and Death)" (EN, slip style, 13 characters, no accomplice) - held 30 hours on a character's own name inside her own question header; first Parent83 package

**Package** `a03fcc0e-fb20-4632-bf54-fd2358c696ab`, conversation `b880b124-63f8-4fd8-9242-60348c88dcd3`, paid 2026-10-12 13:06 UTC, generated in 8 minutes, held in `needs_review` at 13:13 and swept 2026-10-13 19:11 UTC (about 30 hours later; the customer had no package in that time). Parent83 (imported 2026-10-12 12:03) and Child61, so this is the first purchase on Parent83.

**Why it was held.** `self_directed_question.Mistress Calamity`: her `round2_questions` item 1 read "**To Mistress Calamity's neighbor Sapphire Knight/Sebastian Knight:**". Real (a single-generation slip, her own name plus an invented "neighbor"); the auto-remediate worker logged `escalate:no_mechanical_fix`. Fixed by an exact-match replace to "**To Sapphire Knight/Sebastian Knight:**", the gate went NULL, a plain `completed` update moved the package into `reviewing`, and the reviewer released it 19:18:40 (ready email sent then). No trigger bypass needed. Also seen: a `dangling_quote_mark` strip heal (Chardonnay's `final_innocent` and `reveal_confession_guilty`, 13:13) healed it free. This package predates Child63 (it ran on Child61), so it says nothing about whether Child63 removed the cause.

**Detectors.** The New-Purchase detectors (meta text, victim mismatch, unresolved victim name, dangling quote, role tag leak, slip reveal, missing core content, materials language, confession names, matrix, and the rest of the `list_packages_with_*` set) scoped to this package returned nothing besides the self-directed one; the slip reveal and closing name no cast member ("the guilty player"), materials are English, no instruction handed back to the player. Parent76/Parent80 held.

**Reviewer (ran before any hand edit except the gate fix, so recall is valid).** Main pass: 5 applied (`a owner`, `a easy`, "I mean," at the start of Rhinestone's description, Chardonnay's "Velvet get crowned again", Tommy's garbled "mine is the one who has been smiling") plus one `REQUIREMENTS` item, all real. The `+p2` pass at 19:20 applied one more (Baron: "last night ... Actually, ask them how they're sleeping tonight"), real. First live `requirements_extract` row (20 lines) and first live `pointform_resync` row (Chardonnay's rumors bullets, 0.05 USD; bullets match the corrected prose): both ADR-0152/0155 post-import watch items are confirmed working. Reviewer cost 0.73 USD.

**Full-cast read (13 of 13).** Nothing was contradicted across `master_context`, `detective_script` and evidence cards (funnel, jug, masks, lanyard, tank wiped all agree). Found by hand and not by the reviewer: Sapphire's introduction ended with stray `**` (stripped by hand). Missed by me and caught by the reviewer: the Tommy and Baron slips above.
