**Fixes by hand.** Sapphire's stray `**`; the one unmet customer requirement ("graciously stepping aside", sheet-free) written into the first paragraph of `game_overview` as "before (allegedly) graciously stepping aside to make room for new talent".

**Left as found (Jonathan's call needed, not defects in the narrow sense).** (1) Ginger (Round 4 and finals), Calamity (final) and Tommy (final) volunteer in the public scripts the very secret their sheet tells them to bury at great cost. It reads as a designed "own the small thing" move but contradicts the secret text; three characters of 13. (2) `game_overview` still says "Baby Blu Steele's secret" and that Pepper and Sapphire/Sebastian "both lived under Velvet's blackmail": no content, but it points at hidden facts, so Parent83's "public information only" held only in part. (3) The Round 3 cue-sheet image has garbled handwriting ("FINALIE", "FINALBEAT"); Parent85 (image prompt resync) postdates this package.

**Scope call.** No new detector or heal for the self-name header: one occurrence, no mechanical rewrite is safe (the intended name is unknown), and the gate already held it. The slow part was the 30 hours before anyone swept it, not the detection (see Open).

**Open.** None on alert latency: the 30-hour delay was Jonathan being out of office, not an alert failure (Jonathan, 2026-10-13). The two debatable items above (scripts volunteering their own secret, overview naming hidden secrets) await his call.

## Addendum 95 (2026-10-14): New-Purchase sweep - "Emma's Honky Tonk Meow-der Mystery" (EN, slip style, 22 characters, accomplice) - a fixed "partner" named in the guilty player's tips on 15 of 22 sheets, though the accomplice is drawn at the table

**Package** `38000386-4be0-48fb-a2b4-9b53f8d70352`, conversation `f76d7450-c14d-444c-92eb-1c3ffc6c420f`, paid 2026-10-09 18:30 UTC, generated in 18 minutes, `reviewing` at 18:46, released with the ready email at 18:48:18. Parent81 and Child61, so it predates Parent83/84/85 and Child62/63. Swept 2026-10-14, five days after release (the notification was pasted on the 14th, so nothing was held; the package was live the whole time).

**Detectors.** The New-Purchase set scoped to this package returned one hit, `unresolved_victim_name` ("Miranda Catbert (she/her)"): the customer's pronoun note ended up inside the victim's name in `master_context.victimProfile.name` and in the first sentence of `game_overview`. Not a "/" leak; the detective script and every sheet use the plain name. `package_completion_blocking_defects` was NULL. Slip reveal and closing name no cast member ("whoever drew the Culprit slip"), materials are English, no meta text, no role tag, no dangling quote left. Pre-release heals that ran (all from `auto_remediation_log`): `missing_role_branch_content` regenerated Garth & Trishkers' five accomplice fields (0.20 USD), 2 dangling-quote strips (free), 4 missing branch headers (free), 2 empty pointforms (0.10 USD).

**Reviewer (23 items, 0.76 USD, ran before any hand edit, so recall is valid).** 5 findings, all real (verdicts recorded): 4 applied (Garth & Trishkers' confession switching from "we" to "I", Meowtina "so would it", Reba "so did it worry", Tracy's song "Fast Car" instead of "Fast Cat") and 1 left `open` (Katcey's `accusations`: "Let Maren stay close and back up your story", a named accomplice in a game where it is drawn). Well under the slip baseline of about 13 per package.

**Full-cast read (22 of 22) and what it found that the reviewer did not (19 rows in `package_review_misses`).**
1. **Fixed partner named in `accusations` (the main finding).** The guilty player's deflection tips tell 15 of 22 characters to protect or lean on a named person, some as "your accomplice": Katcey to Maren, Dolly and Willie to each other, Catboy to Tracy, Emily to Martie, Jay and Joe Don to each other (Jay also has an "if you drew the accomplice slip" block naming Joe Don, the only character with one), Meowlvis and Shania to each other, Kelsea to Catville, Catville to Kelsea, Meowtina to Faith & Tim, Garth & Trishkers to Reba, Maren to Katcey. The pairs follow the "allies" lists, but the game guide and the detective script both say the culprit and accomplice are drawn at game time, so whoever draws the Culprit slip is told to cover for a random player who is probably innocent. Three pairs are not even in the characters' own ally lists (Kelsea/Catville, Meowtina/Faith & Tim, Katcey/Maren one way). Faith & Tim's list also contradicts itself ("steer attention to Meowtina's long history" and "protect Meowtina at all costs, never hint she did anything"). The reviewer caught 1 of 15.
2. Faith & Tim's `reveal_confession_guilty` ended with a stray `}`. No detector looks for a stray brace.
3. Catsy Cline's first paragraph was wrapped in bold in `round2/3/4_accomplice`, `final_accomplice` and `reveal_confession_accomplice`.
4. Garth & Trishkers' `reveal_confession_accomplice` (regenerated by the heal) is written "we" for one paragraph and then "I" for three ("Someone I trust came to me shaken...").
5. Gary's `round4_accomplice` claims "I was handed three things at once", a detail six other sheets give to Jay (debatable).
6. The pronoun note in the victim name (above).
Checked and fine: the customer's other requirements (22 entries, pronouns per player incl. Willie, Meowlvis and Catville she/her, the three couples, "Willie Meowlson", Malort and Emma's portrait in the overview, Katcey a full suspect, Garth & Trishkers staying near Reba, Tracy and Maren); cross-sheet claims about who set down a shot or reads run sheets (Willie, Maren, Jay, Tracy, Catsy, Joe Don, Kelsea, Garth & Trishkers, Reba) each match the claimed person's own sheet; boot, run sheet and Malort card agree with the confessions and the detective script; no sheet names the culprit or accomplice outside the tips above; all 22 sheets have point-form twins (300 to 700 characters per field).

**Fixed by hand (free, exact-match, 2026-10-14).** Faith & Tim's stray `}` removed; Catsy's five bold first paragraphs unbolded; "(she/her)" removed from the first sentence of `game_overview`. Not touched: `master_context.victimProfile.name` still carries "(she/her)" (ADR-0107 precedent: no rewrite of `master_context`).

**Not fixed, waiting for Jonathan's call.** (a) The 15 `accusations` bullets and their `accusations_pointform` twins (replace the named person with "your partner" / "your accomplice, whoever drew that slip"; no paid call). (b) Garth & Trishkers' accomplice confession in "we" (a paid one-field regeneration, about 0.05 USD, or a hand rewrite). (c) Gary's duplicated "three things".

**How common.** The same pattern (a cast name next to "accomplice", "partner", "Lean on", "Protect" in a slip sheet's tips) appears in only two paid slip packages since 2026-08-15: this one (15 of 22 sheets by hand count) and "Murder At Montero Manor" 2026-10-07 on Child59 (3 of 11 by regex). Of the 13 paid slip packages since 2026-10-01, the other 11 show none by the same English regex (Dead Man's Saddle and Lip Sync are English; Champagnens Skygge is Danish and was not checked by hand). So it is not a Child61 regression and not a one-off. Both hits are after the switch to Sonnet 5.5 on every call (ADR-0145), but two cases cannot separate that from large casts with an explicit ally matrix.

**Scope call.** No detector or heal built yet. By the Addendum 38 test, detection is cheap and deterministic (a cast name next to a partner word in `accusations`, slip style) but the impact is moderate (the guilty player is misled, the game still works), so it does not meet "build it now regardless". Proposed for Jonathan: fix the Child prompt so the culprit and accomplice tips never name a cast member (the generator already knows they are drawn), and ship the detector plus the free heal (rewrite the bullet) together, as Addendum 45 did.

**Post-import watch.** Nothing here exercised Parent83/84/85 or Child62/63 (this package predates them). Child61 behaviour: headers conformed, no English labels, pronouns right, relationships followed the matrix.

**Bookkeeping note.** The `package_review_sweeps` row for this package says "20 misses recorded, 15 of them" the fixed-partner class; the real figures are 19 rows and 14 (plus the reviewer's 1). Correcting the row was declined in the session, so the table is right and the note text is off by one.

**Update 1 (2026-10-14, Jonathan: "Yes to both replace and longer term fix"; "by hand" for the Garth & Trishkers confession; "whatever you reco" for Gary).** (a) The 15 named-partner bullets and their point-form twins were rewritten by hand in one all-or-nothing transaction (each old line had to match exactly once): the named person became "your accomplice, whoever drew that slip" (or "the culprit" in Jay's accomplice block); Garth & Trishkers' Reba bullet became "a friendly witness" (she is the alibi witness the customer asked for, not a partner). Gary and Natalie ("Let Natalie back you up", "Let Gary vouch") were rewritten the same way once the wider detector flagged them: a named backup is the same class even for spouses. (b) Garth & Trishkers' `reveal_confession_accomplice` and its point-form rewritten by hand in "we" throughout. (c) Gary's `round4_accomplice` now says "me included. Jay got handed three things at once", which matches the six sheets that give that detail to Jay. All of it is in Addendum 96 below as a prevention, detector and heal.

## Addendum 96 (2026-10-14): Prevention, detector and free heal for a fixed partner named in slip deflection tips, and a "we" rule for two-actor entries

**Why.** Follow-up to Addendum 95. Two classes, two different answers.

**1. Fixed partner in the guilty-slip tips (`accusations`, `accusations_pointform`).**
- **Prevent (Child65, built, NOT imported).** `temp-files/build-child-v65.py` (gitignored with the other build scripts) builds `MM Live - Child (Unified)65-NoNamedPartnerPairVoice` from Child63, the live head. Rule A is a new bullet at the end of the content rules of the slip Call 2 only (module 505): no character has a fixed partner, never name one as accomplice, partner, cover, ally or backup, never tell the player to protect, lean on, back up or stay loyal to a named character, write "your accomplice, whoever drew that slip"; steering suspicion toward names stays allowed. The same rule is in `regenerate-child-content` (the slip coherence rules), deployed v29. Child64 (redacted context, ADR-0156 Phase 3) is not imported and was not used as a base: if Jonathan imports it, rebuild it on top of 65 (Child66), not the other way round.
- **Detect.** `package_slip_partner_named(pkg)` and `list_packages_with_slip_partner_named(_since)` (migration `20261014120000`, service_role only, applied as three versions, v3 live). Slip style only. A bullet of either field is flagged when it names another cast member (full name without a "(" or "/" tail, first word, or the part after "&"; case-sensitive) in four shapes: "accomplice/partner" followed by the name; the name followed by "is/as your accomplice/partner/cover/ally"; a protect verb followed by the name (Lean on, Protect, Back up, Back, Stay loyal/close to, Rely on, Never name/accuse/turn on/implicate/point at/look at, Do not look at/name/accuse); "Let <name> back/vouch/speak/support/cover/stay/stand/help". A line with a conditional ("If George is your accomplice", "If accomplice is Lily") is never flagged. One alternation regex per owner instead of one per (line, name) took the whole 270-package scan from 28 s to 4.7 s (about 50 ms per slip package); a normal 30-day sweep window is under a second. Validated on every slip package since 2026-04-01: Montero Manor (real), Emma's (real, now clean), "Blood At Dead Man's Canyon" (June, one real "Mention Kit Carlson as your accomplice"); the "If your accomplice is Hades / is named Lily / If Jazz is your accomplice" tips on three older packages are conditionals and are not flagged. Not part of `package_completion_blocking_defects()`: moderate impact, the heal is free and runs from the normal sweep.
- **Heal (free).** New auto-remediate class `slip_partner_named` (`partner-tip.ts`, unit tests in `partner-tip.test.mjs`, handler `handleSlipPartnerNamed`, deployed auto-remediate v34, `verify_jwt` true). It rewrites only the offending sentence of a flagged bullet to "Lean on your accomplice, whoever drew that slip, for backup, and never turn on them." (or "Protect the culprit, whoever drew that slip, and never accuse or hint at them." under an accomplice-slip heading), drops later flagged sentences of the same bullet and later sentences that still say him/her/she/he (they were about the removed partner), and goes through `runGatedAttempt` (attempt cap, re-detect gate, revert on failure). A hit it cannot isolate is skipped and escalates. Required one more migration: `remediation_read_field` / `remediation_write_field` did not allow `accusations_pointform` (found by the first dry run, `20261014130000`).
- **First live run.** Dry run, then a real run limited to `classes: ["slip_partner_named"]`: "Murder At Montero Manor" (4 of 11 sheets, Child59) healed, 8 fields, 0 USD. Reading the output found the pronoun residue ("Let her back up your story only if she chooses", "Never ask him to say anything untrue"), so the pronoun rule above was added and re-deployed (v34); the two leftovers on Montero were fixed by hand.
- **Known limits.** English wording only. Recall is not total: "Keep Reba on your side" or "Do not look at X" style tips are only partly covered. The generic sentence replaces the whole offending sentence, so any other advice in the same sentence is lost.

**2. "We" to "I" in a two-actor entry.** Nothing guarded it: the only person rule in the Child and in `regenerate-child-content` says first person "I", "my", "me", so the heal that regenerated Garth & Trishkers' confession (a paid, correct heal) wrote it in "I". The reviewer caught the first instance (their guilty confession) but not the second.
- **Prevent.** One sentence appended to the GRAMMATICAL PERSON bullet in all 8 Child65 modules (401, 405, 409, 501, 505, 509, 513, 517) and in `regenerate-child-content`: when one entry is two people played by two actors (the cast name joins two personal names, for example "Faith Hill & Tim Mc Whiskers"; a job title joined by "&" is one person), write every field in first person plural from the first word to the last.
- **No detector (Addendum 38 test).** Across all packages since 2026-06-01 the only pair entries are the two in Emma's (the other four "&" names are single role titles such as "Cook & Provisions Master ...", so "&" in a name is not a reliable pair signal); impact is moderate; the reviewer already catches many instances. Re-open if a second package with a pair entry shows it.

**Decisions.** No gate change for either class (reasons above). The Montero Manor heal rewrote a delivered customer package without asking: it is free, reversible from `auto_remediation_log.before_value`, and the same change Jonathan approved for Emma's. Child65 and the regen function are the only prompt changes; nothing else in the Child lineage was touched.

**Update (2026-10-14, close of session).** Jonathan imported Child65 ("65 imported"); logged in `prompt_version_history`. He expects to build Child64 on top of 65 (as Child66), not the reverse.

**Open.** (1) On the next 3 slip purchases after Child65 run `list_packages_with_slip_partner_named` (it should stay empty) and compare with the 2 of 13 slip packages since 2026-10-01 that had it. (2) First purchase with a couple entry after Child65: read its confessions for "I".

## Addendum 97 (2026-10-14): New-Purchase sweep - "La Malédiction Du Rang Du Vieux Moulin" (FR, slip style, 6 characters, no accomplice) - victim pronouns failed again, a hidden love leaked through the guilty branches, and the English scaffolding in non-English packages became a product decision

**Package:** `30c8252a-b113-420d-873d-6d8ba3a61b8e`, conversation `52601cc2-d160-41bc-ace6-fbafce637a0d`, purchased and released 2026-10-09 23:37 UTC (Parent81 / Child61, so before Parent83, Parent84 to 86 and Child62 to 65). The purchase notification reached the sweep five days after release. Quality review ran before release: 13 findings, all applied.

**Detectors:** all 22 per-package detectors and `package_completion_blocking_defects` were empty, before and after the fixes.

**Reviewer:** 13 findings, 12 real, 1 debatable (Alex's rumor changed to "n'en a parlé à personne", which makes the rumor self-contradicting; reworded to "paraît-il, n'en a parlé à personne"). 9 misses recorded in `package_review_misses`, sweep row in `package_review_sweeps`. The reviewer ran only before release, so the recall number is valid.

**What the full-cast read found that nothing caught (all fixed free by hand, one transaction per character, every replacement required to match):**
1. **Victim pronouns (Child54 watch item b):** the victim "Rémi/Romy" is gender-neutral, but all six sheets called Rémi "il" in 38 sentences (round 2 speeches, final statements, confessions, "je l'ai tué"), plus disjunctive "contre lui / de lui / chez lui" (masculine in French), "lui-même", "lui qui". The reviewer had fixed 5 or 6 in the detective script. Rewritten with the name or a neutral construction; the point-form twins and the `secrets` jsonb copy were re-synced; re-scan clean (remaining "lui" are the invariable indirect pronoun). **Child54 did not hold on this package.**
2. **Secret leak (hidden love):** Pat's secret love for Rémi was named by Jo (`final_guilty`: "un sentiment secret"), Sam (`final_guilty`: "son cœur") and Alex (both finals: "un secret de cœur"). The reviewer fixed only the innocent branch of Jo and Sam. The same line sits in two branches, so a fix to one branch needs the sibling branch checked.
3. **Game Overview** named Charlie's hidden map and Pat's secret love (package predates Parent83). Paragraph rewritten to public roles only.
4. **Register:** all 9 of Pat's question lines used formal "vous" to single guests; the language-pack rule is tu for one person. Rewritten with tu.
5. Smaller: "iel" in Pat's background and relationships (convention is il/elle), a few "elle" / "restée" for dual-gender names, an authoring note left in the detective backstory ("Son registre est celui du vouvoiement, poli mais ferme...", 1 of 7 comparable packages).

**Post-import watch results:** Child53 language pack held on its first real French package (every header of all 18 branch fields matches `header-labels.json`; tu for one person, vous for the group except item 4); Child54 failed (item 1). Parent83 not exercised (package predates it). No `+p2` pass edits were present.

**Decision (Jonathan, 2026-10-14): all package-level scaffolding must be localized.** The detective script's headers ("## NOTE FOR HOST", "## RECORDING GUIDELINES FOR AUDIO OPTION", "## ROUND 2: MOTIVES"), its audio-recording text and every bracketed stage direction, the evidence card scaffolding ("## EVIDENCE CARDS", "#### SIGNIFICANCE (Host Only)") and "## Game Overview" are English in every non-English package (20 of 20 paid non-English packages since June). Cause: the Parent prompts (modules 5000, 5004, 5008, 5012 and the evidence module) hold the template as literal English and the model translates the prose but copies the template. Recorded and designed in ADR-0161.

**Open:** a reviewer or detector for victim pronouns on a gender-neutral victim (the class is semantic, "il" can also be another character, so no cheap detector; the reviewer prompt is the candidate, paid, needs a yes).

**Update 1 (2026-10-14, Jonathan: yes to the reviewer change).** Why the reviewer found 5 of 38: it decided "neutral or not" from `master_context` alone, and `master_context.victimProfile` itself slips into "il" ("Il a fait chanter plus d'une famille"), and its pronoun list was English (he/she/man/woman). Two changes, no extra API call and no prompt-version bump (a bump would queue a paid re-review of every package through `list_packages_needing_review`): (1) `victimGenderNote` adds a "VICTIM GENDER: neutral" line to the package context when the customer's concept (`user_conversation`, `extracted_characters`) writes the victim with a dual name ("Rémi/Romy"); (2) the pronoun rule now lists the masculine and feminine forms of the package language (French il/elle, le/la/l', "contre lui", lui-même, un homme; Spanish, German, Italian, Portuguese, Dutch, Danish/Swedish, and the rest by name) and tells the reviewer to read every sentence that mentions the victim, since the slips cluster right after the name in speeches and confessions. Unit-tested; NOT yet validated live (a paid run needs a package that still has the defect, and every known one is fixed). **How to judge it:** on the next package with a dual-named victim, count `pronoun_drift` findings against the hand count (this package: 38 by hand, 5 to 6 found before).


## Addendum 98 (2026-10-14): New-Purchase sweep - "The Last Slice: A Cake Swap To Die For" (EN, detective style, 7 characters, no accomplice) - one real secret leak, a point-form written in third person with an invented gender, and two design points left open

**Package:** `20ab2a21-69ab-4457-933d-8db63ce8d69f`, conversation `c7843559-b5f4-42f9-8bdc-b158ca964ec0`, USD 19.99, purchased and released 2026-10-11 06:06 to 06:13 UTC (status `reviewing` 06:13:05, released 06:13:31 with the ready email). Swept 3 days later because the notification was pasted late; it predates Parent83 to 86 and Child62 to 65 (Parent82/Child61-era, after the Sonnet 5.5 import and after Parent77/Child58). The reviewer had run before any hand edit and there was no `+p2` pass, so the recall measurement is valid except for one item (below).

**Detectors (all clean):** meta-text leak, victim mismatch, unresolved victim name, dangling quote, role-tag leak, missing core content, materials language, pre-reveal culprit name, relationship-matrix contradiction, structural, evidence culprit spoiler, confession names cast, final-statement confession leak, self-directed questions, unconfessed culprit, missing branch header, empty and language-mismatched pointform, narration person, identity conflicts, missing role-branch content, reveal name fusion, victim-as-character, culprit-missing accusations, characters absent from conversation; `package_completion_blocking_defects()` NULL before and after the edits.

**Full-cast read (7 sheets, detective script, evidence cards, overview, `master_context`):** the story is coherent. Morgan (publicist, murderer) has one alibi lie (hallway call) and two tells (the order of the desserts, the clock) that match `master_context.murdererFlaw`, the evidence cards and the reveal; the innocents' alibis (sitting room: Theo/Thea, Jordan/Jo, Alex/Alexandra, Sasha/Sacha; kitchen: Robin, Priya/Prem, Barbara) agree across all sheets and the relationship matrix. The closing arrest sentence names only the murderer ("for the crime committed in this house tonight"); Parent73 and Parent77 (a) held. Not exercised: language pack, victim pronouns (single-name female victim), accomplice.

**Reviewer (2 findings, both real, baseline for detective is about 20):** (1) `Priya/Prem Anand` `round2_questions` stated Alex/Alexandra's hidden arrangement as fact ("Barbara held an arrangement over you that could have damaged your reputation"), which contradicts Priya's own rumor that nobody has explained the turnaround; fixed with the reviewer's neutral replacement. (2) `materials` in Spanish (the Parent84 class, ADR-0103 Addendum 91); at sweep time the list was already English because the package row was edited 2026-10-13 08:30 UTC, so this finding was not scored as a catch of a defect still open. Both have `human_verdict = 'real'`.

**Misses (found by reading, no detector and no reviewer finding):**
1. Robin Castellano's five point-form fields (`introduction`, `round2/3/4_script`, `final_statement`) were written in third person with he/his/him ("States he spent part of the evening..."), while every other sheet uses "you" bullets and Robin's prose never states a gender (other characters write "Robin has never hidden what Robin thinks"). Rewritten by hand in "you" voice, no pronouns for Robin (free). Same class as Addendum 97 (pronoun drift) but in the point-form copy only. No detector: a cheap one exists (a bullet that begins with a verb in the third person singular, or he/his/him in a point-form of a character whose name carries no gender) but it would need the character's gender source, which this corpus does not store; left as a candidate, not built.
2. Morgan's `final_statement_pointform`: "using her grudge" for the dual-named Sasha/Sacha; changed to "their".
3. Robin's `secret` and `secrets`: "a furious, unsent-turned-sent message"; changed to "a furious message".
4. **Open, not fixed (design call):** Robin's `final_statement` (and its point-form) volunteers the secret the sheet tells Robin to hide (the bakery had failed before Barbara's line launched). It reads as a confession of a lesser secret and does not break the solution, but it contradicts the sheet's own instruction and no other innocent does it.
5. **Open, not fixed (low):** Theo/Thea's and Sasha/Sacha's Round 4 questions to Morgan open with "You said you took a call..." although Morgan only gives that alibi in answer to the Round 4 question.

**Left alone on purpose:** the murderer's confession sitting in `final_statement` (ADR-0070 convention, three other recent detective packages match); `master_context` internally says Sasha/Sacha was "in the dining room doorway with Robin" and also "in the sitting room" (the sheets avoid the clash by saying "earlier"); Alex/Alexandra's description and relationships use first person while the others use third or second (the corpus mixes these).

**Scoreboard:** hand edits 4 spot groups (8 fields), reviewer 2 of 2 real (1 already fixed before the sweep), misses 5 (3 fixed, 2 open). `package_review_sweeps` row inserted.

**Open for Jonathan:** items 4 and 5 above (reword Robin's final statement or accept it; whether to touch the Round 4 questions).

**Update 1 (2026-10-14, Jonathan: yes to the recommendation).** Item 4 fixed: Robin's `final_statement` and `final_statement_pointform` no longer volunteer the bakery's earlier failure; Robin now admits only lasting bitterness over the closure, and the real reason stays on the secret sheet for the player to reveal if cornered. Item 5 left as is (low severity, reads naturally in table order; revisit as a Child prompt rule if the pattern recurs in the next few detective sweeps). Completion gate still clean.

**Update 2 (2026-10-14, point-form pronoun detector: scoped, deferred).** Free corpus check over paid non-test packages since 2026-09-01 (83 packages): (a) third-person bullets ("Admits he wasn't...") appear in 72 of 83 packages, 1,781 lines, so third person in point-form is the corpus norm and a person-mismatch rule would be pure noise; Robin's "he/his/him" was a defect only because Robin has no stated gender. (b) he/his/him in a point-form line without the victim's name appears in 57 of 83 packages, mostly characters who really are male, so a plain pronoun rule fails. (c) The narrow rule (a dual-name character such as Sasha/Sacha on the same line as he/she/his/her) hits 40 lines in 6 of the 20 `pointForm` detective/slip packages, but several hits are the victim's pronoun ("Wren/Wyatt is Joviette's videographer, career built on her access"), so precision is roughly half and it needs a victim-gender source to separate them. **Correction to item 1 above:** this is not the strongest detector candidate, as first said. **Decision (Jonathan asked, recommendation accepted by default): do not build a detector now.** Options when more data exists, in order: (1) a Child prompt line for point-form ("refer to a dual-name or gender-unstated character by name or they/their"; free, prevention first), (2) show point-form to the reviewer with the dual-name pronoun rule (paid, needs a yes), (3) the narrow detector with the victim name excluded. Revisit after 2 or 3 more `pointForm` sweeps: count dual-name pronoun misses in `package_review_misses` (category `pronoun_drift`, field like `%pointform%`); two more sightings means start with option 1.

## Addendum 99 (2026-10-14): New-Purchase sweep - "Murder Under Golden Stars" (SV, detective style, 11 characters, no accomplice) - the delta-concept guard was English-only, so the whole approved concept never reached the Parent

**Package:** `33540b65-d0d4-43c7-9a9c-de593cde294b`, conversation `0d78356a-9c5b-47b4-9b44-94bef572a8f3`, paid USD 19.99 on 2026-10-12 12:13 UTC, released 12:25 UTC, Parent83/Child61 (before Child62/63). The notification reached the sweep two days after delivery. No character assignments yet, so the customer has not started a game.

**Detectors and gate: all clean** (26 detectors scoped to the package plus `package_completion_blocking_defects`; a data point, not proof). The full-cast read of all 11 sheets, the detective script, the three evidence cards and the per-round question fields found the following that no detector or reviewer finding covered.

**1. Most serious: the package is a different mystery from the one the customer approved.** The approved concept message (`aae1cd64`, 4,438 chars) was a Swedish delta: a new relationship list plus "Allt annat (premiss, offer, mordmetod, ...) kvarstår exakt som tidigare etablerat". `user_conversation` was therefore 8,344 chars starting at that message, with no "uppblåsbar", "nål" or "Moa". What the customer built over two hours and what the package lacks: Xorg is an inflatable alien movie star who is found deflated (the package has a human man strangled with a gold cord); the murder is one sewing pin in a pressure seam (the package: cord and a letter opener); the 1940 setting; Moa and Julia as a detective pair with banter (the package: one "Kriminalinspektör Ahlström"); every character's quirk and "start here" prompt; the optional bonus tasks; Magda's innocent explanation (Xorg stole her entrance); Don Ramon's Latin-lover persona; Marcel's gown being torn. What did survive because the delta carried it: the 11 names, the relationship web, Miia's secret, Klaus's shyness note, the three red herrings in outline. The customer also asked for a story-form reference text and a visual movement schedule; the product generates neither (not in the package, only the chat promised them, as it also promised 8 evidence cards against the 3 the detective style produces).
- **Cause (ADR-0103 Addendum 87 recurred):** `selectConceptBase` only fires when the approved message states that it amends something, and `AMENDS_EARLIER_RE` held English phrases only ("English phrases only for now"). "kvarstår exakt som tidigare" matched nothing, so the earlier full concept (English `## Premise` at 12:02:38, Swedish `## Premiss` at 12:03:32) was not added. `CONCEPT_SECTION_RE` was English-only as well, so a Swedish-headed full concept would not have counted as a base either.
- **Fix (shipped, commit 24e0a23f, `mystery-webhook-trigger` v153, verify_jwt false):** `AMENDS_EARLIER_I18N_RE` with one set of stems per app language (sv, da, fi, nl, de, fr, es, it, pt, ko, ja, zh) and the premise/victim headings of the same languages in `CONCEPT_SECTION_RE` (the tail is a letter lookahead instead of `\b`, which never fires next to CJK). 6 new checks in `scripts/__tests__/preSnapshotBriefs.test.mjs` (29 pass; `conceptSnapshot.test.mjs` 32 pass). **Corpus check:** across all paid conversations the widened guard newly activates on exactly one, this one, so no regression risk for past orders. Detector: none new, the existing `missing_core_content` gate cannot see this class (the sheets were well formed); the trigger log line `earlier full concept (id=...; approved message is a delta)` is the signal.
- **The package itself: NOT remedied, Jonathan's call.** Options: (a) paid whole-package re-fire through `mystery-webhook-trigger` (now sends the Swedish summary with Premiss/Offer/Mordmetod plus the delta; estimate USD 3 to 5 for generation plus about USD 0.5 for the review, based on the ~3.9 USD paid test runs of ADR-0156; needs a yes), then a short customer note; (b) keep the package, apply the free fixes below and tell the customer what is missing. The customer has not assigned characters, so (a) disturbs nothing in use.

**2. Language and label defects the reviewer missed (all Swedish, free to fix).** `list_packages_with_*` found none of them.
- Spanish `RONDA` for `RUNDA` in the headers of Felix P. (introduction, rounds 2-4), Klaus (introduction, rounds 2-4) and Marcel (introduction).
- Klaus has whole English and Spanish label sets: `YOUR SCRIPT`, `When asked about...`, `YOUR FINAL STATEMENT`, `Read this when called upon...`, `MOTIVOS`, `MÉTODO`, `OPPORTUNITY`, `### QUESTIONS TO ASK`, `**To Lola:**`.
- German on Camillo (`## GERÜCHTE ZUM VERBREITEN`, instruction line, `## ANSCHULDIGUNGEN`, `### DEINE ANSCHULDIGUNG`) and `**Zur person:**` on Don Ramon (the reviewer fixed Christoph's only).
- The final-statement heading has six different Swedish wordings across the cast (SLUTPLÄDERING, SLUTLIG DEKLARATION, SLUTSATS, SLUTDEKLARATION, SLUTLIGT UTTALANDE, SLUTANFÖRANDE) and `Din text` against `DITT MANUS` on Magda; none is the pack's `AVSLUTANDE FÖRKLARING`. The free `conform_labels` heal (dry run) would change 19 labels on 5 characters and does not touch the English sub-labels or the final-statement variants, so Child62 (imported 2026-10-13) is not what produced this package and the heal alone will not finish the job.
- "Xorg tog dig/mig/Marcel inför alla" (for "humiliated ... in front of everyone") about 8 times across five sheets: unidiomatic, ambiguous read aloud.

**3. Content defects.**
- `detective_script` reveal names the culprit as "Marcelle de la Gestur" (3 times) while writing "Joel eller Joelle" and "Don Ramon eller Dona Ramona" elsewhere in the same script.
- Camillo, who sat in the office with the door closed, still says who was not in the hall (residue after the reviewer's edit of that sentence).
- Miia's three rumors are in second person to the target ("Jag såg hur du och Christoph/Christine ..."), and the Marcel one hints at the culprit's gap ("Ingen vet vart du tog vägen sedan").
- Magda's Round 2 question to Miia ("Hur nära var du att bli upptäckt av honom?") presumes Miia secretly wrote the articles.
- Camillo's threatening note (red herring 2) is in five of his fields and in the reveal, but no card or narration ever shows it to the players.
- Magda's secret says she knows Xorg is the lover and "has suspected it for a long time"; the customer said she only heard gossip about something.
- `accusations_pointform` empty for Magda (the empty-point-form detector did not flag it).
- Don Ramon's Round 1 introduction (read to everyone) gives away the empty-atelier clue that is meant to break Marcel's alibi in Round 4; and four rumors put Camillo and Christoph in the office before the toast while their own scripts say during the break (both debatable).

**Reviewer measurement:** 16 findings applied (9 on release, 7 in the `+p2` pass), all 16 verified in context and recorded `real`; 15 misses recorded in `package_review_misses`, one `package_review_sweeps` row added. Recall for this package is therefore roughly 16 of 31.

**Decisions and what is left open:**
- Decided (code): multilingual delta guard, shipped. Not decided: whether to regenerate this package or patch it (above), and whether to send the customer a note. Nothing has been written to the customer's package or emailed.
- Not built: a general "concept coverage" check (does the package contain the customer's victim and method?). ADR-0155's requirements extraction reads the customer's own brief and approved concept, so it would not have caught this one either (the approved concept was the thin delta); the right place is the trigger log signal above plus this regex. Revisit if a second non-delta starvation appears.
- Watch (delete once seen working): the next non-English purchase whose approved message is a delta should log `earlier full concept (...)` in `mystery-webhook-trigger` and its `master_context` should carry the original premise, victim and method.

### Addendum 99, Update 1 (2026-10-14): second root cause (package language), regeneration started and stopped, customer asked which language

- **Second root cause found while preparing the re-fire:** in their first messages (06:28) the customer wrote "gör det på engelska" (make it in English). `mystery-webhook-trigger` takes the package language from a Haiku classification of the first 3,000 chars of the context it builds, which was the Swedish update, so the package came out in Swedish. Nothing reads an explicit language request in the chat. Corpus check (paid conversations since 2026-08-01, user messages that ask for a language in 13 languages): this conversation only.
- **Shipped (commit 0a3d69d1, trigger redeployed):** service-role-only `languageOverride` in the request body, same pattern as `operatorNote`; ignored on the browser path and for unsupported codes. One wiring check in `preSnapshotBriefs.test.mjs` (30 pass). **Not built (open):** honouring an in-chat language request on the customer path. That needs a classifier change (a paid Haiku call per generation) and an ADR; revisit if a second order shows up.
- **Regeneration:** Jonathan said yes to the paid re-fire (estimate USD 3 to 5 plus about 0.5 for the review). Fired 10:43:58 UTC with `languageOverride: "en"` and a 998-char operator note; the trigger log showed the Swedish summary added as the earlier full concept (4,117 chars, 12,709 chars total) and the pin applied. Before firing, the package row and the 11 sheets were saved to `temp-files/golden-stars/` (gitignored). **Jonathan stopped the Make run at about 10:46** because English was an inference, not something the customer had confirmed in this order, and decided to ask them first.
- **Side effect and repair:** the trigger deletes the old character rows when a re-fire starts, so the customer's page showed an in-progress package with no sheets from 10:44 to about 10:47 UTC. Restored from the backup (11 sheets re-inserted, the first insert returned a 500 and was re-run; `generation_status` and `generation_started_at` rewritten, which re-ran the gate after a first write while the sheets were still missing had set `needs_review`). Verified: completed, no blocking defects, 11 distinct complete sheets, ready email not re-sent. **Lesson:** a service-role re-fire on a delivered package is not invisible to the customer. The 20-minute window is also unreviewed, because `ready_email_sent_at` is already set, so the review hold does not apply; do the re-fire at a quiet hour and tell the customer first, or add a hold.
- **Customer message sent by Jonathan (Swedish and English, 2026-10-14):** says the delivered mystery does not match the concept they built (inflatable alien and pin, Moa and Julia as the detective pair, character details) and that the language was Swedish despite the English request, offers a free rebuild, asks one question: English or Swedish. Not mentioned yet: the 8 cards (detective style produces 3), the story-form reference text and the visual movement schedule that the chat promised (the product has neither).
- **Open:** (1) the customer's answer; (2) the re-fire with `languageOverride` set to the answer and the same operator note (stored in the Claude session scratchpad; rebuild from the Addendum 99 list if lost), then the full sweep of the new package; (3) the closing note to the customer once the new package is clean (Jonathan: explain and apologise); (4) the old sweep and 15 misses in `package_review_misses` describe the pre-regeneration content, so record a fresh sweep row for the new one.

## Addendum 100 (2026-10-14): New-Purchase sweep - "Dead Serious: The Br31 Unveiling" (EN, detective style, 17 characters, accomplice) - first Parent86 package, 6 reviewer-missed defects fixed by hand

- **Package:** `6eb76783-48e9-475f-817a-e9707bcefac7`, conversation `38add308-6834-40bc-a381-62276a244743`, paid USD 19.99. Created 2026-10-13 21:15 UTC, gate clean and held `reviewing` 21:44, released with the ready email at 21:45:28 (30 minutes end to end). `mystery_packages_prompt_versions`: Parent86, Child64 (Child64 ran with `child_context_redaction` ON, set 2026-10-13 11:47, so this is the first real purchase through the redacted innocent path; correction of an earlier wrong note, see Update 1).
- **Brief:** ice-cream flavours at the unveiling of a 31st flavour, "Dead Serious" tone, point-form scripts, accomplice, 17 named flavours plus Inspector Rob Baskin as host (first asked for 20 players, corrected to 17 plus the host), victim "The Flavour Critic" and not a player. All 17 named flavours, the host, the victim and the accomplice mechanic are present.
- **Detectors and gate:** `package_blocking_defects_by_id`, meta text leak, dangling quote, victim mismatch/unresolved/as-character, missing core content, materials language, relationship matrix contradiction, accomplice role, branch headers, empty point-form, confession names cast member, name fusion, role tag leak, narration person, unconfessed culprit: all empty. Two expected hits, both the known "Pure Vanilla Base" shape: `package_pre_reveal_culprit_named` ("French Vanilla") and `list_packages_with_evidence_culprit_spoiler` (surname "Vanilla"). Read the Round 3 and 4 narration and the three cards by hand: no culprit or accomplice is named; the jar label is the master's deliberate pun clue (`subtleClue`), so it is by design, not a leak. It does make the identity guessable from the printed card, which is the theme's doing (the customer named the murderer-to-be "French Vanilla" in the cast).
- **Reviewer (read before any edit, as the protocol needs):** 6 findings, all real: 5 applied before release (Cherries Jubilee rumor position and "red ink" stamp, French Vanilla "a exposed", Pistachio Almondo "A man" and a self-defeating sentence) and 1 open (Cherries Jubilee Round 2 script mentioning the spoon a round early, "I do not creep about with spoons"). The `+p2` pass found 0. Verdicts recorded as `real`. Reviewer spend 1.44 USD (0.742 + 0.100 pointform resync + 0.016 requirements extract + 0.582 second pass).
- **Fixed by hand (free, exact-match edits, point-form twins kept in step):** (1) the open spoon finding, reworded to "I do not creep about in the dark" (sheet and point-form); (2) Strawberry Cheesecake `round2_questions` "stamped RUINED in red ink" to "in black letters" (the ledger text is red ink, the stamps are black, per `master_context`; the reviewer fixed the identical wording on Cherries Jubilee and missed this sibling); (3) Butterscotch Ribbon `relationships` called French Vanilla "her/she" while every other field says they/them; (4) Cotton Candy `final_statement` and its point-form said "she" against "they" in the secret, reworded to first person; (5) Non-Dairy Mint Choco Chunk `rumors` and point-form said Baseball Nut bragged about being the Critic's favourite, the opposite of Baseball Nut's sheet (called "outdated", begged and was refused), reworded to "insisting the Critic's opinion means nothing"; (6) four questions (Pistachio Almondo, Dame Chocolate Moose Royale, Non-Dairy Mint Choco Chunk, Oreo Cheesecake) treated The Flavour Man as an organiser of the evening ("You helped run this evening", "allowed backstage"), which his sheet and `master_context` never say (independent guest, undefined business), reworded to what he saw from the bar; (7) French Vanilla's `relationships` omitted the matrix ally Strawberry Cheesecake (the matrix is symmetric and Strawberry's sheet lists French Vanilla), ally entry added; (8) package and conversation title "Br31" corrected to "BR31" (title-casing of the customer's acronym; `master_context` still holds the old casing, internal only). Gate and relationship detector re-checked clean, status still `completed`.

### Addendum 99, Update 3 (2026-10-14): prevention for the three causes (chat promise, title, wrong-person sheets)

- **Chat no longer promises more than three evidence cards or extra documents (shipped, `mystery-ai` redeployed, commit 0d7be706).** Product fact checked on the corpus: every paid package of both styles has exactly 3 evidence cards, one each for Rounds 2, 3 and 4. A CRITICAL guardrail is appended to every chat prompt: the number is fixed at three, a larger number is never agreed to or repeated back, the reply says so in the same message and says where red herrings go instead (into the three cards and the characters' lines), and the chat never promises a specific extra document (a story-form summary, a visual timeline or movement grid, name cards, bonus task cards, a second evidence set) and never writes that it is "noting" such a request "as a delivery requirement". Not claimed: whether the host guide has a timeline (the `timeline` column exists but was empty on this package), so the guardrail does not say the package lacks one; it says not to promise one. `scripts/__tests__/chatEvidenceGuardrail.test.mjs` (7 checks, in `npm run test:roster`; force-added because `*.mjs` is gitignored). Not verified with a live chat call (paid; the next customer chat is the test): watch that a request for more cards gets the "three cards" answer in the same reply.
- **Title changed** to "Murder Under Golden Stars" on the package and the conversation (Jonathan's yes). The customer-facing note mentions it.
- **Wrong-person sheets: cause, prevention, measurement.** *Cause:* Call 1 of the Child prompt (modules 401 detective, 501 slip) has a strong GRAMMATICAL PERSON rule ("write it entirely in FIRST PERSON") for the spoken fields and nothing about the person of `description`, `background`, `relationships` and `secret`; the schema placeholders are neutral ("[One significant secret this character is hiding]", "[1-2 sentences grounded in both characters' backgrounds]"), which invites third person, and the first-person rule bleeds into the sheet fields. The headings say "YOUR SECRET" and "YOUR RELATIONSHIPS" and the point-form is second person. *Prevalence (new, bigger than the first reading):* English paid packages created since 2026-09-20: 342 of 383 secrets (89 percent) and 345 of 383 relationship sections (90 percent) are not written to the player. It is the normal output, not an occasional slip; the Swedish Golden Stars package had second person throughout, so this is English-specific behaviour of the current prompt. *Prevention (built, NOT imported):* `temp-files/build-child-v67.py` builds `MM Live - Child (Unified)67-SheetFieldsSecondPerson` from the live Child66: a new bullet before the GRAMMATICAL PERSON rule (relationships and secret in second person, with generic examples that name no cast; description and background in third person; the first-person rule applies only to the spoken fields it lists) and the schema placeholders of secret and relationships (both calls) say so. Only modules 401 and 501 change, every difference asserted, the child-context module 414 and its key untouched. *Detection (shipped, migration `20261014140000`):* `list_packages_with_sheet_wrong_person(_since)` lists English packages with characters whose secret (the "Why This Matters" tail excluded) or relationships (heading excluded) contain no "you/your". English only on purpose (Spanish, Italian and Portuguese drop the pronoun). Sweep and before/after tool: no heal (the fix is a rewrite), not in the gate, not alerted, because it flags nearly everything until Child67 is live. Golden Stars no longer appears in it.
- **Decision needed from Jonathan (Child67 import):** it changes the voice of every new English secret and relationship section, an improvement but a visible one, and the secrets will read "You ..." in new packages while delivered ones do not. Success test after import: `select * from list_packages_with_sheet_wrong_person(<import date>)` should show 0 or near 0 bad secrets on the next 3 English purchases; also watch `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch` and the spoken fields for the opposite error (a script drifting into second person). If it holds, delete the watch line. Delivered packages are not rewritten in bulk (about 340 secrets); a customer who notices gets the by-hand fix used on Golden Stars.

2. **Pam to Shannon Ridgewood.** Rule: "Pamela 'Pam' Ridgewood" and "Pamela Ridgewood" to "Shannon Ridgewood", then "Pamela" and the word "Pam" to "Shannon", then a cleanup for doubled forms. Applied to 16 character columns (name, description, background, relationships, secret and secrets, intro, round 3 and 4 questions, round 4 script, accusations, final statement and the pointform twins), `detective_script`, `master_context` (including the relationship matrix), `evidence_cards`, `extracted_characters`. 4. **Rounds: not changed in the package.** Round count is not configurable (see the round-count note in the vault), and Round 4 is the elimination round: it holds the forged note and the mourning ring, the clues that name the guilty. Deleting it makes the mystery unsolvable. The customer was told to run Rounds 3 and 4 together instead, and a one-page combined Round 3 script (the original wording of both sections joined, no new clue text) was written for Stephanie as a separate attachment, `00_INBOX/blackthorn-manor-combined-round-3-script-2026-10-14-mystery-maker.md`. ## Addendum 102 (2026-10-14): `list_packages_with_final_statement_confession_leak` - two false-positive shapes removed (health-check issue #5)

**Trigger.** The scheduled health check (issue #5) listed two slip-style packages for a confession in the Final Statements round: Dead Man's Saddle (Dr. Adeline Marsh) and Murder At Montero Manor (Madame Celeste Devereaux). The same comment also listed a `needs_review` package and a self-directed-question hit; both were already cleared (the package `b880b124` is the Lip Sync For Your Life conversation, released 2026-10-13, and the health table read 0 for both). El Último Trago stays flagged until it ages out of the 14-day meta-text window; it is the package held on purpose since 2026-10-06 (customer declined the adaptation, full refund 2026-10-06), nothing owed.

**Finding.** Read both flagged sheets in full: both are denials, and the real confessions sit in `reveal_confession_*`. Two regex shapes caused the hits.
1. Adeline Marsh `final_guilty`: "I could have left that tox screen in a drawer ... and I did not. ... I did it because he deserved better" - "I did it" means speaking up, not the killing.
2. Celeste Devereaux `final_accomplice`: "I hid none of it" - the negation comes after the verb, the negation list only looks before it.

**Decision (Jonathan: "yeah lets do that change").** Two narrow exclusions, no change to anything else: (a) `final_guilty` is ignored when "I did not" precedes "I did it because/for/so" within 250 characters; (b) the accomplice pattern `hid ` gets a lookahead excluding none/nothing/no/nobody. Migration `20261014210000_final_statement_confession_leak_fp_shapes.sql`, applied through the MCP after diffing the live function against the repo file (identical).

**Why not the broader fix.** Excluding every "I did it because" would hide real confessions ("I did it because she threatened me"), so the guilty exclusion needs the earlier "I did not" as evidence that "it" is something else. Residual risk accepted: a guilty statement that denies first and then confesses with "I did it because" within 250 characters is missed. The detector is alert-only, and the reveal-round fields and the unconfessed-culprit detector are untouched.

**Verification.** Corpus check over every slip-style character since 2026-08-06 before applying: only the two known false positives flipped to clean, no other row changed. Synthetic controls after the change: plain "I did it", "I did it because she threatened me", "I killed him", "I hid the body", "I helped hide the knife" and "I chose them over the truth" all still flag; the two false-positive shapes do not. The live function now returns 0 rows. Pre-existing and not changed: "Do not think I killed him" still flags (bare "do not" is not in the negation list).

**Known behaviour, not a bug.** GitHub issue #5 is still open on the El Último Trago meta-text line; do not close it earlier (the next run reopens a new one, see the 2026-10-06 alert-policy note).

**Key files.** `supabase/migrations/20261014210000_final_statement_confession_leak_fp_shapes.sql`.

**Package.** `545860b6-7199-4eb7-9dfc-8de6e6c34236`, conversation `c35afa15-df6a-4b26-a90e-145f2685d362`, paid 19.99 USD 2026-10-14 23:32 UTC, released with the ready email 23:40:44 (review-before-release held it 90 s). `mystery_packages_prompt_versions`: Parent87, Child67 (Child66 imported 11:39, Child67 13:10, so every call on the live versions). Passive probe check: `applied` 0, `total` 0 (the leak and fact probes ran on this package with 0 findings each, so the passive check has still not seen a finding; keep the note).

Verdicts: the open one is `false` by my read: Remy states the reviews fact in Round 2 (both branches), Wren says the fake in her own introduction, and Max's own rumor 3 already carries the Remy fact, so all of it is public in the game by the final statements; applied one `real`. Twin candidates: the same Max sentence in `final_guilty` and a shorter one in `round2_guilty`, fine for the same reason.

**Full-cast read (all 8 sheets, prose and point-form, plus detective script, overview, evidence cards, materials, `master_context`).** Matrix vs relationship blocks: 0 contradictions. Alibi claims agree across sheets (Vic's call in the garden, Blair's photographs, Dez's lights check, Max's bottles). Victim is "Jo" everywhere, "she" everywhere. English throughout, materials English.

**Hand-found defects the reviewer did not flag (9, all recorded in `package_review_misses`, 8 fixed by hand, free):**
1. Wren `introduction` ended with a stray HTML `<br/>` (C11; `list_stray_markup` does not look for HTML tags).
2. Wren `accusations` tip (and its point-form twin) told the culprit to say they had "no history with her to settle", contradicting Wren's own introduction and Round 2 (C5).

6. Max `introduction_pointform` said Dez invited Jo, Max's own secret and background say Max did (C5).
7. Max `round2_innocent` "Ash lost his name" for the dual-name Ash, the same sheet says "their" elsewhere (C7). Other gendered self-words (woman, hostess, businesswoman) left: the host picks a side per player.
8. The stolen-pearls red herring was in one character's rumors (Vic), the detective Round 1 and a prop, so "woven through rumor" was thin (C6, medium). `requirements_extract` ran (17 lines) and produced no `REQUIREMENTS` finding, so this is a reviewer miss. Added one neutral pearl line to Remy's and Charlie's rumors (prose and point-form) that points at Ash, consistent with Vic's existing rumor.
9. Dez's secret is abstract (see Parent87 below). **Not fixed.**
Also logged, not fixed: Max's relationships block omits Dez (matrix Friendly; the background carries it; C4 low).

## Addendum 104 (2026-10-16): New-Purchase sweep - "Till Death Do Us Part-y" (EN, detective style, 23 characters, accomplice) - second purchase on Parent87 + Child66 + Child67

**Package.** `82241054-34c5-4827-80d2-4353da4cd529`, conversation `4ace6072-7f25-4e55-a37a-35ec2354417d`, paid 24.99 USD 2026-10-16 06:05 UTC, generation finished by 06:44, review-before-release 06:44:00 to 06:46:21, ready email 06:46. `mystery_packages_prompt_versions`: Parent87, Child67 (Child66 live since 2026-10-14 11:39). **Detectors.** 12 `list_packages_with_*` functions (meta-text leak, victim mismatch, unresolved victim name, dangling quote, role-tag leak, slip partner, slip reveal names, missing core content, materials language, pre-reveal culprit name, narration person, sheet wrong person) scoped by `_since`: 0 hits, before and after my edits. `package_completion_blocking_defects`: NULL (my first call passed a partial row without `conversation_id` and returned a false `accomplice_role_mismatch.has_accomplice=null`; **pass the full `mystery_packages` row**, not `{"id": ...}`). `list_stray_markup` hit the statement timeout, so I scanned every character field by hand for odd `**`, braces, combining marks, indented lines, HTML tags and a missing `#` header: 0. One free pre-release heal fired (`strip_dangling_quote:final_statement`, second package since Child63 to need it). Passive probe check: `applied` 0 of 7 (6 `+fact`, 1 `+leak`), so the passive note in CLAUDE.md is deleted.

**Reviewer.** 24 findings (main 12: 8 applied, 4 open; `+p2` 5: 3 applied, 2 open; `+leak` 1; `+fact` 6, all open) against a detective baseline of about 20. All ran before my first edit (`reviewer_ran_before_edit = true`). Verdicts: 23 real, 1 debatable (Willy "drifting around the champagne", the canon itself is ambiguous about where Dylissimo sat). The 11 applied replacements read in context: all good, none reverted. The requirements extract ran (27 lines) and produced 2 `REQUIREMENTS` findings, **both real**: the customer's quoted last line and "the night everything comes out" appeared nowhere in the shared script. First real hits for the extract (it had 0 findings on Br31 and Thrifted).

**Full-cast read (all 23 sheets, prose and point-form, detective script, overview, cards, materials, `master_context` positions).** Victim "Gino", "he", consistent. Relationship matrix vs sheets: no contradictions found. Register and headers: English throughout, every branch field has its header. Closing arrest line (Parent73/77): generic, no victim name, murderer and victim not in one sentence. Overview (Parent83): names no hidden secret. Printed card text names no culprit. Round 3/4 narration names nobody (`pre_reveal_culprit_name` clean). Zoya's "turned him gay" joke is in her confession as the customer asked; Bianca's "this is the last time" is in her background only.

**Hand-found defects the reviewer did not flag (18 rows in `package_review_misses`, 15 fixed by hand, free):**
1. **Culprit's position pointed at before Round 3 (C2, high, 3 sheets).** Lord Baron's read-aloud Round 1 introduction says he could see Zoya at the far side of the head table; Lulu's Round 1 rumor says Zoya "was standing right at the head table when the toast began"; Lulu's Round 2 question to Zoya says the same. The `pre_reveal_culprit_name` detector cannot see this (no name in the narration, the pointer is in sheet text). The same witness lines are correct in Round 4 and stay there. Removed from the three Round 1/2 places, point-form mirrored.

The Willy line in the reveal "Own what you have already admitted" (he admitted nothing before the final statements; low). Benito's envelope contents and Collette's drunken remarks are never stated (C17, both because the concept itself is vague).

Standing yes (up to 2 re-rolls, about 0.04 USD each): round 4 re-rolled once with a prompt for table outlines and no letters at all, clean; round 2 re-rolled twice (attempt 1 still gibberish, attempt 2 rejected as NSFW, probably the "lipstick smudge"), original restored (it at least has the correct heading). The round 3 image is a plain tumbler beside a print, not an engraved flute; left.

**Edits applied.** 39 exact-match text edits on 15 characters (every prose edit mirrored into its point-form twin by hand, so no paid `pointform_resync`), plus the detective script opening statement, which now carries the victim's quoted last words and the "night everything comes out" hint (the two REQUIREMENTS findings). Gate and detectors re-run after: clean; package still released.

**Watch items resolved or advanced.**
- **Child67 wrong-person sheets:** 0 hits on 23 sheets, `sheet_wrong_person` empty. 2 of 3 clean (Thrifted To Death, this one).
- **Parent87 concrete hidden wrongs (ADR-0162):** 21 of 23 concrete (what the victim did, to whom, roughly when). The two exceptions are the two where the concept itself is vague (Benito's envelope, Collette's remarks), the same shape as Dez on Thrifted: the wording holds where the concept gives a hook and does not invent where it gives none. 2 of 3.
- **Child66 redaction cost (statements about another character that contradict canon):** 17 found (11 by the reviewer, 6 by hand) across 23 detective sheets, mostly who stood where. This is the suspected cost and it is visible, roughly 0.7 per sheet; the early-pointer rows above are a different failure (the observer sentence being used before Round 4). Not attributable to Child66 alone (a 23-player cast has far more pairs), so the number needs a no-redaction comparison before it drives a rollback.
- **Parent86 observer sentences:** every `keyMomentPositions` entry carries `whatAnObserverSees`; the culprit's sentence states no act or intent and is about as long as the others.
- **Parent85 images:** check failed on 2 of 3 rounds (above).

**Decisions and scope calls.**
- **No detector for "culprit's position named in Round 1/2 sheet text" yet.** ADR-0131 test: detection is partly deterministic (the murderer's name within 100 characters of a location word in an introduction, rumor or Round 2 field) but the location words are scenario-specific ("head table", "clutch bag"), so a regex tuned to this package would not generalise and a general one would be noisy; impact is a spoiler but not a name. First sighting of this exact shape, so it stays a hand check (added to the sweep text) and gets a detector design on the second sighting. The prevention is in the data: the observer sentences should only reach Round 4 fields.
- **Eddie's public debt left as found** (pervasive, the customer's own concept lists the debt as Tyson's secret and Eddie as the creditor, so the contradiction starts in the concept).
- **Misses recorded, not forced to zero:** 18 misses against 23 real findings is recall about 56 percent on this package, a better figure than the corpus 50 percent but with 3 high-severity spoilers inside it.

**Key files.** None changed in the repo; package content edited through the REST API (15 `mystery_characters` rows and `mystery_packages.detective_script`), evidence image `round4.png` replaced via `generate-evidence-images`, `round2.png` re-stored unchanged via `store-evidence-images`.

### Addendum 104, Update 1 (2026-10-16, later): the "left for Jonathan" items decided and fixed

Jonathan: "I will never read a customer's mystery so I want those creative decisions to be included as part of the new fixes or reviews", and no customer note. So every item the first pass left open was decided in the same session, by the rule now in CLAUDE.md step 7d (creative calls belong to the sweep, not to Jonathan).

Eddie's sheet, Zoya's lines and the Leverage List card are consistent with a public debt and were not touched. Gate clean after.
- **Reveal line "Own what you have already admitted":** replaced in `detective_script` with "Own what your distraction allowed." (Willy had admitted nothing before the final statements; the new line is true of what he did).
- **Benito's envelope and Collette's drunken remarks:** decided to leave abstract. Concrete contents would invent facts about Pedro, Pablo and the laundering (Benito) or about other relatives (Collette); the customer's own concept keeps both vague and Collette's secret says she does not remember. Recorded as decisions in the `package_review_misses` notes, not as open questions.
- **Round 3 image (tumbler instead of an engraved flute):** re-rolled once (about 0.04 USD, within the standing yes) with a prompt for a tall crystal flute with an etched swirl pattern; the result is a flute lying on its side with candlelight and no lettering, kept, URL versioned. The judge had passed the tumbler as "partly"; a stricter "partly" rule was not added (one case, and the re-roll cost is small).
- No customer message sent (Jonathan's decision).

## Addendum 105 (2026-10-17): Health-check alert on "Dead Serious: The BR31 Unveiling" - a lone `character_name` edit ("Madame Vanille") reverted

- **Alert:** the scheduled health check (GitHub issue #5, 2026-10-16) reported `name_background_mismatch: Madame Vanille -> background says French` on package `6eb76783` (detective, EN, paid, 17 characters), alongside the known El Ultimo Trago item.
- **Finding (real, not a false positive):** character row `f93040e0` had `character_name = 'Madame Vanille'` (`updated_at` 2026-10-16 16:41 UTC, the only row on the package touched since 2026-10-14) while its background, description, introduction and point-form said "French Vanilla", as do `master_context` (60 references), the other 15 sheets (about 50), the detective script (2), the evidence cards (3), `extracted_characters` and the customer's conversation. "Madame Vanille" appeared nowhere else in the package or in the customer's chat, and `auto_remediation_log` had no row for the package, so no heal made the change. The edit path is unknown (an editor edit is the likeliest); the detector's exemption for renames that keep two or more name words (CHANGELOG, S'more Heist) does not apply because the two names share no word.
- **Decision (mine, per rule 7d):** revert the one column to "French Vanilla" rather than propagate the new name. Reason: nothing shows the customer asked for a rename, reverting is one free row edit against about 120 text replacements across 16 sheets and their point-form twins, and the original name is the pun the whole concept is built on. Not regenerated (the detector's "needs regeneration" wording is generic escalate-only text; regeneration is paid and would not have been the right fix).
- **Result:** `list_packages_with_structural_defects('2026-10-01')` returns 0 rows for the package and `package_completion_blocking_defects` is empty; status still `completed`. The alert clears on the next health-check run. No customer message sent.
- **If the customer does ask for "Madame Vanille":** that is a propagate-everywhere rename (all sheets, point-form twins, `master_context`, script, cards, matrix); do it as one reviewed bulk edit and keep the character they/them (the sheet text is gender-neutral).

Small cast and one package, so not yet evidence; one more detective purchase decides.
